# Module map

RADD-1282: issue comments distinguish ordinary comments from resolvable threads
(`comments.is_thread`). Workflow's `require_resolved_threads` check calls the
comments service's audience-independent existence query. The existing project
transitions, issue-type conditions and target states define where it applies.
Comment changes refresh the UI's allowed transitions. See [resolvable threads](resolvable-threads.md).

RADD-1283: who may resolve a thread is `comments.thread_resolution_rules` (project → optional
issue type → `ThreadResolvers` author|assignee|anyone|managers; migration `d1283threadpolicy`,
both FKs CASCADE), served at `GET/PUT /projects/{id}/thread-resolution` (project.manage, full
replace, audited as `comment.resolution_policy_updated`, not a trigger). `comments/resolution.py`
is the ONE answer: `resolve_reach` (any|own|none, once per parent) — `set_resolved` enforces it
and every comment read carries `can_resolve`, so the SPA no longer restates the rule. `comments`
now `depends_on` `itemtypes` (override validation). Feeds take `unresolved=true` as a FILTER that
composes with `section` (a page's Discussion is `section=discussion&unresolved=true`); a reply may
carry `unresolve: true`. Pages (no project, no type) always use the default rule with page.manage
as the managers. SPA: `components/comments/ThreadResolution.tsx` (badge, resolve button, filter,
rule style) shared by `items/CommentsThread` and `pages/PageComments`;
`settings/ThreadResolutionSection.tsx` under Project settings → Workflow.

RADD-1291: **cycles have a home project** (`cycles.project_id`, nullable, SET NULL; migration
`d1291cyclehome` backfills each cycle to the project holding most of its issues). Not a scope: a
cycle still holds any project's issues. `GET /cycles?project_id=` = homed there OR holding its issues
(`cycles.directory.for_project`); Planning, project Reports' burnup and the issue/new-issue cycle
pickers ask for it (pickers switch to "All cycles"). Writes: the instance `cycle.*` atom OR
`project.manage` on the home project (`router._require_cycle_write`); re-homing needs both sides.
Series drafts inherit the latest cycle's home.

RADD-1285: **shipping is a workflow transition.** `workflow_transitions.on_release`
("moves automatically when a release is published", needs a from-state, one per from-state)
replaces spec 112's `release_waiting_state`/`release_shipped_state` settings (deleted, with their
config defaults; migration `d1285releaseflow` converted every resolvable pair into an on-release row
and rewrote hand-built `require_field(release, set)` into the named `require_release` check).
`releases.pipeline.sweep` performs each on-release row; `waiting_state_id` (the VCS connectors'
merged-PR target) is the first row's from-state; `workflow.release_transitions` is the seam. An
on-release row ALWAYS requires a release (`transitions.effective_rules`): as an exact-from row it
outranks an "Any → shipped" guard, so without it a person could hand-move work past the gate.

RADD-1202: Planning uses active/upcoming/draft sprint sections, a separate open historical-work rescheduling section, an independently paged/searched/sorted backlog and explicitly opened per-sprint history. `planning-query.ts` / `PlanningControls.tsx` own these controls; `cycle.status` delegates to the cycle directory’s lifecycle expression. See [Planning workflow](planning-pagination.md).


RADD-988: service-desk public replies export only verified images from explicitly enabled storage hosts. `attachments/email_export.py` owns the host/ownership/ACL/size policy; `mailintake/attachments.py` resolves explicit local comment references at delivery; `mailtypes.MailAttachment` carries MIME parts through the shared transport. Storage admin UI exposes `email_images_allowed` (migration `d988emailimages`, default off). See [service-desk email images](service-desk-email-images.md).


How the server is assembled and how modules connect. **Update this file in the same change that adds a module, event type, or cross-module dependency.**

## Kernel (spec 93 — `radd/kernel/`)

The kernel is the generic machinery every feature (plugin) builds on; it never disables (docs/plugin-platform.md §1). It holds the **plugin contract** (`kernel/plugin.py` — `RaddPlugin`, a manifest of contributions that evolves the pre-kernel `RaddModule`; all 52 builtin plugins construct `RaddPlugin` directly, and the migration is finished — the `RaddModule`/`load_modules` aliases are deleted), the **contribution registries** (`kernel/registry.py` — one dict per contribution kind: `entities`, `event_types`, `permissions`, `crud_resources`, `capabilities`, `tasks`, `consumers`, `integrations`, `nav`; populated by the loader, read blind by exactly one generic consumer each), the **loader/lifecycle** (`kernel/loader.py` — `load_plugins` aggregates contributions, checks `depends_on` + semver `api_version` compat; `import_models` unchanged), and the **contribution specs** (`kernel/specs.py` — `EventTypeSpec`/`CapabilitySpec`/`PermissionSpec`/`CrudResourceSpec`/`EntitySpec`/`TaskSpec`/`IntegrationSpec`/`NavItemSpec`/`PluginUiManifest`, all pure — the kernel imports nothing from `radd.modules.*`). The `radd/module.py` shim is deleted — import the contract from `radd.kernel`. The generic-mechanism modules (`events`, `access`, `auth`-core, `settings`) physically stay under `radd.modules.*` for import stability but are kernel-governed. Core-invariants: `tests/test_kernel.py`.

**The manifest REFUSES a bare value where a tuple is declared (RADD-756).** Every contribution field on `RaddPlugin` is a `tuple[Spec, ...]`, and `on_startup=_startup` instead of `on_startup=(_startup,)` is a defect Python will not catch — the dataclass stores whatever it is handed and the failure surfaces wherever the field is finally iterated. That shipped once, in 0.8.0, as an image that could not complete `lifespan`, with 1391 green tests behind it. `__post_init__` now raises for all 26 such fields, derived from the annotations rather than listed by hand, so a contribution added later is covered the day it is declared. It REJECTS rather than normalising: two valid shapes for one field is how the next module learns the wrong one. It also catches `depends_on="items"`, which is worse than a crash — a `str` is iterable, so that one fails silently as five one-character plugin names. `tests/test_app_startup.py` runs the real `create_app` lifespan, which nothing in the suite had ever done.

**`ContributionSource` (RADD-748)** records which plugin registered a contribution, kept beside the specs rather than on them: a spec is authored BY the plugin, so a self-declared `source` could disagree with reality, and only the registry is in a position to know. `page_extension_sources` is the first adopter; unregister drops the source with the spec, or a stale one outlives it and mislabels the next contribution reusing the name.

## Assembly

`radd.app:create_app()` reads `settings.modules` (ordered dotted paths), imports each package's `plugin: RaddPlugin`, verifies `depends_on`, then:

- mounts every router under `/api/v1`
- registers module `exception_handlers`
- runs module `on_startup` hooks in the lifespan
- applies `openapi_augmentors` on every OpenAPI render (this is how dynamic schema stays live)

`RaddPlugin` (see `radd/kernel/plugin.py`) is the plugin contract. Alembic discovers models the same way (`import_models(settings.modules)`), so a module's tables migrate iff the module is enabled.

**Frontend UI manifest (spec 93 / A7, chokepoint 3):** `GET /capabilities` now returns a backend-assembled UI manifest — `capabilities` (the status flags) + `nav` (plugin-contributed nav items from `registries.nav`, each with `requires` atoms / `capability` gate / `order`). The SPA sidebar (`web/src/components/shell/Sidebar.tsx`) fetches `capabilitiesQuery` and renders the plugin nav (`pluginNav`, filtered by the user's atoms + enabled capabilities) alongside its builtin nav — so an enabled plugin's nav lights up with no edit to the shell. Additive over the existing hardcoded nav (full manifest-driven replacement of `SETTINGS_NAV`/router + generic plugin pages via declarative-UI/federation are the §8b follow-up).

**Frontend module federation (spec 94, §8b-A):** the SPA is a real plugin platform — a plugin ships its own built ESM bundle that a *running* Radd loads at runtime, with zero host imports. Mechanism (native ES modules, no MF plugin): the host (`web/index.html`) declares an **import map** for the shared singletons (`react`, `react-dom[/client]`, `react/jsx-runtime`, `@tanstack/react-query`+`react-router`, `@radd/plugin-sdk`) → `/shared/*.js` shim files (generated by `web/scripts/gen-shared-shims.mjs`) that re-export the host's singletons published on `globalThis.__RADD_SHARED__` (`web/src/shared-runtime.ts`); a plugin **remote** is a Vite lib build that EXTERNALIZES those ids (keeping the bare specifiers the import map resolves) and `export default definePlugin({activate})`. The public **`@radd/plugin-sdk`** (`web/packages/plugin-sdk`) is the only frontend surface a plugin imports: the **slot registry** (`registerSlot`/`unregisterPlugin`/`useSlot`/`<Slot>`, ids `issue.panel.section`/`issue.tab`/`sidebar.nav`/`route.page`/`settings.page`/`dashboard.widget`/`item.action`), theme **tokens** (semantic `--radd-*` over the host zinc scale) + **primitives** (Button/Field/Select/Chip/Card/Avatar/…), permission-aware **hooks** + api client, and the semver **`UI_API_VERSION`** gate. The host loader (`web/src/lib/plugin-loader.ts`, driven by `PluginRemotes`) reads each ENABLED plugin's remote from `/capabilities` `remotes[]`, version-gates it, `import()`s + `activate()`s it (registering its slots), **quarantines** failures, and when the manifest withdraws a remote calls `unregisterPlugin`; pending loads are invalidated and failed activation contributions are rolled back. Base views render `<Slot>` and know no plugin: `IssueProperties` hosts `issue.panel.section`, a splat route → `PluginPage` hosts `route.page`. Backend: `PluginUiManifest.remote` + `ui_api_version` on the manifest; `/capabilities` surfaces enabled remotes; bundles are served at `/plugins/<name>/` from each plugin's OWN `ui/dist` dir, recorded in the kernel registry `registries.plugin_ui_dirs` (`_mount_plugin_assets` in `app.py`); external plugins are discovered via the **`radd.plugins` entry point** (`pluginmgr/discovery.py`). Builtin remotes are colocated with their plugin (`<plugin>/ui/`, built by `web/scripts/build-all.mjs` into `<plugin>/ui/dist/`): `participants` (issue.panel.section), `milestones` (route.page CRUD). The acceptance example `examples/acme-notes/` is an independent project (own pyproject entry point + own web build) proving a third-party plugin loads with zero core edits. Verified in headless Chromium by `web/scripts/render-proof.mjs`. Tests: `tests/test_frontend_federation.py`.

**Plugin-UI depth (spec 94 addendum):** the SDK slot vocabulary grew: `view.type` + `dashboard.widget` (keyed by the type key, backed by kernel `registries.view_types`/`widget_types` from `ViewTypeSpec`/`WidgetTypeSpec` on `RaddPlugin`, surfaced in `/capabilities`), `plugin.manager.section` + `profile.section`, and SLQ-field registration (`registries.slq_fields`, `SlqFieldSpec` → `items/slq/compiler.py` routes an unknown field to it). **Per-contribution toggles are two-scope + plugin-owned:** a contribution renders iff enabled in BOTH the GLOBAL scope (instance-wide, admin — stored per-plugin in `InstalledPlugin.config`; `GET /plugins/contribution-settings` any-user + `PUT /plugins/{id}/contribution-settings` admin) and the PER-USER scope (Profile; `users.preferences` JSONB + `GET/PUT /auth/me/preferences`). The kernel forces nothing — a plugin mounts `<GlobalContributionToggles>`/`<UserContributionToggles>` (On/Off switch) into the `plugin.manager.section`/`profile.section` slots (`toggleable:false` opts a control-surface contribution out). A disabled contribution leaves EVERY manifest-driven surface too: `useDisabledNavPaths`/`useDisabledMatches(slot)` drop its sidebar nav link, its view-type/widget-type dropdown option, and show a `MissingPluginType` "turned off" notice for an existing view/widget of a turned-off type. `PluginRead` gained `name` (the slot-registry tag). `docs/plugin-ui.md` is the full map.

**Managed plugin packages (spec 125, RADD-1220):** `pluginmgr/store.py` validates uploaded pure-Python wheels, publishes checksum directories through an atomic catalog, and owns file cleanup. `live.py` reconciles UI-only optional plugins independently in web and worker processes; backend contributions retain restart semantics. Package removal checks shared process acknowledgements. Settings provides upload/install, progress/errors and Remove files after Forget. `RADD_PLUGINS_DIR` is persistent shared storage; the Helm chart mounts a dedicated plugin PVC. [Spec and limits](specs/125-managed-plugin-packages.md).

**Plugin manager / lifecycle (spec 124, RADD-1219):** `pluginmgr` owns desired installation state, package discovery/diagnostics, dependency checks and boot ordering. Settings changes apply on restart of all web/worker processes; HTTP requests never mutate routes or run package managers. The API reports local loaded/pending state separately. Forgetting preserves data and removes plugin-specific grants after unloading. External development tooling lives in `radd.plugin_cli` (scaffold, check, editable link, wheel/UI build, checksum and deployment recipe); see [plugin development](plugin-development.md) and [spec 124](specs/124-external-plugin-workflow.md).

**Entity auto-wiring (spec 93 / A3) + north-star:** `kernel/entities.py` turns a declarative `EntitySpec` (field DSL, or a `model=` escape hatch) into a first-class feature: it builds the SQLAlchemy model (imperative Table mapping) into `Base.metadata`, and auto-registers the `<key>.created/updated/deleted` event types (→ automations/webhooks/audit), the `<key>.create/update/delete` CRUD-resource RBAC atoms, and a generic permission-guarded CRUD router (`crud_router`, mounted by `app.py` from `registries.entity_routers`). `ensure_tables()` (called in the lifespan) creates entity tables idempotently. The loader auto-wires on `plugin.entities`. The **`milestones`** plugin (`radd/modules/milestones/` — one `EntitySpec` + a `NavItemSpec`, `core=False`) is the north-star: declaring it lights up automations/RBAC/nav/CRUD with zero edits elsewhere (`tests/test_north_star.py`). It's not in the always-on `config.modules` bootstrap — it's a non-core plugin installed (migration `a29615cf474d`) + runtime-enabled via the plugin manager (§10). `config.bootstrap_plugins` is the placeholder for that lifecycle set.

**RBAC registries plugin-contributable (spec 93 / A2):** permission atoms + CRUD resources are no longer a closed enum/tuple. A plugin declares `permissions=(PermissionSpec…)` / `crud_resources=(CrudResourceSpec…)` in its manifest (or calls `kernel.register_permission`/`register_crud_resource`); the merged-view accessors in `auth/types.py` (`all_permission_keys`, `permission_scope_of`, `permission_description_of`, `implied_map`) fold them in LIVE alongside the builtin `Permission` enum / `CRUD_RESOURCES`. The union engine (`authz.combine_permissions`, `effective_permissions`, admin = `all_permission_keys()`) now flows atoms as **strings** (StrEnum members already are strings — builtins byte-identical), so a plugin atom appears in `GET /permissions`, is held by admin, and is grantable in a role. `RoleCreate/Update/Read.permissions` + `MeRead.permissions` + `PermissionRead.key` are `list[str]`/`str`; `RoleCreate/Update` validate atoms against `all_permission_keys()`. In the default config the registry is empty, so every accessor equals the builtin set exactly. Tests: `tests/test_rbac_registry.py`.

**Chokepoint-2 inversion (spec 93):** the `capabilities` core plugin (`radd/modules/capabilities/` + `kernel/capabilities.py`) serves `GET /capabilities` — it evaluates every plugin's `CapabilitySpec` (static `enabled` or a `check()` returning `{enabled, **detail}`). Feature/connector plugins declare their own capability in their manifest (`capabilities=(CapabilitySpec(...),)`); the `capabilities` plugin owns the infra flags (workers/smtp/mfa). `projects`' `/instance/status`, `/instance`, `/instance/login-options` now read the registry (`kernel.capabilities.capability_map()`) instead of inlining `bool(settings.*)`; `connectors` is derived from every `category=='connector'` capability, so a new connector appears with zero edits. Parity pinned by `tests/test_capabilities.py`.

**Chokepoint-1 inversion (spec 93):** automation triggers are no longer a hardcoded catalog. Each producing module declares its triggerable events in its manifest (`event_types=(EventTypeSpec(...),)` — the trigger metadata label/group/item_scoped/has_changes now lives next to the producer, not in a central list). The loader aggregates them into `kernel.registries.event_types`; `automations/catalog.py`'s `TRIGGERS` reads `registries.triggers()` live (PEP 562 `__getattr__`), so a new plugin's events appear as triggers with zero edits to automations. Parity is pinned by `tests/test_trigger_registry.py` (65-trigger snapshot).

Two app-level pieces outside the module system: `radd/middleware.py` (`CommitBeforeSendMiddleware` — buffers each HTTP response until the request's session has committed, closing the response-before-commit race FastAPI's yield-dependency teardown ordering creates) and SPA serving (`_mount_spa` in `app.py` — serves `web/dist` with SPA fallback when present, `RADD_WEB_DIST` overrides). One shared core util: `radd/smtp.py` (spec 62) — THE synchronous outbound-email helper (`send_message(to, subject, body, *, to_name, headers, config, html_body)`) over the `smtp_*` settings; callers wrap it in `asyncio.to_thread` and keep the empty-`smtp_host`-means-disabled guard themselves; `html_body` makes the message `multipart/alternative` with the text part FIRST (RADD-967). Consumers: notify digests, mailintake acks + comment replies (spec 62), csat surveys (spec 65); the automation send_email action (spec 66) later. Beside it, **`radd/mailrender.py` (RADD-967) — THE mail composition layer**: pure (no session, no `radd.modules.*` import, no settings read — the base URL is an argument), returning `RenderedMail(text, html)` for the outbound comment reply, the intake ack and the notification digest, plus the canonical `issue_url`/`page_url`/`inbox_url`/`preferences_url` (trailing-slash normalised; `googlechat.formatter` dropped its private copy of the issue URL). **RADD-985**: `comment_reply`/`notice`/`digest` take an optional `preferences` URL and print a "Notification settings" link in BOTH parts when given, and `unsubscribe_headers(url)` is the `List-Unsubscribe` header (RFC 2369; the one-click POST variant needs a tokened endpoint and is not claimed) — notify's two loops pass both, the requester-facing ack and reply never do. HTML is self-contained: inline styles, table layout, no assets, no webfonts; EVERYTHING interpolated is `html.escape`d and a comment body is inserted as escaped text with `<br>` line breaks — markdown is NOT rendered and no dependency was added (the mirror of `mailintake/html_body.py`, which strips markup on the way in). A core util beside it, **`radd/tasklists.py` (RADD-1296)** — ticks ONE GFM task-list marker in stored markdown (`toggle(body, TaskToggle{index, checked, expected_body})`, fenced code skipped, blockquotes and CRLF kept). It refuses rather than guesses: `TaskToggleConflict` when the stored body is not the one the reader rendered (the optimistic lock — items and comments have no version column) or box N is not in the opposite state. Three thin endpoints reuse each surface's own write path and check permission BEFORE comparing bodies: `POST /items/{id}/description/tasks` (items), `POST /comments/{id}/tasks` (comments — the edit gate), `POST /pages/{id}/tasks` (pages — versioned, so the spec-122 guard refuses it during a live session). The SPA side is `RichViewer`'s `onToggleTask` (+ `taskScope`, so a page split around `radd:*` blocks counts boxes across the whole body). A second core util, `radd/worker.py` (`PeriodicLoop`) — THE background-loop scaffold (tick / log-don't-crash / sleep, restart-safe stop) every dispatcher-style module instantiates with its `run_once`, gate lambda (usually `run_workers`), and poll-interval lambda; used by webhooks, automations, search, notify (×2), csat, mailintake (×2), googlechat, and the SLA engine.

**Module-internal package splits (the barrel is the boundary).** When a module's `service.py` outgrows the 300-line rule it becomes a `service/` PACKAGE of per-concern modules stacked bottom-up with no cycles, and the package `__init__` is a barrel that re-exports **only the module's public surface** — never the `_`-prefixed helpers, whose privacy is the whole point of rule 1. `items/service/` is the reference: `visibility`/`queries` (leaves) → `relations`/`read`/`listing` → `links` → `core`, barrel = the same 25 functions the former god-file exposed. An intra-module caller that genuinely needs a helper imports the concern module directly (`from .service.visibility import _internal_visible` in `items/bulk.py`); a caller in ANOTHER module does not get one — it asks for a public function.

**Timesheet: group by epic.** The timesheet's grouping axis gained `epic` alongside issue/person/category. Each entry now carries its `epic` (the epic the logged item belongs to), hydrated in `timesheet.build` through a NEW public items seam — `items.service.epics_for_items(session, ids) -> {item_id: EpicRef}` — so the timesheet groups by epic without learning the hierarchy: one aliased child/parent/grandparent chain resolves the whole batch in a single query (the kind ladder caps depth at 3), the same shape `slq/ancestors.py` uses for `epic.*` but as a batch seam rather than a correlated subquery. An item that IS an epic maps to itself (time logged straight onto an epic belongs under it) — **since both sides share ONE definition, `items/hierarchy.py::nearest_epic_case`** (see the `epic` note below). "No epic" is a first-class row, not an error state: general worklogs have no issue at all and plenty of issues sit outside an epic, so folding them together would hide how much time never rolls up.

**`epic` is reflexive — an epic belongs to itself (spec 83 amendment).** `epic` names a CONTAINER, not a relative position: `epic = TD-3` returns TD-3 **plus** its issues and subtasks, and `epic.state != Done` returns each unfinished epic alongside the work it governs. Before this, `epic` was a strict ancestor, so an epic's epic id was NULL and it matched NO `epic.*` condition — not even negated ones, because `NOT (NULL IN (…))` is NULL — while spec 98's timesheet grouping already mapped an epic to itself. Two definitions of one concept; now there is **one**, `items/hierarchy.py::nearest_epic_case(item, parent, grandparent)` (self arm, then parent, then grandparent), which both consumers build and bind differently — `service/queries.py` joins it for a batch, `slq/ancestors.py` correlates it per outer row (correlating on the ITEM and outer-joining upward, since a parentless epic yields no row when you correlate on its parent). `parent`/`parent.*` are untouched: an item is never its own parent. **Behaviour change:** `epic IS EMPTY` / `epic = none` no longer match epics — they mean "work no epic governs", the exact complement of `IS NOT EMPTY`; `kind = epic` asks the old question. Epic nesting can't complicate the rule — `REQUIRED_PARENT_KIND` gives epics no parent kind, so every item has at most one epic in scope. `docs/specs/83-*.md` (amendment).

**Worklog SLQ — the timesheet's dialect (spec 98).** SLQ is now TWO dialects sharing one language. The worklog dialect is rooted at the WORKLOG because that is what the timesheet returns; an item-rooted query can never surface general worklogs (spec 59), which have no item to return, so this is a correctness requirement rather than a convenience. Fields are bare (the root is implied, exactly as item queries say `priority` not `item_priority`): `author`, `category`, `project`, `worked_on`, `time` (duration literals in the log-work language, resolved with the instance's hours-per-day), `note`, and `issue`. **`issue` carries the design's whole idea** — `issue IS EMPTY` (general time), `issue = DEV-123`, and `issue.<anything>` DELEGATED to the item compiler and wrapped as `worklog.item_id IN (SELECT id FROM work_items WHERE …)`. The delegation means this module never restates the item field surface: builtins, custom fields (`issue.cf.foo`), ancestors, labels and plugin fields all work here permanently, with nothing to keep in sync. It is exact, not an approximation — a worklog has exactly one issue, so per-condition delegation and a subquery select the same rows, which is why the braces/sub-query grammar was dropped. Autocomplete delegates the same way (`issue.assi` → the item dialect's fields, `issue.assignee = ` → its values incl. `me`/`none`) by splicing the prefix out and shifting the offset back. Reuses items' lexer/parser/helpers (generic query machinery, now exported from `items.slq`); supplies only a seven-field catalog + compiler + suggest. API: `GET /timesheet?q=` (compiled and ANDed onto the scope filters inside `timesheet.build(where=…)`, so a query can only NARROW what the actor may already see), `GET /timesheet/slq/validate`, `GET /timesheet/slq/suggest`. SPA: the shared `SlqFilterBar` gained a `dialect` prop; the timesheet passes `ApiPath.timesheet` and persists `q` in the URL. Solves the author≠assignee case that motivated it: `author = me AND issue.assignee != me`. `docs/specs/98-*.md`, `tests/test_worklog_slq.py`.

**Relational SLQ fields (spec 97).** SLQ can ask about what HAPPENED to an item, not only what it is: `logged_by` (timelogging) and `commented_by` (comments) find issues by who logged time on them / commented on them — `logged_by = me`, `commented_by = "alice@corp.example"`, `logged_by ~ alice`, with `!=` negated by the engine. No new machinery: both ride spec 94's `SlqFieldSpec` registry, so each module contributes a `Select` of matching work-item ids over its OWN table (plus the `auth.User` spine) and `items` never learns that worklogs or comments exist. One contract change — `item_ids` now takes `(contains, value, ctx: SlqFieldContext)`; the context carries `current_user_id` + an `is_me` flag, because `me` is a grammar sentinel that `helpers.plain()` rejects, so resolvers could not previously honour it. Itemless worklogs (spec 59) never match — no item to name. `commented_by` matches on authorship alone, so an internal comment can make its item match for someone who could not read it (existence, never content; the item still passes normal RBAC). `docs/specs/97-*.md`, `tests/test_relational_slq.py`.

**In-transaction hooks** (`radd/hooks.py`): a module can subscribe to another module's mutation and react *inside the same transaction* (`@hooks.on(ProjectEvent.PROJECT_CREATED)`). Use only when atomicity with the trigger is required (e.g. workflow seeds default states with the project); everything else belongs to outbox consumers, which run after commit.

## Modules

| Module | Purpose | Depends on | Emits events | Extension points used |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `events` | Transactional outbox **and durable audit log** (append-only, never pruned, every row `actor_id`-attributed): `events` table, `emit` inside the caller's txn, `GET /events?after=` for consumers (any active user; spec 86 dropped `workspace_id` from events + the delivery predicate — the realtime frame is authenticated-only). Filtered reads added for audit/history: `query_events` (the admin audit query) and `entity_activity(entity_type, entity_id, related_event_types)` (an item's full feed — direct events UNION related events whose payload `item_id` matches). Indexes `ix_events_entity` (entity_type, entity_id) + `ix_events_actor_id`. **`runner.py` (consolidation): `run_head_seeded(consumer_name, *, batch_size, plan, deliver) -> int`** — THE shared scaffold for delivery-flavored outbox consumers: no offset row → seed at the stream head + commit + return 0 (never replay the backlog at a channel/recipient); read a batch; per-event `plan(session, event)` with log-don't-crash (planning MAY write rows — csat does); cursor + planning writes COMMIT BEFORE `deliver(plans)` runs post-commit (at-most-once). Used by the csat sender, the googlechat notifier, and mailintake outbound. The semantically-divergent consumers stay hand-rolled: search indexer (backlog replay IS the index build, savepoint per event), notify (watch-only bootstrap over the backlog), automations engine (one txn per event), webhooks (fan-out rows are the delivery). **Quiet scope (spec 100): `events.quiet` + the `events.silent` column.** A `ContextVar` (`quiet.py`, re-exported from `service`) that `emit` reads, so a bulk importer marks its whole event stream without any service in the call chain (items/comments/worklogs) learning that an import is running — and because a ContextVar is copied into tasks created inside the scope, ONE `with` around a background run covers every write it makes. Consumers that reach OUTSIDE the instance skip silent rows: notify, webhooks fan-out, the automations engine (`should_process`), the realtime broadcaster, and everything on `run_head_seeded`. Consumers that build INTERNAL state deliberately do NOT: the search index (an imported issue must be findable) and `query_events`/`entity_activity` (it must have history). Each skips **inside its loop and still advances the cursor** — filtering silent rows out in SQL would make an all-silent batch read as an empty stream and wedge the cursor. Preferred over advancing consumer offsets past an import's event range, which would also swallow unrelated concurrent activity. Tests: `tests/test_quiet_events.py`. **THE PAYLOAD CONTRACT (RADD-922): every ITEM-SCOPED event carries `payload["item"]`** — one ref (`items.service.item_ref` / `ref_from`) with `id`, `key` (`TD-42`), `title`, `kind`, `priority`, `state{id,name,category}`, `project{id,key,name}`, `team{id,name}|null`, `assignee{id,name}|null`; the ITEM events nest their whole `ItemRead` there instead, and `changes` stays at the ROOT because it describes the event, not the item. People are `{id,name,email}` refs via `auth.service.user_ref` (`author` on comments/worklogs, `voter`/`requester` on approvals), never bare ids — a payload exists to be READ, and `assignee_id` alone renders a uuid at someone and calls it a notification. **Before this the stream had fourteen shapes**: the key was `key` on item/csat events, `item_key` on sla/page_link, and ABSENT from the other nine (comments, worklogs, attachments, vcs, weblinks, approvals, participants, watchers); `project_id` was on two families; `item_id` was on everything except the item events, which carried `id`. The cost was in the consumers — `notify/consumer.py` resolved the key FOUR ways (two payload spellings + two DB round trips), `googlechat/formatter.py` branched on the spelling, `search/indexer.py` opened with a silent `if "project_id" not in payload: return`, and `gates.state_category_is` read a key NOTHING emitted (dead: always false). Enforced structurally by `tests/test_event_payloads.py`, which AST-parses every `emit()` call — resolving payloads built as a local or a helper — and fails an item-scoped emitter that does not build the ref, or one that reintroduces `item_key`/`item_id`. Migration `d922payload` DELETES the pre-change rows rather than shipping a compat reader (owner decision): the stream is append-only, so History/audit/cycle-reporting start empty; the search index is unaffected (own table + startup sweep). **RADD-923 — the contribution seam**: an emitter now passes `subjects={"item": id}` and the KERNEL writes the ref (`registries.entity_refs`, an `EntityRefSpec` per entity type; `items`, `projects` and every auto-wired `EntitySpec` register one). You cannot forget to build a ref you never build, which is why eleven emitters stopped importing `items` to describe an item. `EventTypeSpec.subjects` declares what an event is ABOUT and the LOADER refuses to boot a plugin naming a subject nothing describes; `EventTypeSpec.payload_schema` declares only the REMAINDER (the kernel wrote the refs, so deriving beats declaring) and is served beside the samples, which is the half sampling cannot answer — an event that has never fired still shows its shape. `Packet` carries `subjects` per entity type (`item_ids` is a property), and `apply_event` seeds EVERY ref the event names, so a non-item event is first-class. **Contributed ACTIONS execute**: `AutomationNodeSpec` gains `subject` + `apply`, and the executor dispatches a registered spec before the `ActionType` enum — previously it raised, logged "unknown action type" and dropped the node, so a plugin could contribute a trigger and a gate but never an action. `plan` runs on every walk (free, identical dry run); `apply` runs inside the executor's SAVEPOINT, its `RunBudget` and its `events.automated()` scope, so a third-party action cannot spin the engine, escape the budget or take a branch down. Its declared atom is enforced where the automation is WRITTEN, like `act_as`. The SPA generates a form from `params_schema` (`SchemaFields`) — the promise the spec made and nothing kept. North-star: `milestones/automation.py` contributes an action on its OWN entity, driven by its OWN auto-wired event, with zero edits to `automations`, the kernel or the SPA (`tests/test_event_subjects.py`, `web/scripts/plugin-contribution-proof.mjs`). The identity sequence is NOT restarted — restarting it would put new events below the stored consumer offsets and every worker would skip them in silence. **Spec 123 — the LEDGER columns:** `project_id` (from the subject refs), `entity_label` (the entity's display label at write time — outlives the row), `search_text` (event words + label + changed fields and values; trigram GIN, guarded on `pg_trgm`) + a GIN over `payload -> 'changes'` (`d123ledger`, SQL-backfilled). `emit(changes=)` writes the `kernel.changes` diff; an event whose `EventTypeSpec.has_changes` is True and carries none raises `ChangesRequired`; `emit` also adds the event's own entity as a subject when a ref is registered. `EventTypeSpec.audited=False` marks noise (`mail.failed`, `notification.created`, `automation.scheduled`). `query_events` gained `project_id`, `entity_type` (comma list), `changed_field` (containment), `source` (`EventSource`: people/automations/system), `exclude_event_types`; `q` matches `search_text` only. `ledger.py` is the pure derivation. | — (router imports `auth.deps`/`auth.authz` for enforcement — the one tolerated backward edge) | — | — |
| `projects` | **Spec 86 stage 3 FINISHED the workspace eradication:** the `workspace` module was renamed to `projects` and the `workspaces` + `workspace_memberships` tables were DROPPED (destructive migration `f4a9c31e77d2`). No workspace entity, singleton resolver, or `default_workspace` accessor survives — projects are global containers with globally-unique keys. `service.create_project`/`get_project`/`list_projects` only; `POST /projects` takes no `workspace_id`, `GET /projects` lists ALL projects. `ProjectRead.permissions` = the CURRENT user's effective permissions (hydrated via `authz.permissions_for_projects`, one batched pass). Global builtin roles + default work categories seed via on-startup ensures (auth `subscribers.ensure_seeded`, timelogging `categories.ensure_seeded`) — replacing the retired `workspace.created` hook. `types.py` exposes `ProjectEvent.PROJECT_CREATED` + `ProjectEntity.PROJECT` (`WorkspaceEvent`/`WORKSPACE_CREATED` gone). **RADD-1009 — rename + describe:** `projects.description` (Text NOT NULL '' — migration `d1009projdesc`), `ProjectUpdate {name?, description?}` (the KEY is deliberately not a field: item keys derive from it), `service.update_project` (emits `project.updated` with a `changes` diff; a no-op PATCH emits nothing), `PATCH /projects/{id}` gated on `project.manage` for that project, `ProjectRead.description`, and the `update_project` MCP tool (kernel-enforced, `project_param`). SPA: `ProjectIdentityCard` at the top of Settings → Project → General; the description shows under the name on the projects index and the settings header. **RADD-1174 — deletion, owned here:** `service.delete_project` runs three layers inside one transaction — `inspect_project` (dispatches the in-txn hook **`project.inspecting`** with a mutable `ProjectInspection{counts, blockers}`; a blocker is a 409 naming it), the hook **`project.deleting`** (`ProjectDeleting{project, actor_id}` — owners remove what the DB cannot cascade), then the RADD-892 `ProjectPurgeSpec` registry in FK-safe order (`purge_project_rows`, the loop jiraimport's rollback used to own) and the row. `project.deleted` is emitted BEFORE the delete so the subject ref resolves; its payload carries `key`, `name`, `removed` (the counts). Subscribers (`<module>/subscribers.py`, registered at import like the seeding hooks): **comments** (count + delete on the project's items — polymorphic parent, no FK), **attachments** (rows + grants in-txn, bytes best-effort after; the orphan GC keys on `item.deleted`, which a purge never emits), **views** (grants on the project's views), **fields** and **linktypes** (definitions scoped ONLY to this project are DELETED — with their scope rows cascaded away they would become GLOBAL, since no rows = every project; link types bypass `delete_type`'s usage guard because the project's own links are still present at hook time), **settings** (`scoped_settings` at project scope — bare `scope_id`), **notify** (project subscriptions), **items** (count + detaches children moved to OTHER projects: `parent_id` has no ON DELETE action), **releases**/**forms**/**timelogging** (counts; worklogs + seconds over both anchors), **mailintake** (BLOCKS when a source's `default_project_id` or a rule's `project_id` is this project — with the default nulled `intake.accept` raises on every message and the webhook 5xxs, so refusing up front beats degrading). `GET /projects/{id}/content` (the inspection) + `DELETE /projects/{id}` (204), both gated on the new GLOBAL atom **`project.delete`** (auth-owned beside `project.create`, `implied_by=global.manage`; global on purpose — `PROJECT_PERMISSIONS` feeds the builtin project Admin role and a delegated admin must not be able to destroy the project). MCP `delete_project` (kernel-enforced on the atom, no `project_param`). jiraimport's rollback now calls `delete_project` instead of its private purge loop. SPA: `DeleteProjectCard` (Danger zone, last on General, shown only to holders of the atom) → dialog with the server's counts, the blockers linked to Settings → Email, and the KEY typed back before the danger button enables. Tests: `tests/test_project_delete.py`. | events | `project.created`, `project.updated`, `project.deleted` | dispatches the in-txn `project.created` hook (workflow/views/itemtypes seed per-project defaults; builtin roles + work categories seed at startup instead) and the `project.inspecting` / `project.deleting` teardown hooks (twelve subscribers, listed left) |

| `settings` | **Two-scope** scalar settings cascade (specs 50/67 — spec 67 retired the workspace layer; a single-workspace deployment only duplicated the instance one): `scoped_settings` table (`scope` ∈ instance/project, nullable `scope_id` = project id or NULL for instance, `key`, JSONB `value`); a typed `SettingKey` alias + the kernel `SettingSpec` **registry** (RADD-891 — see the addendum row below: each owning plugin contributes `settings_keys=(SettingSpec(...),)`, and `types.py`'s `SETTINGS_REGISTRY` is a live Mapping over it, not a dict literal) — a key opts into the cascade by being registered on the module that reads it, its ultimate default = the matching `config.Settings` env attr (`SettingSpec.default`, dynamic `getattr` dispatch — now a kernel property since kernel purity does not forbid `radd.config`), `SettingSpec.scopes` declares where it may be SET (most keys both scopes; `timelog_hours_per_day` is INSTANCE-ONLY), and **`SettingSpec.choices`** (spec 107 cleanup) enumerates a STRING key's accepted values — writes outside the set 409 in `_coerce` (a free-typed "gaurds" must never reach a StrEnum coercion at read time), `list_for_scope` serves `choices`, and the generic `ScopedSettingsEditor` renders a SELECT with friendly labels (`lib/meta.SETTING_CHOICE_LABELS`) instead of a free-text input — `workflow_transition_mode` is the first adopter. `service.resolve(key, project_id?)` returns the project override else the instance override else the env default — the lookup order is filtered to the spec's scopes, so a stale narrower row can never shadow an instance-only key; `set_value`/`clear_value`/`list_for_scope` (workspace scope now 422s at the enum; a write at a scope outside the spec's `scopes` 409s). `PUT/GET/DELETE /scoped-settings` gated per scope (instance-admin / project.manage). Registered keys (now declared where they are READ, not here): `work_week_days` (SLA resolves it per item project; `GET /instance` at instance scope via a deferred import — the one workspace→settings request-time edge), **`timelog_hours_per_day`** (INT, **instance-only** since the spec-67 follow-up — one global `1d`↔seconds factor for every duration parse/format: worklogs, estimates, timesheets, cycle handles; exposed on `GET /instance` for the SPA formatter; migration `c4f8a25d91e7` defensively dropped project-scope rows), **`workflow_transition_mode`** (STRING off\|guards\|strict, spec 61 — `workflow.check_transition` resolves it per item project), **`csat_enabled`** (BOOL, spec 65 — the csat sender resolves it per item project; config default `csat_enabled = False`, so surveys are per-project OPT-IN), **`estimation_points`** (BOOL, spec 70 — config default `False`; gates every story-points surface per project), and the four **directory keys** (spec 85, ALL instance-only, edited on Settings → Directory — the General tab's editor filters them out): `ldap_user_sync_base` (STRING — its env default is the pre-existing `RADD_LDAP_USER_SEARCH_BASE`, wired via the `SettingSpec.config_attr` override for keys whose default lives under a different config attr), `ldap_user_sync_enabled` + `ldap_user_sync_deactivate_missing` (BOOL, default False), `ldap_group_search_base` (STRING). Empty base-DN values fall back to `ldap.service.base_dn` at USE time (in the ldap module's resolvers), so the registered default stays the verbatim env value. **`GET /scoped-settings/resolve?key=&project_id=`** (spec 70) returns one key's cascade-RESOLVED value to ANY member (item.read on the project when given) — the SPA's `usePointsEnabled(projectId)` gate reads it, unlike the manage-gated scope editors. Frontend: workspace-settings **General** tab = the instance-scope editor (product defaults, admin-only), each project's Settings → General = its overrides; the **Server** tab (was "Instance") is deploy status only. Collections (fields/SLA/labels) stay ADDITIVE, not here. Migration `a7c2e19b4f30` promoted workspace rows to instance (one per key, newest wins) and dropped the rest. No events yet (admin-low-frequency). **RADD-891 — settings hosts the cascade mechanism, it no longer owns the keys.** Same shape as RADD-890's fix for `auth.types.Permission`, one registry over: `settings_keys=(SettingSpec…),)` on `RaddPlugin` and `RaddPlugin.settings_sections` (kernel `SettingSectionSpec`, still unwired — no consumer groups the editor by section yet) existed since spec 93 and were read by nothing; now every feature's tunables live on the manifest of the module that resolves them, and `settings` validates against the kernel registry instead of hardcoding every key. `SettingKey` SURVIVES as the typed alias enum (ratcheted by `tests/test_setting_ownership.py`: registry ⊇ enum, registry ⊆ enum, `settings` itself declares zero keys) for the same import-order reason `Permission` did — it types a FastAPI query param and a pydantic field at `settings`'s own fixed, early position in `RADD_MODULES`, long before most owning plugins (`ai`, `ldap`, `releases`, `timelogging`, `workflow`, `items`, `csat`) have registered theirs. Unlike `Permission`, nothing computes eagerly from the full key set at `settings`' import time (no equivalent of `PROJECT_PERMISSIONS`), so there is no static per-key scope/type table to keep in sync — `setting_spec(key)` reads type/scopes/prose/default LIVE off `radd.kernel.registries.settings` on every call. | `auth` | Users, session-cookie login (`radd_session`), personal access tokens (`radd_pat_…`, hash-only storage). **Spec 86 — the authz semantics:** there are no membership rows — every ACTIVE user holds the global member floor (`effective_permissions`/`permissions_for_projects` synthesize the role: inactive → nothing anywhere; instance_role=admin → ALL; any other active user → the member global set + per-project role grants). No `workspace_id` kwarg on the seams; `authz.is_admin(session, user)` is the one admin predicate. The `/workspaces/{id}/members` endpoints are DELETED — **`users.instance_role` (admin\|member) is THE role ladder**, managed via `PATCH /users/{id}` (instance admin; self-demotion 409); the `workspace_memberships` table + its service functions are GONE (SSO/LDAP now set `users.instance_role` directly). `/auth/me` emits a FLAT shape: top-level `global_role` (admin\|member) + the global-scope `permissions` union (`MeRead`). **Roles as data** (spec 06, `auth/roles.py` + `roles_router.py`): `roles` table (builtin admin/member/viewer are GLOBAL rows, ensured on startup via `subscribers.ensure_seeded`, + custom permission sets; `GET/POST /roles` are global, key globally unique `uq_roles_key`), `GET /permissions` catalog (`{key, description, scope, resource, action}` from `PERMISSION_SCOPES`/`PERMISSION_DESCRIPTIONS` + `permission_parts` — spec 50 CRUD atoms feed the resource×action roles matrix; **scope wire values `project`\|`global`\|`instance` — renamed the middle scope from "workspace"** [scope-language only: atom KEYS like `workspace.manage` are unchanged — its resource IS the workspace entity — and the authz helper is now `global_scope_permissions`]), direct project membership as a project-scoped role grant (RADD-929 — `GET /role-grants?project_id=` reads it, `POST`/`DELETE /role-grants` write it; the `project_members` table and its `/projects/{id}/members` endpoints are gone); exports `CurrentUser`/`OptionalUser` deps (`auth/deps.py`) and the RBAC seam `auth/authz.py`: `Permission` enum (defined in `auth/types.py`, re-exported), `effective_permissions` union engine, `require`, `permissions_for_projects`, `subjects_for` → `{role_ids, team_ids}` (feeds the fields grant checks; unit-tested in `server/tests/test_authz.py` — `uv run pytest`). `dashboard.create` doubles as the dashboard-sharing broadcast gate; the builtin admin role carries the triple as global-scoped riders (display-only — global checks key on the member floor; migration `9176930be113` mirrors them into stored rows). **Spec 84 (user administration):** `users.source` (`UserSource` local\|ldap\|oidc\|unknown; The row REALLY goes (cf. merge, which keeps a deactivated shell). Authored work repoints to the successor — REQUIRED when the account owns anything (409), omitted when it owns nothing; **worklogs are DELETED not moved** (crediting someone with hours they never worked would corrupt every timesheet). **RADD-784: ACCESS DIES WITH THE ACCOUNT** — `_DELETE_WITH_ACCOUNT` destroys project/team/group memberships, `team_managers`, user role grants, form shares, participations, watches, stars and the inbox instead of the old merge-dedupe transfer (which silently added the successor to every project the leaver was in, at the leaver's role); user-subject `access_grants` are deleted, never repointed; `teams.owner_id` is EXCLUDED from the delete repoint (SET NULL — a new owner is chosen deliberately, `owned_teams` no longer forces a successor). The flip side is the **viability gate**: `successor_viability` compares EFFECTIVE permissions per scope (global + every project via 2 batched `permissions_for_projects` calls + granted wiki spaces), lattice-aware (`item.read` covers `item.read@own`), and the delete 409s naming what is missing; `GET /users/{id}/successor-check?candidate_id=` is the dialog's preview (`SuccessorCheck{viable, gaps[{scope_type,label,scope_id,missing}]}`); an admin leaver needs an admin successor. `tests/test_successor_viability.py`. Guards (`ensure_deletable`, pure): no self-delete, successor must be active and not the deletee. It is the ONE builtin whose permission set is mutable (`ensure_permissions_mutable` exempts it; `ensure_deletable` still refuses, since a missing baseline drops every non-admin to nothing) — `update_role` calls `authz.forget_baseline(session)` so the admin's own confirming read is not served from the request-scoped memo. **This narrowed live behaviour**: `page.write`, `cycle.manage` (+ its create/update/delete expansion) and `timesheet.view` used to be free for every active user via the second hardcoded set, which is how a member granted nothing anywhere could edit any wiki page and delete any cycle with no screen anywhere saying so; they are ordinary grants now. The decision is forced by the field, never defaulted: **multi_select** drops the value from each list (a shorter list is still valid, no substitute exists to ask for); a **required single select** must name a SURVIVING option; an **optional** one may pass null to clear. Removing the last option, or naming the option being removed as the replacement, is a 422. The field's own `default_value` is migrated in the same transaction — a default pointing at a removed option would seed the dead value onto every item created afterwards, and it is the one nobody would think to check. | `access` | **Generic, scopeable, plugin-registerable access grants (spec 92) — the ONE ACL primitive every RBAC resource shares.** `access_grants` (resource_type + resource_id [string: a uuid or a stable key], subject_type user\|team\|role, subject_id, access [read\|write\|…, resource-declared], `project_id` NULL=global\|set=that project; unique per (resource,subject,access,scope); polymorphic subject_id/resource_id, no FK — a resource clears its grants on delete). **Plugin extension point** = `registry.register_resource(ResourceSpec)` — a module declares its `accesses`, `default_open` (fields open / views closed), `hierarchical` (flags vs ordered levels), `subjects`, `project_scoped`, `implied_by` (write⇒read), and a `can_manage(session, actor, resource_id, project_id)` authz hook; the generic `/grants` API + the reusable `<AccessGrantsEditor>` then work for it with NO new code. Pure resolution (`resolution.py`, unit-tested via test_field_grants): `SubjectContext{user_id, role_ids, team_ids, has_manage}` + `has_access(grants, ctx, access, project_id, spec)` (flag model — open until an in-scope grant restricts, then a matching/implied grant or manage) + `effective_level` (hierarchical) + `in_scope`/`subject_matches`. Service: `list_for_resource`/`grants_for_resources` (batch, no N+1)/`add_grant` (validates subject exists + access in spec + project + dedupe incl. NULL-scope)/`remove_grant`/`clear_resource`. `GET/POST /grants` + `DELETE /grants/{id}` (authz delegated per-grant to the resource's `can_manage`), `GET /grants/resources` (specs for the editor) — **which this row claimed for three specs before RADD-947 actually added it**: `ResourceSpecRead` was written for a route nobody implemented, so `AccessGrantsEditor` took `accesses`/`subjectKinds` as per-call-site props and rendered the project ScopePicker unconditionally, putting an "on <projects>" control on the page-restrict dialog whose write path answers "page grants can't be scoped". The editor now reads the registry (accesses, subjects, `project_scoped`), the props remain as an override, and an unregistered type keeps the old behaviour. Any signed-in caller may read it: it is the SHAPE of the access model, not anyone's grants. | `cycles` | **Spec 86: global scope — the workspace entity is gone; this module has no `workspace_id`, no singleton resolver.** Workspace-level iterations/sprints spanning projects (specs 14+60; **team visibility**: `cycle_teams` join — no rows = public, rows = those teams' members only, bypass = workspace ADMINS deliberately not cycle.manage [every member holds it]; `GET /cycles` filters via `visible_cycles`, get/stats 404 hidden cycles via `cycle_visible_to`; `team_ids` on Create/Read/Update [`[]` on update = public again]; cycle NAME still shows on item chips/SLQ — item-level data, documented; **nullable dates + DRAFT staging, spec 23**): `cycles` table (name, **nullable** start_date/end_date, goal). `CycleStatus` (**draft**/upcoming/active/completed) is **DERIVED, never stored** — `types.cycle_status(start, end, today)` is pure and returns `DRAFT` when either date is unset (a staging area — never active, off the roadmap/velocity/burnup until scheduled); `today` is injected at the service boundary (`date.today` in the router; tests pass a fixed date). `GET /cycles[?status=]`, `POST`/`GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /cycles/{id}` (create/edit/delete gated on `Permission.CYCLE_MANAGE`; list/get on `item.read`, workspace scope). Validation: end_date ≥ start_date **only when both are set** (409); create with no dates = a draft, `CycleUpdate` uses the `model_fields_set` idiom so explicit `null` on a date clears it (scheduled → draft). Deleting a cycle nulls items' `cycle_id` via the FK's `ON DELETE SET NULL` (and CASCADE-drops its stint history — complete cycles instead of deleting them if the carryover trail matters). **Cycle stint history (spec 56):** `item_cycle_records` (item_id FK CASCADE, cycle_id FK CASCADE, added_at, removed_at NULL=open) — one row per VISIT; `history.record_cycle_change` (re-exported via service) is called by the items service on every cycle assignment change (create + update — the complete-cycle carryover move routes through update), closing the old open stint and opening the new one; `past_cycles_by_item_ids` batch-feeds items hydration (`ItemRead.past_cycles`, closed stints only, deduped oldest-first). Migration `c536f26534fb` seeds open rows from current assignments; `scripts/backfill_cycle_history.py` mines historical stints from the event log's `changes` diffs (names → ids; stints of deleted cycles are unrecoverable). **Sprint close (Jira-style):** `completed_at` column = the explicit "Complete sprint" marker (`cycle_status` gained a 4th arg — completed_at wins over dates, so a sprint closes mid-window); `POST /cycles/{id}/complete {move_open_to: cycle|null, start_next}` (gated `cycle.update`) moves OPEN items (state category not done/canceled, not archived) to the target cycle or backlog via the items seam `items.move_open_cycle_items` (per-item `update_item` as the CALLER → authz/events/history apply; cycles→items is a **deferred import**, items imports cycles for hydration), optionally starts the target today (end = start + closed cycle's length, fallback 2 weeks), then tops up drafts. **Recurring series (per-label auto-provisioning):** `cycle_series` table (label, drafts_ahead, next_number, nullable start_weekday [0=Mon]+duration_days; unique GLOBAL `uq_cycle_series_label`) — a cycle belongs to a series by NAME (`types.parse_cycle_name`/`cycle_label`: label + trailing number, separators cosmetic, case-insensitive `same_label`), NO FK, so imported/pre-existing cycles join automatically. Created via `CycleCreate.recurring` (+`drafts_ahead`, optional `next_number` for a bare label — Jira-import continuity, numbering can start at 120); managed at `GET /cycle-series` (member) + `PATCH/DELETE /cycle-series/{id}` (cycle.update/delete; delete = stop recurring, cycles untouched). `ensure_series_drafts` tops the label up to `drafts_ahead` future (draft/upcoming) cycles named `label - next_number` (pure `series_draft_names` skips numbers an import already took); runs on cycle create + complete + series update, deliberately NOT on cycle delete (would make drafts undeletable). **Cadence (both-or-neither, 409 on half):** with start_weekday+duration_days set, provisioned cycles get real timelines — windows from pure `series_windows` (chained after the label's latest scheduled end, or today when stale/none; starts snapped to the weekday via `next_weekday_on_or_after`, so 14-day Monday cycles run back-to-back and unaligned durations gap to the next weekday), existing DATELESS drafts of the label are scheduled first (number order), and a recurring create without explicit dates self-schedules its first window. The complete-flow UI offers same-label future cycles as move targets. **Stats:** `GET /cycles/{id}/stats?assignee_id&team_id&project_id` (item.read) → per-state-category counts + estimate/logged/remaining totals (remaining = Σ max(est−logged,0) per item) formatted with the workspace's hours-per-day + **`points_total`/`points_done`** (spec 70 — always computed via the items seam `cycle_points_totals`, done = done-category states, same filters; UI shows points only where enabled) — composed in the router from the items seam `cycle_state_category_counts` + timelogging seam `timesheet.cycle_time_totals` (both deferred imports; both honor the cycle page's assignee/team filters). **`project_id` narrows the workspace-spanning cycle to one project's slice — project-scoped surfaces (planning page, project views' cycle handles) MUST pass it or a DEV handle shows TD time; the workspace-level cycle page stays unscoped by design.** Helpers other modules import: `get_cycle`, `cycles_by_ids`, `active_cycles(today)` (excludes explicitly-closed), `list_cycles`, `cycle_status` | projects, auth, events, settings (stats hpd), items + timelogging (deferred: complete/stats only) | `cycle.created`, `cycle.updated`, `cycle.deleted`, `cycle.completed` (payload: name, start_date, end_date); `cycle_series.created/.updated/.deleted` (payload: label, drafts_ahead, next_number) | — |

Setting `status→released` stamps `released_at` server-side (and `→planned` clears it). A merged PR moves work to the waiting state — which lives in the **`done` category**, so throughput counts the day the work was finished rather than the day a tag was cut, and the later move to the shipped state does not count it twice (the timeline collapses same-category transitions). **A version that BECOMES released sweeps, whichever path did it (RADD-1007):** every write goes through `pipeline.create_release`/`pipeline.update_release` (REST POST/PATCH, MCP `create_release`/`update_release`, the connectors' `on_release_published`), which sweep every waiting item into the shipped state with `release_id` set on the planned→released transition — as SYSTEM for a connector, as the actor otherwise — which is what satisfies a project guarding entry to Done on a release being present. Re-saving an already-released version does not re-sweep; `POST /releases/{id}/sweep` (release.update), the MCP `sweep_release` tool and the Releases page's **Sweep** button re-run it on demand for work that reached the waiting state later, and are the recovery for a missed webhook. `on_release_published` find-or-creates the version (webhooks are at-least-once) and stores the notes WHOLE — the pipeline used to slice them at 2000 characters, which cut every substantial changelog mid-word (RADD-907). Deliberately "everything waiting", not commit-range-derived. MCP: `list_releases` (lean: no notes), `get_release` + `update_release` by (project_key, version) (RADD-908 — an agent can read and amend the notes of a release a connector published), `create_release`, `sweep_release`, `set_item_release`. Tests: `tests/test_release_pipeline.py` (12). |

workspace-spanning roadmaps (project_id NULL) span readable projects; collapse/tray-filter localStorage is VIEW-scoped. **Queue views (spec 64):** `ViewType.QUEUE` — the service-desk triage skin: a fixed-column list variant (reporter, age, ALWAYS-ON spec-63 SLA chips via `POST /items/sla/batch`; default order = breached first → ascending due_at → created_at asc, computed client-side over the loaded page when the view's SLQ has no explicit ORDER BY), axes stored-but-ignored like planning; quick filters/SLQ bar/pagination unchanged. **`POST /views/counts`** `{view_ids: [≤50]}` → `{view_id: count}` (`views/counts.py`): one compiled-SLQ `SELECT count(*)` per view, visibility-filtered exactly like the read path — invisible/unknown ids OMITTED (never errored), quick filters NOT applied (base membership count), archived items + projects without item.read excluded so the badge matches the rows the actor would see; a stored query that no longer compiles omits that view instead of failing the batch. Powers the sidebar's collapsible **Queues** section (one batched call, 60s re-poll; queue views render there only — excluded from the generic Views lists). **Seeded project views (simplified per user direction — NO surface/designation concept):** every project ships with four PLAIN views — Board (group_by state), List, Planning, Roadmap (the fourth since spec 79) — created by an in-txn hook on `project.created` (`views/subscribers.py` → `defaults.seed_project_views`; backfill migrations `62681dd4006c` + `d1f80c2eedc3` [Roadmap, spec 79], the interim `project_default` marker column was dropped by `004bbb60df13`). They're ordinary views: workspace-visible (`workspace_access` viewer), owner NULL (managed via the view.* atoms → admins), editable and DELETABLE like any other. The sidebar simply lists each project's views (queues excepted — own section); `/p/$projectKey` redirects to the project's first view (`ProjectHomePage`; empty state when none). Project-scoped view pages carry the New-item button + `c` hotkey (moved from the deleted builtin pages). **Ownership + sharing (spec 57):** `owner_id` = the creator (never surrendered by sharing; full control — edit/share/delete; NULL only on LEGACY pre-57 shared rows, managed via the view.* atoms); per-subject shares are **access grants since spec 92** (resource_type=`view`, accesses viewer|editor|**owner** [= co-ownership: full control incl. re-share/delete/transfer], hierarchical, owner-closed default; the `view_shares` table was migrated into `access_grants` + DROPPED by `925809931622`), managed through the generic `/grants` API; `views.global_access` (ShareLevel|NULL = what every active user gets; NEVER 'owner' → 409) stays on the View row. Visibility = owner ∪ user/team grantees ∪ active users when global_access set — everyone else 404s, ADMINS INCLUDED (privacy over acknowledgment); editor grantees edit the definition, owner + co-owners re-share/delete/transfer. `PUT /views/{id}/sharing` sets ONLY `global_access` since spec 92 (owner-gated; enabling global_access needs view.create — it's a broadcast); per-subject grants go through `/grants` (RADD-869 fixed the endpoint description that still promised the full pre-92 replace). `POST /views/{id}/transfer {user_id}` reassigns owner_id (target needs item.read in scope → 409; previous owner kept as editor grantee — no accidental lockout). `ViewRead` carries `owner`/`global_access`/`shares` + per-actor `can_edit`/`can_manage` (client never re-derives team membership). Table `views` (cont.): `project_id` NULL = workspace-spanning, `query` TEXT (SLQ, '' = everything; compile-validated on write against the view's scope registry → 422 `{detail, position}`), `group_by`/`swimlane_by` axis tokens (`state\|assignee\|priority\|kind\|team\|cycle\|cf.<key>` where `<key>` must be a select-type registry field in scope — non-select/unknown cf → 409, same token on both axes → 409, malformed token → 422; nullable, accepted for lists too; **`cycle` (spec 23)** is a builtin axis, no extra validation — buckets are the workspace cycles + a Backlog bucket, resolved client-side), `cycle_filter` TEXT (**spec 23, regex since spec 56**, nullable, ≤200 chars — an optional cycle-name REGEX narrowing the `cycle`-axis header set, case-insensitive + unanchored, e.g. `^TS` or `PIPE|TS`; compile-validated on write → 409, matched client-side by `matchesCycleFilter`, `model_fields_set` clear on PATCH; the ViewModal always exposes the field for PLANNING views — they're implicitly cycle-grouped), `position` for ordering; **`wip_limits` (spec 76)** — nullable JSONB `{state_id: int>=1}`, SOFT limits for STATE-axis board columns only: the column header renders `n/limit` (amber at the limit, red above, tooltip "WIP limit N") and nothing ever blocks a drop; with `project_id` also surfaces workspace-spanning views; ordered position→name), `POST` (item.read; `global_access`/legacy `shared:true` alias: view.create), `PATCH` (owner / editor grantee / legacy view.update), `DELETE` (owner / legacy view.delete).
