| `reporting` | **: every report endpoint takes an optional SLQ `q`** — the dashboard-wide filter seam: the service intersects each report's item-id universe with `items.bulk.visible_matching_ids` (uncapped, RBAC'd; new seam) BEFORE the history fold. Deliberate semantics: SLQ evaluates CURRENT item state, so `assignee = me` means "items assigned to me now — that subset's history". `POST /views/counts` likewise gained `extra_q` (compiled separately, predicate-level AND with each view's own query). Invalid `q` → the usual 422; a bad `extra_q` omits the count like a stale view query. **Spec 86: global scope — the workspace entity is gone; this module has no `workspace_id`, no singleton resolver.** Read-only analytics/dashboards (spec 16) computed on the fly from the event log + cycles — **NO tables, NO migration**. `timeline.py` reconstructs each item's state history from `item.created`/`item.updated` payloads: `item_state_timeline(session, item_ids)` (the spec helper) / `build_item_timelines` walk an item's events in id order and diff `state.id`/`category` into ordered `(entered_at, exited_at, state_id, category)` segments (open final segment = still there); entering a `done`-category state from a non-done one is a *completion* tagged with the item's cycle at that moment, and the `(at, cycle_id)` trail feeds burnup's scope-over-time. Item scope is itself read from the outbox (`item_ids_for_project` via `item.created` payload `project_id`; `item_ids_for_cycle` via any event's `cycle.id`). Endpoints all gated on `item.read` for the scope: `GET /reports/throughput?project_id=&start=&end=&interval=day\|week` (items entering a done state per bucket, `[{bucket,count}]`), `/reports/cumulative-flow` (per-category counts at each bucket end, `[{bucket,counts}]`), `/reports/time-in-state?project_id=[&kind=]` (avg+median hours per category over *completed* stays, `[{category,avg_hours,median_hours,sample}]`), `/reports/velocity?workspace_id=&last=N[&measure=count\|points]` (items completed while assigned, per recent *completed* cycle, `[{cycle,completed}]`; `measure=points` — spec 70 — sums CURRENT `estimate_points` [null = 0] over the same item sets via the items seam `estimate_points_by_ids`, row shape unchanged, default `count` = exact prior behavior), `/reports/burnup?cycle_id=[&measure=]` (daily `{date,scope,completed}` over the cycle window, same measure switch; **409 for a draft/undated cycle — no window to plot, spec 23**), and **`/reports/sla?workspace_id=[&project_id=][&weeks=1..26]`** (spec 63, default 12, `item.read` at workspace scope like velocity): weekly buckets over the SLA engine's `sla_item_states` bookkeeping (read via `slas/report.py`, a models-only seam) joined to items and bucketed by ITEM-CREATED week → `[{week, items, response_met, response_breached, resolution_met, resolution_breached, breach_rate, avg_response_seconds, avg_resolution_seconds}]` — met = met without a breach stamp (met-late counts as breached), averages are wall-clock from item creation to the met stamp; **csat fields (spec 65)**: each bucket also carries `csat_avg`/`csat_count` folded in via `_SlaWeekFold` from the csat module's models-only seam (`csat/report.py`) — DELIBERATE ASYMMETRY: SLA counters bucket by item-CREATED week, csat by the week the RESPONSE arrived (`responded_at`). Windows default to the last 30 days; reversed window → 422. NOTE: reporting↔slas is a declared-one-way soft cycle — slas depends_on reporting (uses `timeline.py`); reporting's service imports `slas/report.py` but CANNOT declare `slas` in depends_on (slas loads later in RADD_MODULES); safe because `slas/report.py` imports models only. The same idiom covers reporting→csat (`csat/report.py`, models only — csat loads last in RADD_MODULES). **RADD-789 — the cross-project reports were unscoped.** `_matching_ids` returned None whenever `q` was absent, so the ONLY thing narrowing velocity/burnup/sla was the dashboard query — `sla_report(project_id=None)` folded every project's bookkeeping rows (and every project's csat ratings) into the averages, with the global `item.read` gate the sole thing in front of it. It now ALWAYS applies visibility and intersects the query on top, so the RBAC half cannot be skipped by omitting `q`; `csat/report.py`'s row gained `item_id` so ratings intersect the same set. The three cross-project responses are WRAPPED — `VelocityReport {rows, scope}`, `SlaReport {buckets, scope}`, `BurnupSeries` gains `scope` — carrying `ReportScope {covered: project keys, total}`: a filtered LIST is visibly shorter, a filtered AVERAGE is just a different number, so the figure states what it was computed over and the SPA's `ScopeNote` renders "across 2 of 5 projects you can read" — and nothing at all when covered == total, since a note on every report stops being read. Floor gates are `authz.require_member` (RADD-788); `/reports/sla?project_id=` resolves against that project instead. | events, projects, auth, workflow, cycles, items, slas + csat (report seams, undeclared — see note) | — | — |
| `forms` | Intake forms (spec 17): `forms` table (project_id FK, name, description, enabled, `fields` JSONB [ordered `{field_key, label_override?, help?, required}`], `defaults` JSONB [`{kind?, state_name?, priority?, labels?[], assignee_email?, cycle_name?, release_version?}`], `title_prompt`, and the ITEM-description area config `description_enabled` [default true] / `description_prompt` / `description_required` — submits carry `description`, validated like a required override and passed into `ItemCreate`; disabled area ignores it). CRUD `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE /forms?project_id=` gated on `Permission.FORM_MANAGE` (project scope); `GET /forms/{id}` open to `item.create` so submitters can render it. Write validation: every `field_key` must exist in the project's registry scope (`definitions_for_project`) → 409 unknown; unknown state/label/cycle/release default names are stored verbatim and resolved at submit, an unknown `assignee_email` → 409. `POST /forms/{id}/submit {title, values}` (gated `item.create`, 409 on a disabled form) builds an `ItemCreate` from the defaults (state by NAME, assignee by EMAIL, cycle by NAME, release by VERSION — unresolved names degrade to unset/the project default state) and calls `items.create_item`: the form's own `required` overrides run first → 422 naming the fields (pure `validation.missing_required_keys`, unit-tested in `test_forms.py`), then the registry `validate_custom_fields` → 422 (unknown key / bad value); returns the created `ItemRead`. **Public forms, then requesters (spec 62 → superseded by RADD-828):** Form originally gained `allow_public` + a unique nullable `public_token` for a tokened, no-login `GET`/`POST /public/forms/{token}` submit path — `submit_form` ran as the SYSTEM actor, an unmatched email left the reporter NULL and called `mailintake.upsert_contact`/`send_ack` to make the requester addressable, and spec 74 hung a tokened `GET /public/forms/{token}/deflect?q=` KB-search off the same public router. **RADD-828 deleted all of it**: `public_router.py`/`public.py` are gone, `public_token` is gone (dropped column), and this module has ZERO mailintake or docs imports left — see the `forms`/`mailintake`/`auth` addendum below and `docs/specs/62-requester-loop.md`'s pointer. `allow_public` SURVIVES, narrowed to exactly what spec 73 already used it for: the portal-visibility flag (paired with share rows, below) — a signed-in eligible user, never an anonymous one. External requesters now reach the tracker exclusively through mailintake's email intake (an unrecognized sender auto-provisions a `UserSource.EMAIL` account as reporter); `submit_form`'s keyword `reporter_id` override survives for the two SIGNED-IN surfaces that still use it (the authenticated submit page's default "actor becomes reporter", and the portal's explicit `reporter_id=actor.id`). **Spec 106** (now describing the two remaining, both-authenticated form pages): each renders the item description in the rich markdown editor (`FormDescriptionArea` → `LazyRichEditor`) and shares a two-column layout with `FormAssistPanel` (title-seeded deflect via `GET /search/deflect` + whole-draft `POST /ai/similar`) beside the form — the shared `IntakeSubmitShell` (RADD-901) composes both the authed submit page and the portal page, mounted twice (inline narrow / sticky aside wide — the queries dedupe). **Requester portal (spec 73)**: `form_shares` grant rows (id, form_id FK CASCADE ix, exactly-one-of user_id/team_id — the item_participants DB CHECK idiom, both FK CASCADE; unique per (form,user)/(form,team); presence = the subject SEES the form on the portal AND may SUBMIT it, no levels; user-merge dedupes via auth `_MERGE_DEDUPE` (form_id,user_id)). `PUT /forms/{id}/sharing {shares:[{user_id|team_id}]}` — full-replace (delete-then-insert, views idiom), `form.manage`; subjects validated 409 (dupe / inactive-or-non-member user [instance admins pass] / cross-workspace team); `FormRead.shares` populated on manage surfaces for the builder; emits `form.updated` w/ `share_count`. Authenticated portal router (`portal_router.py` + `portal.py`): `GET /portal/forms` (ANY signed-in user — eligibility IS the gate) lists enabled forms across ALL workspaces where `allow_public` OR a share matches the actor/their teams (`teams.all_user_team_ids`), grouped `[{project:{id,key,name}, forms:[{id,name,description}]}]`; `GET /portal/forms/{id}` renders for an eligible actor (ineligible/disabled/unknown → one 404) via the spec-62 trimming (`public.trimmed_read`, definitions inlined) + form id/project ref (`PortalFormRead`); `POST /portal/forms/{id}/submit {title, values, description?}` runs `submit_form` as the SYSTEM actor with `reporter_id=actor.id` (known user — NO mail-contact/ack path; the share is the grant, no item.create needed) → `{key, title}`. Web: sidebar "Portal" entry (everyone) → `/portal` directory (cards grouped by project) → `/portal/forms/$formId` submit page (public-form layout + the authed `FormAssistPanel`, spec 106); builder "Portal sharing" block (user/team chips, full-list PUT) beside the public-link toggle. Tests: `test_portal.py`. **RADD-785 — `GET /portal/requests`**: the items where the actor is the REPORTER, scoped by relationship rather than permission (no `item.read` is asked of anyone) — which is the whole point, because the configuration that makes a clean requester (a Baseline carrying no read) is exactly the one that would otherwise leave them unable to see the ticket they filed. `PortalRequestRead` is the security boundary and a test asserts its field set: key/title/state/state_category/project/timestamps, never description, comments, assignee or fields — being the reporter must not become a back door into an issue's contents. It rides its own `/portal/requests` ROUTER, not `/portal/forms/requests`, which `/{form_id}` would shadow (RADD-761). **RADD-786**: My Work renders the eligible forms + a compact request list (both render NOTHING when empty), so a requester's landing page is the whole product for them. **Spec 119 — submission goes through intake validation.** `FormSubmit` gains `commit` (`"pass"` default | `"always"` = the advisory submit-anyway), and `service._create_validated` routes the build through `automations.intake.create_or_refuse` instead of calling `items.create_item` directly — DEFERRED and feature-detected (the `email_action.py` shape), because `automations` is optional and loads after `forms`; with it absent a submit is exactly the one `create_item` it always was. The `form_id` goes in, which is what makes a FORM-targeted binding match rather than only the project's. **Ordering with `staging.claim` matters and already held**: `portal.submit_portal_form` claims staged attachments AFTER `submit_form` returns, so a rejected draft never takes ownership of its files — they stay unclaimed, get swept on the usual schedule, and a resubmission can claim them again. **`service.validation_context(session, form)`** answers `{governed, mode}` for BOTH submit pages — `PortalFormRead.validation` and `FormRead.validation` (populated by `render_form` only; null on the LIST, which would otherwise resolve it per row for nobody). Off the form's own payload rather than `GET /items/validate/context` for two reasons: a portal visitor's right to be there is the SHARE, and an endpoint gated on `item.create` would 403 exactly the people the form exists for; and only the server knows the TYPE a submission will carry — **`service.effective_type_id`** resolves the form's `type_name` default and falls back to the project's default type exactly as `create_item` does, so a type-targeted binding is visible on the button instead of arriving as a 422 (the client passed `type_id=null` and could not have known). | workspace, auth, teams, fields, workflow, labels, cycles, releases, items, events (weak: attachments, automations [SYSTEM actor id]) — **mailintake and docs are GONE from this list since RADD-828**: the tokened public path that reached them directly is deleted, so forms has zero imports of either now (KB deflection reaches `search`, not `docs`, from the authenticated `FormAssistPanel`) | `form.created`, `form.updated`, `form.deleted` (payload: name, project_id, enabled; sharing updates add `share_count`) | `exception_handlers` (form-required validation → 422) |
| `timelogging` | **Spec 86: global scope — the workspace entity is gone; this module has no `workspace_id`, no singleton resolver.** Time logging + timesheets (specs 22+59) — **per-project-optional** plugin, module-owned tables so `items` stays independent. **Spec 59 itemless/general worklogs:** `worklogs.item_id` NULLABLE + optional `project_id` anchor (`ck_worklogs_scope` CHECK: `item_id IS NOT NULL OR category_id IS NOT NULL`) — `POST /worklogs` logs meetings/admin time with **category REQUIRED** (an itemless row's identity; clearing it 409s), project-anchored → worklog.write + enablement, itemless → worklog.write on ≥1 project (any-project gate; the global member floor untouched); edit/delete authz is scope-aware (`service.worklog_scope`); timesheet outer-joins items (`TimesheetEntry.item` nullable + top-level `project_key`; a project filter keeps only that project's itemless rows), worklog event payloads carry null-safe `item_id` + `project_id`/`category_id`; UI: per-category totals strip + **By-category grouping** + Log-time modal on `/timesheet`; cycle totals unaffected (item-joined only). `project_timelogging` (project_id PK, `enabled`; absent row = disabled) gates writes via `require_enabled` (409); `PUT/GET /projects/{id}/timelogging` (PUT = `project.manage`). `work_categories` (GLOBAL — `uq_work_categories_name`, seeded on startup via `categories.ensure_seeded`, archivable, `GET/POST /work-categories`, `PATCH /work-categories/{id}`; POST/PATCH = `canned.manage`-style global gate). `item_estimates` (one row/item, `original_estimate_seconds`; remaining DERIVED = estimate−logged; batch seams `estimate_seconds_by_items`/`logged_seconds_by_items` feed the items rollup — spec 76 — and `POST /items/timelog/batch` — spec 78: `{item_ids: [≤200]}` → `{item_id: {estimate_seconds, logged_seconds}}`, readable ids only via `authz.permissions_for_projects` (sla/batch idiom, readable ids always present so None/0 ≠ "not allowed", cap 422 schema-enforced), the roadmap auto-scheduler's duration source). `worklogs` (item_id, author=current user, `worked_on` **Date** so the timesheet needs no TZ math, `time_spent_seconds`, optional `category_id` SET NULL, `note`): `POST /items/{id}/worklogs` + `PATCH/DELETE /worklogs/{id}` (author+`worklog.write` OR `project.manage`). `PUT/DELETE /items/{id}/estimate` (`item.update`) + `GET /items/{id}/timelog` (`item.read`) = per-item summary (estimate/logged/remaining + entries). `GET /timesheet?start&end[&user_id&team_id&project_id]` = flat worklog entries + total for the day/week/month reports (UI pivots them; team filter expands via `teams.list_team_members`; without `timesheet.view` the caller is scoped to their OWN worklogs). Jira-style durations (`2h 30m`) parse/format in pure `duration.py` (unit-tested, the module's core invariant; `DurationError`→422 via an `exception_handler`; the `1d`==N-hours factor is **GLOBAL** (spec-67 follow-up: instance override → env default, the per-project value is retired so durations mean the same thing everywhere) — `service._hours_per_day(session)` → `settings.resolve(TIMELOG_HOURS_PER_DAY)`, threaded into `_parse`/`_fmt`/`hydrate`/`item_summary`; the SPA reads the same value off `GET /instance`; days-per-week still the env default). `timesheet.py` joins `work_items`/`projects` read-only (a tolerated inward read like reporting reads `events`). **RADD-1258 — mirrored worklogs:** `worklogs` carries provenance (`external_source` = VcsProvider or '', `external_scope` = the ref's external id `pr:<repo>:<n>`, `external_id` = the provider's own entry id) under the partial UNIQUE index `uq_worklogs_external (external_source, external_id) WHERE external_id <> ''` — the guarantee that a re-delivered webhook, a backfill after a webhook or two deliveries racing land on ONE row. `external.py` is the seam the connectors reach through `vcs.timemirror`: `upsert_external_worklog` (savepoint insert → update on the index conflict, ordinary `worklog.*` events as the AUTHOR) and `reconcile_external_worklogs(source, scope, entries)` — upsert every entry, DELETE the mirrored rows under that scope the source no longer has (so `/remove_time_spent` un-logs here), skip + count items whose project has time logging off. Deletion is scoped by the ref, never the item: two MRs may log to one issue. `authorize_mutation` REFUSES (409) update/delete on a row with `external_source`, admins included — the entry is corrected where it was logged; `WorklogRead`/`TimesheetEntry` expose `external_source` (+ `external_scope`), the SPA badges them and disables their controls with the reason. | events, projects, auth, teams, items, settings | `worklog.created`, `worklog.updated`, `worklog.deleted` (payload: item_id, author_id, worked_on, time_spent_seconds) | `on_startup` ensure `categories.ensure_seeded` (seeds default categories globally; spec 86 retired the `workspace.created` hook), `exception_handlers` (DurationError→422) |
| `automations` | **What is actually STORED (spec 116, revised — an automation IS a DAG). Four tables, and none of them is `automation_rules`:** **`automations`** (id, name, enabled, **`nodes` JSONB** `[{id, kind, type, params, x, y}]`, **`edges` JSONB** `[{source, port, target}]`, `position`, `orientation`, `created_by_id` — `graph.validate` runs on WRITE *and* again on read, so a hand-crafted row cannot wedge the engine); **`automation_triggers`** (PK `(automation_id, node_id)`, `event_type`, `schedule` JSONB — one row per TRIGGER node, the engine's and the scheduler's index, rebuilt wholesale by `service._sync_triggers` on every write, never diffed); **`automation_schedule_state`** (PK `(automation_id, node_id)`, `next_run_at`/`last_run_at`); **`automation_validations`** (spec 119 — see the end of this row). The pre-116 `automation_rules` table and its `trigger`/`event_conditions`/`condition_slq`/`actions` COLUMNS are gone: what follows names that vocabulary because the node `params` still speak it, not because any of it is a column. (Spec 86: global scope — no `workspace_id` anywhere in this module, no singleton resolver.) **The vocabulary, specs 15+58** — `trigger` = a RAW EVENT-TYPE string from `catalog.py`'s `TRIGGERS` map [63 subscribable types across items/comments/worklogs/attachments/links/cycles/releases/docs/admin (incl. the spec-61 `workflow_transition.*` admin rows) + the spec-65 "Service desk" group (`csat.requested`/`csat.responded`, item-scoped; spec-71 added the five `approval.*` events there, item-scoped) — validated on write; `"manual"` = on-demand], `event_conditions` JSONB [spec 58: nestable all/any/none tree of `{subject, qualifier?, operator, value?}` over the TRIGGERING EVENT — subjects `actor`(id/email/name)/`changed_field`/`old_value`/`new_value`(per-field diff incl. labels added\|removed + custom-field keys)/`state_category`/`payload`(dotted path); 11 operators incl. regex + numeric; pure evaluator `conditions.py`, tested in `tests/test_automation_conditions.py`; depth≤5/nodes≤50 guards], `condition_slq` TEXT [`''` = always; compiled+validated on write against the field registry → 422 `{detail, position}`, reusing `items.slq`], `actions` JSONB [validated `{type, params}` union — `ActionType`: set_state/set_priority/set_assignee(email\|none)/set_team(name\|none)/add_label/remove_label/set_cycle(name\|none)/set_release(version\|none)/set_custom_field/add_comment + spec-58b UNIVERSAL actions create_item(project key + templated title/desc)/send_webhook(HMAC-SHA256 `X-Radd-Signature` when secret set)/post_chat({"text"} to Chat/Slack-style webhook)/notify_user(in-app `NotificationType.AUTOMATION`)/**send_email (spec 66: `{to, subject, body}` — subject/body templated; `to` = literal address or `EmailRecipient` role reporter/assignee/contact [spec-62 mailintake seam, DEFERRED feature-detected import in `email_action.py` — mailintake loads after automations]; a role resolves through `mailintake.service.mailable_user` since RADD-983, not a bare `user.active` check — the account must be a real, active, non-service, non-system mailbox; role w/o a resolvable target OR outbound mail unconfigured → skip-log; sends via `mailintake.service.send_plain_mail` [the ONE outbound seam, RADD-983 — was `radd.smtp.send_message` direct], emits nothing = loop-safe)**/**assign_round_robin (RADD-1044: `{team}` — a team NAME whose members it rotates through, item-arity like `set_assignee`; walks a stable user-id order per `team_assignment_cursors` [one row per team, migration `d1044rrcursor`], skipping inactive members and anyone AWAY [deferred feature-detected reach into `leave`, optional — without it only inactive members are skipped]; the cursor advances to whoever got the ticket, never to a skipped member, so a run with no eligible member leaves it where it was)** — universal actions run even on itemless triggers, `{{token}}` templates (`templating.py`, pure: event_type/actor.*/payload.path/item.key) render per event; `ITEM_ACTIONS` registry gates the item-bound set], position). CRUD `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE /automations` gated on `Permission.AUTOMATION_MANAGE` (global-scoped → instance admins only); `GET /automations/catalog` (trigger/subject/operator metadata the rule-builder UI renders from); `POST /automations/{id}/test {item_id}` dry-run preview (matched + which actions WOULD apply, no writes). **Manual rules (editor quick actions):** `AutomationTrigger.MANUAL` rules never event-fire — they run on demand via `POST /automations/{id}/run {item_id}` (gated `item.update` on the item's project, not `automation.manage`: manual rules are admin-curated precisely so members can invoke them; condition SLQ still respected → `{ran:false}` on no-match) and are listed member-visible (names only) at `GET /automations/runnable` — the editor's `/` menu shows them as custom actions, so rules created in settings, by extensions, or via MCP become slash actions with zero editor changes. **Engine** (`engine.py` + `dispatcher.py`, mirrors the webhooks dispatcher, `on_startup`/`on_shutdown`, consumer offset `automations.engine`): polls the outbox; for each CATALOG event whose actor isn't the system actor (spec 58: any subscribable type — the target item resolves from the event entity or the payload's `item_id`, the stream-wide convention; itemless triggers evaluate event conditions but skip item actions with a log), loads enabled automations with a `automation_triggers` row for that event type, evaluates `event_conditions` against the event facts (actor id/email/name resolved once) then `condition_slq` against THAT item (compiled SLQ WHERE guarded to the one item id — reuses `items.slq`, no reimplementation), and on a match applies actions via the items/comments/labels/cycles/releases **services** as the system actor. Each action runs in its own SAVEPOINT (best-effort: a failing/unresolved action logs and continues, never crashes the engine); one transaction per event; names→ids resolved at apply time (unknown target = skip+log). **Loop guard:** every engine-applied mutation carries `actor_id = SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID` (a real, login-disabled instance-admin `users` row seeded by the migration so `comments.author_id` resolves), and the engine skips any item event with that actor — so a rule keyed on its own effect applies once and never spins. **Scheduled rules (spec 69):** `AutomationTrigger.SCHEDULE` joins `manual` as a sentinel trigger — the rule carries a `schedule` JSONB (`ScheduleConfig`: `{kind: interval, minutes>=5}` \| `{kind: daily, time HH:MM}` \| `{kind: weekly, time, weekdays [0=Mon]}` \| **`{kind: monthly, time, day 1-31}`** \| **`{kind: cron, expression}`** (RADD-909/910), shape-validated 422; schedule present iff trigger==schedule + event_conditions must be empty → 409, `service._check_schedule_consistency`). Times run on ONE instance clock, `RADD_SCHEDULER_TZ` (IANA, default UTC). Bookkeeping in **`automation_schedule_state`** (rule_id PK FK CASCADE, `next_run_at`, `last_run_at`; naive UTC like every engine timestamp) — (re)computed on rule create/update/enable via pure `schedule.py: next_run(cfg, now, tz)` (interval anchors on now; daily/weekly = next wall-clock occurrence strictly after now via zoneinfo, DST keeps local time). **RADD-909/910 — monthly + cron.** Monthly is CLAMPED to the month's last day (the 31st fires on 28 February) rather than skipping: "monthly on the 31st" is someone asking for month-end, and a maintenance ticket that silently misses four months a year is worse than one arriving three days early. Cron is five fields via `croniter`, resolved in `RADD_SCHEDULER_TZ` like every other kind — delegated rather than parsed here because cron ORs day-of-month against day-of-week when both are restricted, a rule every hand-rolled parser gets wrong for years. The 5-minute floor cannot be READ off a cron config (`* * * * *` names no number), so `cron_shortest_gap` MEASURES it from consecutive occurrences. **Shape validation moved into `radd/schedule.py: validate_config`** — automations and backups each carried their own copy, already disagreeing about a stray `weekdays` on a daily schedule, and two more kinds in two places is how that becomes three differences; a falsy field counts as ABSENT, since stored configs carry `weekdays: []`. `service._check_trigger` calls it on write: when spec 116 moved triggers into node `params`, that envelope stopped being typed and a nonsense cron was stored happily and became a 500 in the scheduler — it is a 409 on the form again. **`POST /automations/schedule/preview`** (authenticated; body = a CANDIDATE config, deliberately not `ScheduleConfig`, whose validator would 422 before the handler could phrase the answer) returns the next five occurrences or the refusal as `error` — the only readable form of a cron expression, and where the monthly clamp becomes self-evident (31 Aug, 30 Sep, 31 Oct). Served rather than computed in the SPA because it is only worth anything if it agrees with the engine, and calling `next_run` is how that is guaranteed; the shared `ScheduleEditor` debounces it and shows the error inline, so an expression that would be refused on save says so while it is still being typed. It lives on this router because this module already owns the schedule vocabulary the API exposes — without `automations` loaded the backup form simply shows no preview. A second PeriodicLoop (`scheduler.py`, `RADD_AUTOMATION_SCHEDULER_INTERVAL` default 60s, gated `run_workers`) fires due rows: emits synthetic outbox event **`automation.scheduled`** (entity `automation`, actor SYSTEM, payload `{rule_id, node_id, scheduled_for}`) + advances `next_run_at` in the SAME txn — at-most-once per occurrence, a missed window fires once, never a backlog. The engine consumer special-cases it BEFORE the loop-guard skip (`should_process`), then `engine.apply_scheduled`: loads the payload rule (skip disabled/deleted/re-typed); empty `condition_slq` → UNIVERSAL actions once, itemless; non-empty → compiled against the field registry and ITEM actions run per matching active item (rank order, capped `RADD_AUTOMATION_SCHEDULE_MAX_ITEMS` default 200, truncation logged), then universal actions ONCE with `{{matched_count}}` available to templates; per-action savepoints as on the event path. Loop safety holds: the resulting item events carry the SYSTEM actor, which the event-rule path still skips. `RuleRead` carries `schedule`/`next_run_at`/`last_run_at` (hydrated via `service.rule_reads`); the catalog gains `schedule_trigger` + `schedule_kinds`; the builder UI gains the "On a schedule" trigger + schedule editor and next/last-run chips on the rules list. **Spec 116 — a rule IS a graph** (`nodes`/`edges` JSONB replacing trigger/condition/action columns; `trigger` + `schedule` are denormalised FROM the trigger node so one place says what starts an automation): a DAG whose nodes pass a `Packet` of `(event facts, item set)`. Kinds — **trigger** (several per graph: "on create OR every Monday" is one automation; a firing event starts the run at the trigger that matched), **source** (RADD-919 — `search.slq`, the only kind that PRODUCES items: runs SLQ mid-graph, `mode` replace\|add, optional project scope, capped by `automation_schedule_max_items`; `engine._scheduled_matches` delegates to the same `search.find_items`, so a schedule trigger's `query` and a search node share one compile/cap/log path), **filter** (per-item predicate → `matched`/`unmatched`), **gate** (a question about the EVENT → one port), **action**. `graph.py` is pure (parse/validate/topological order; cycles rejected on WRITE, so a cycle is a form error rather than a hung consumer); `executor.py` walks it with a `RunBudget` that RECORDS what it dropped, per-action SAVEPOINTs, and `events.automated()` as the loop guard (relocated from actor identity, because an `act_as` action runs as a real person — `automation.act_as` gates that on write). Node types are kernel contributions (`AutomationNodeSpec`: `ports_for(params)`, `params_schema`, `plan`/`plan_items`, `arity`) — `ai.classify` is the cross-module proof, an enumerated-answer classifier whose ANSWERS are its ports; **RADD-1064 adds a static `ports` tuple beside it** (one resolution seam, `spec.ports_at(params)`, static first) and serves it as `contributed_nodes[].ports` on `GET /automations/catalog` — the canvas has to draw a node's handles before the server ever sees the graph, and with nothing to read it fell back to the KIND's table and drew `ai.validate` with a gate's TRUE/FALSE, so every edge dragged off one named a port the engine never emits; an empty list is the positive statement "these depend on the params", which is why `ai.classify` still declares none. An edge's `port` is a plain string checked against the source node's real ports, never coerced through `NodePort` (that coercion made the five built-in names the only wireable ports, silently disabling every dynamic-port node). **RADD-918 — `NodeArity`**: each node declares whether it reads the SET (runs once) or each ITEM (runs per item; a ROUTER then PARTITIONS its input across its ports instead of routing all of it down one). There is no loop node — a back-edge would break the DAG, and in a dataflow model over sets "for each" is how a node reads its input, not control flow. `ACTION_ARITY_DEFAULT`/`BUILTIN_ARITY` are the defaults (reproducing the pre-arity `ITEM_ACTIONS` split exactly, so no data migration) and `ACTION_ARITY_CONFIGURABLE` names the five that offer a choice; `nodes.arity_of` is the single resolution seam (stored param → spec → built-in table) and `GET /automations/catalog` serves `node_arity` so the editor's default cannot disagree with the engine's. Per-item universal actions consume the item-action budget; `create_item` emits a **`created`** port carrying what it made; `send_email`/`notify_user` role targets force per-item (refused on write — set arity resolves no recipient, which is what "email each reporter" silently did); set-shaped tokens `{{items.count\|keys\|list}}` + `items[]` in the webhook body. **RADD-921 — the two things an author could not see**: `GET /automations/samples/events?event_type=` flattens RECENT REAL events into dotted paths with the values seen at each (`samples.py`, pure; list elements are the addressable path — `changes.field`, marked repeated — because that is what `_payload_path` descends to), plus the field names the diffs really name (which lead the "field changed" picker, since the full list includes custom fields the diff never names = a condition that can only ever be false) and one whole payload; SAMPLED, never synthesized, because a hand-written shape would be a second copy of what twenty modules' `emit` calls define and would drift silently, and an event type that has never fired says so rather than showing a plausible shape. And the dry run became per-NODE: `RunReport` keeps the item ids that left each port (capped `SAMPLE_ITEMS`, the count stays exact), `POST /automations/{id}/test` takes an OPTIONAL `item_id` + a `trigger_node_id` (a schedule trigger seeds from its own query) and returns `nodes[]` with per-port counts, sample KEYS and `taken` — a port that emitted zero items (a filter matching nothing) renders differently from one never emitted (a gate's untaken branch), on the canvas and in the panel, because they mean opposite things. `automations/router.py` also lost two spec-116 regressions (RADD-920): `run_rule` read `rule.trigger`, a column the graph replaced, and `list_runnable_rules` validated the `(automation, node_id)` pair as an automation — both 500s, on the editor's `/` quick-action path **Spec 119 — intake validation is a graph, run as a QUESTION.** A third sentinel trigger joins `manual`/`schedule`: **`validate`** (`AutomationTrigger.VALIDATE`, deliberately NOT in the event catalog, so the outbox consumer can never start a run from it). Its params are the binding — `targets` (a LIST of `{kind: form|issue_type|project, id}`, the relational shape; never three parallel scalars) and `mode` (`advisory`|`required`) — projected into **`automation_validations`** (PK `(automation_id, node_id, target_kind, target_id)`, `mode`; migration `d119valbind`) by `service._sync_validations`, the same rebuild-wholesale pattern `_sync_triggers` uses and for the same reason: intake has to answer "is anything validating this draft" in ONE query, on the request path, before Submit returns. `target_id` carries no FK (the target is polymorphic across three owners' tables) — a deleted target simply stops matching, the card-layout degradation rule. Applicability needs no trigger params beyond the targets: the GRAPH expresses it (`trigger → filter.slq → matched → checks`), and an unmatched packet reaches no check. New node type **`validation.fail`** — ACTION kind (its ports are an action's `out`, so a sixth `AutomationNodeKind` would buy a ports row, an arity row, a SPA kind and a canvas visual for a difference that lives entirely in what the node DOES), params `{message (required on write), field?}` where `field` is a `BuiltinItemField` name or `cf.<key>`, shape-checked only. It records a `Finding{node_id, field, message}` on `RunReport.findings` and passes its packet through so checks chain — and it stays QUIET on an empty packet, which is what makes the filter-gated form mean "only when this applies". Contributed nodes reach the same collection through `_NodeContext.add_finding(message, field)` — a method, not a shared list, because `Finding` is this module's vocabulary and the nodes contributing findings live elsewhere (`ai.validate`, spec 119's other half). `validation.py` holds the pure parsing (`parse_targets`/`parse_mode`/`strictest` — lenient on READ, strict on WRITE), resolution (`governing_graphs`: the three axes UNIONED, enabled-only, deduplicated per (automation, trigger node) so a graph matching on both project and form still runs once, keeping the strictest mode), and `run_graphs`, which walks every governing graph with **`apply=False`** — the existing dry-run switch, not a second walker, so what validation sees is what a run would decide. Verdict = `IntakeVerdict{mode (strictest wins, for DISPLAY), findings (capped `MAX_INTAKE_FINDINGS`, the overflow counted out loud), governed}`; the WIRE (`VerdictRead` + the 422 body) adds **`blocking`** = `verdict.blocks`, the same property the `commit: always` 409 is decided by, because no client can compute it — from `mode` it hides a bypass the server honours, from `passed` it offers one the server refuses; `governed=False` and a clean pass are different facts and are reported as such. **Each finding carries the MODE of the graph that produced it** and `verdict.blocks` = "a REQUIRED graph objected" — aggregating the mode instead refused a draft that satisfied every required graph and only tripped an advisory one, which the plain `POST /items` path (required bindings only) accepted. **Collecting is read off the TRIGGER** (`executor.walk`: `event == "validate"`), so a validate graph collects on the intake path, a manual run and a dry run alike, and no other graph collects at all — `validation.fail` stays quiet and `ctx.add_finding` is a no-op, which is what makes `ai.validate` the pure router the spec always claimed. **Two bounds, because the walk runs inside the create's transaction, holding the project's number lock:** `intake_validation_budget_seconds` (25s, one budget for the whole verdict — past it an AI check resolves per its own `on_unavailable`, default let-through), and a per-graph SAVEPOINT with a `SELECT 1` before the release (the executor swallows a node's exception by design, and a swallowed DBAPI error leaves an aborted transaction where RELEASE is illegal and ROLLBACK TO is not) → `ValidationUnavailable` → **503**, deliberately without a `findings` key. Un-serializing the lock is filed as RADD-1062. **Write-time**: an EVENT gate (`gate.event`/`gate.field_changed`/`gate.changed_by` — `EVENT_GATE_TYPES`) reachable from a validate trigger is refused, since a validation run has no event; scoped by `graph.is_reachable` to that trigger's own branch, and `gate.state_category` + contributed gate-kind nodes stay legal. `intake.py` is the request-path half: `validate_and_create` (savepoint → `items.create_item` → validate → keep or roll back), `validate_intake`, the **public `suppressed()` scope** (the seam a caller whose writes are NOT intake wraps its create in — the savepoint flow's own inner create, `jiraimport.apply` [deferred+feature-detected, `weak_depends`], `mailintake.intake._create` and `alertmanager.service.process`), and the `ValidationBlocked` error; `subscribers.py` registers the `ItemHook.CREATING` handler that enforces REQUIRED bindings for every other caller, with FOUR skips (suppressed / `events.automated` / `events.quiet` / machine intake via the scope) and its own `enable`/`disable` on the plugin's on_startup/on_shutdown, since `HookRegistry` has no removal seam and a hot-disabled plugin would otherwise keep enforcing. `items.create_item` runs the insert → labels → mentions → hook dispatch inside its OWN `begin_nested()` and rolls back to it before re-raising, so a refusal leaves nothing behind even for a caller that CATCHES the error and commits anyway (the importer's per-issue `except Exception` did exactly that, committing a half-created orphan with no `item.created` event) **Spec 120 — DATAFLOW: a node's outputs are named values the rest of the graph reads.** `Packet.vars` (node name -> {field -> value}) joins `facts` and `subjects` as the third thing a packet carries: on the PACKET, not the run, so a value produced on a branch that was not taken is not readable on one that was. Branch = copy (the outer mapping is rebuilt per write, inner ones only replaced), merge = union with the LATER writer winning — and `walk` now feeds fan-in in TOPOLOGICAL order, so "later" means the producer that ran second rather than whichever edge was stored first. A node gains an optional **`name`** (JSONB, no migration) — the left half of `{{triage.priority}}`, separate from `id` because edges are wired to the id and renaming would be a graph-wide rewire. STRICT on write (`[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,29}`, unique per graph, never a reserved token root — the reserved set is DERIVED from `templating.TOKENS`, not listed beside it), LENIENT on read (an illegal stored name makes the node unaddressable, never the automation unloadable). Outputs are DECLARED like ports (`AutomationNodeSpec.outputs`/`outputs_for`/`outputs_at`, ranked identically; `BUILTIN_OUTPUTS` covers `action.create_item` -> key/id/url, and `_apply_plan` now returns the ITEM it created because an id cannot say `TD-42`); the seam a contributed node writes through is **`ctx.set_output`**, shaped like spec 119's `add_finding`. Produced values ride on EVERY port a node emits; a PER-ITEM invocation publishes NOTHING, because N items make N answers and the bag has one slot — a token resolving to some other item's answer is a wrong write nobody notices. **Resolution is the SAME resolver** (`templating.Renderer`): the event/item/actor roots are untouched and `{{<node>.<field>}}` joins them; every item-bound action's named target (state, priority, assignee, team, cycle, release, label, custom-field value) now RENDERS before it resolves, through the by-name seam that was already there. Three ways an action declines with a reason: a VARIABLE token that found nothing (which OVERRIDES whatever else the planner concluded — otherwise a missing `triage` reports "no state '{{triage.state}}' in TD" and sends the reader to the workflow settings), a rendered value outside the vocabulary (spelled out, but ONLY from a list the planner already held — never a second query for the sake of a message), and a WORKFLOW GUARD refusal, which used to land in the generic `except Exception` and be reported by the dry run as an action that "would apply" and never could. `service._check_names`/`_check_token_references` refuse a dangling reference on WRITE, naming the token and the node holding it; `RuleTestResult` gained per-node `produced` (with the token that reads each value, shown even when the node is unnamed) and per-action `resolved`. Client: `lib/automation-outputs.ts` (the same ranking, the backwards edge walk the token picker offers from, the name rules), auto-named producers, a per-param token mode, and `GenerateFields`. RADD-1074: on a validate-triggered graph the finding-producing ports (`ai.validate` `fail`, `validation.fail` `out`) carry a "feedback to submitter" note and an unwired one is drawn as a deliberate CAP — a finding is the intake verdict, not a branch, so the empty handle read as an unfinished graph. `docs/specs/120-automation-dataflow.md` **Review follow-ups:** a workflow-guard / field-registry refusal is caught OUTSIDE the invocation's savepoint — catching it inside let `__aexit__` COMMIT the row change `items.update_item` makes before `check_transition` runs, so the item landed in the refused state with no `item.updated` event (no history, no notification, no reindex) while the report called it a skip. `FieldValidationError` and a refused `ItemCreate` construction join it as recorded skips; `add_label` stays create-on-demand by decision. `Renderer.line` collapses whitespace in every param that NAMES a target or becomes a header (an `EmailMessage` header with a newline raises inside the transport, so a rendered subject sent nothing at all). `AutomationNodeSpec.check(params)` is a new kernel seam for a node's own write-time refusals, and `_check_node_schema` also honours top-level `maxLength`/`maxItems`. | projects, auth, workflow, labels, cycles, releases, items, comments, teams, events, fields (weak: mailintake, notify, leave [RADD-1044 — `assign_round_robin`'s away check, itself deferred+feature-detected inside `round_robin.py`]) | `automation.created`, `automation.updated`, `automation.deleted` (payload: name, `triggers` [plural since spec 116 — a graph may fire from several entry points], enabled), `automation.scheduled` (spec 69 — the scheduler's synthetic tick, never subscribable) | `on_startup`/`on_shutdown` (engine task + spec-69 scheduler loop); consumer-offset pattern; the item events it applies are marked via `actor_id` (the loop guard) **RADD-1248 — page comments, discussion and replies reach automations.** Comment events are item-scoped, and the engine dropped one whose item did not resolve as "vanished" — which is what a page comment (`item: None` by design) looked like, so no page comment ever triggered anything. `engine._parent_is_not_an_item` sends a comment whose `entity_type` names a non-item parent down spec 58's itemless path (event gates, universal actions) instead. Two named gates, in the spec-116 style: `gate.comment` (thread root/reply × public/internal, read from the comment event's own `parent_comment_id`/`visibility`) and `gate.page_space` (slugs, read from a page event's `page_space` ref or a page comment's `page.space`). Tokens `{{page.title|path|space|url}}` and `{{comment.excerpt|visibility|parent_id}}` resolve from the same refs on either surface. Tests `tests/test_automation_page_comments.py` (end to end: a page discussion comment AND a reply in one space create an item in another project with the rendered title; an item comment does not). **RADD-1265 — the audit fixes.** `send_email` asks the mail module whether a sender exists (`email_action.outbound_available` → `mailintake.transport.outbound_configured`) instead of reading the env `smtp_host`, which had skipped every automation email on a rows-only instance since RADD-983. The spec-58 CONDITION TREE is deleted (`gate.event`, `conditions.matches`, the subject grammar, the SPA's `ConditionsBuilder`); `gate.payload` ("Event value is": dotted path + `ConditionOperator` + value, the sample-event paths as suggestions) is the one open-ended gate and the only way to narrow a non-item event. The schedule trigger's `query` param is gone — a `search.slq` node after the trigger is the one way to select a scheduled run's items — and `{{matched_count}}` with it (`{{items.count}}` is the same number). Migration `d1265autolegacy` rewrites stored graphs (query → search node; `all`-of-leaves trees → chains of named gates) and DISABLES + suffixes "[needs review]" anything it cannot express. `{{item.*}}` grew url/state/state_category/priority/assignee/reporter/project/type/labels, loaded once per action node by `planning.load_item_facts` (four batched queries). SPA: canvas nodes carry the palette label (`nodeTitle`), the palette is in a fixed order (Items first, Admin last), a new automation opens on a placed, selected `item.updated` trigger. Tests `tests/test_automation_gates.py`. **RADD-1266 — run history.** `automation_runs` (migration `d1266autoruns`): one row per APPLYING walk, written by `engine.run_graph` in the run's own transaction, holding the dry run's `RuleTestResult` as JSONB (one builder, `engine._result_of`, serves both), plus status (`RunStatus`: applied | nothing_to_do | refused | failed — a refusal outranks the actions that did apply), source (`RunSource`: event | schedule | manual), the event id, the trigger node, the actor, the item keys and the action counts. A dry run (validation walks included) writes nothing. A walk that RAISES is recorded as failed and emits `automation.run_failed` (audited, never a trigger) instead of escaping into the consumer loop. `PlannedAction.refused` marks a guard/registry refusal. `runs.py` owns record/list/get/last_runs/sweep; the sweep rides the scheduler tick under `automation_run_retention_days` (30; 0 = keep). `GET /automations/{id}/runs[?before=]` and `/runs/{run_id}`; `RuleRead.last_run_at/last_run_status`. SPA: `RunsPanel` behind a Runs tab beside the dry run, rendering through the shared `RunResultView` (Applied/Skipped/Refused chips) and labelling the canvas from the selected run; the list shows the newest run's chip. Tests `tests/test_automation_runs.py`; proof `web/scripts/automations-runs-proof.mjs`. **RADD-1267 — coverage.** Twelve more item actions, every one an `ItemUpdate` field or a service verb the module already owned: `set_parent` (key or none), `set_type` (name), `set_reporter`, `set_dates` (ISO or `today+3d`, none clears, empty leaves alone), `set_estimate`, `set_flag`, `set_visibility`, `link_item` (key + link-type key → `items.add_item_link`), `archive_item` (→ `items.set_archived`), `add_watcher` (role or email → `notify.watch`), `add_participant` (feature-detected `participants`, now in `weak_depends`), `move_to_project` (→ `items.bulk.bulk_move_items`, a skip reason raised as a failure). New `PlanKind`s LINK/ARCHIVE/WATCH/PARTICIPANT/MOVE. `gate.project` ("Project is": keys, negate) reads the kernel's `project` ref or the item ref's project — the one way to narrow a release/form/cycle/SLA event by project. Nine events switched on as triggers (`user.created/updated`, `worklog.estimate_changed`, `access.granted/revoked`, `page_space.public_access_changed`, `sla_policy.*`; snapshot 76 → 85). **Page actions live in `pages/automation.py`** (`page.comment`, `page.move`; `subject="page"`, the milestone pattern) — page bodies stay off limits because spec 122's write guard owns them. Tests `tests/test_automation_coverage.py`; proof `web/scripts/automations-coverage-proof.mjs`. **RADD-1268 — versions (delivers RADD-1111).** `automations.version` is the current pointer; `automation_versions` (migration `d1268autover`, v1 backfilled from live rows) holds EVERY version including the current one — name, nodes, edges, orientation, author, `note`, `restored_from`. `versions.py` owns write/list/get; `create_rule` writes v1, `update_rule` writes v(n+1) only when `VERSIONED_FIELDS` changed (a toggle of `enabled` or a reorder writes none), `restore_version` re-validates the old graph, writes a NEW version copying it (history never rewrites), rebuilds the trigger/validation bindings and emits `automation.updated` with `version` in its diff (`version` joined `RULE_FIELDS`). `GET /automations/{id}/versions`, `/versions/{n}`, `POST /versions/{n}/restore`; `RuleCreate/RuleUpdate.note`, `RuleRead.version`. SPA: a Versions tab (`VersionsPanel`: list with the current one marked, a read-only preview canvas per selected version, Restore behind `useConfirm`, the editor reloads onto the restored graph) and a "Why this change" note beside Save. Tests `tests/test_automation_versions.py`; proof `web/scripts/automations-versions-proof.mjs`. |
| `notify` | Notifications + watchers (spec 26): an outbox consumer fanning item/comment/SLA/approval/participant/page events into per-user `notifications` rows, a per-event mailer, and an email digest. **Per-user policy is `notification_rules` (spec 118)** — one row per (user, scope, `scope_id`) carrying a SPARSE `channels` map `{kind: off\|inbox\|email\|both}` (migration `d118notifrules`; two PARTIAL unique indexes, because relationship rows carry `scope_id IS NULL` and Postgres counts NULLs as DISTINCT — one plain UNIQUE would let a person hold two `own` rules and leave the resolver answering with whichever it indexed last). `notification_prefs` keeps `email_digest` ALONE. **Six scopes** (`RuleScope`): relationships `own`\|`participating`\|`teams` (`scope_id` NULL — the matrix's three columns) and subscriptions `project`\|`space`\|`team`. Sparse is load-bearing: subscribing to a project must not overwrite what you said about your own work. **Resolution is `rules.py`, PURE + unit-tested** (`test_notify_rules.py`): most-specific-wins over own > participating > team-subscription > my-teams > project > space, taking the first rule with an OPINION about the kind, then falling back to the FIRST APPLICABLE scope's default. An UNSUBSCRIBED project is not a scope with an empty opinion — it is not in the order at all, or its `off` default would sit above `participating` and watching an issue would stop working everywhere nobody had subscribed. **Personal kinds** (`assigned`/`mentioned`/`participant_added`/`approval`/`automation`) resolve through `own` ALONE — the event chose the recipient, and an approver is frequently neither assignee nor watcher, so resolving `approval` by relationship would have handed them `off` and ended approvals in silence. `DEFAULT_MATRIX` reproduces RADD-686's CHANNELS exactly (pinned kind-by-kind against `DEFAULT_EMAIL_TYPES` rather than a copied list): for anyone the old fan-out reached, zero rows = the old behaviour. `own` and `participating` are identical on purpose (RADD-686 had no relation, so any difference would be a behaviour change nobody asked for); `teams` starts OFF (defaulting it on subscribes every team member to their team's whole traffic on deploy day). A kind missing from the vocabulary degrades (`_default_for`) instead of raising inside the consumer's SAVEPOINT, and a test asserts `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` covers `set(NotificationType)` in BOTH directions. **Vocabulary is `kinds.py`** — one ordered `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` constant (wire value, label, description, personal), 13 kinds, SERVED by the preferences endpoint, because the SPA's own `Record<NotificationType,string>` meant a kind added on the server had no row in the UI and nothing failed. Three kinds are spec 118's and are `off` in every relationship scope: `created` (an issue FILED reached only its assignee), `updated` (a field moved that no semantic type covers, carrying the field names — a reprioritised/relabelled/re-estimated issue was silent to everyone watching it), `page_created` (notified nobody). **`GET/PUT /notifications/preferences`** serves `{kinds, scopes, defaults, rules, email_digest}` — `defaults` carries EVERY scope, because a subscription's unset cell needs one too — and the PUT is a FULL replace on `rules` (removing a subscription IS leaving its row out), bounded `max_length=200`, response read back off the stored rows. **Subscription targets are gated** (`targets.py`): a `scope_id` is a uuid the client picks, and the preferences READ turns it into a NAME, so an unchecked one is a lookup service for every project/space/team name on the instance. The gate is the seam each family's PICKER uses — `authz.visible_projects`, `team.read`, `pages.access.readable_spaces` — applied on BOTH sides (the write drops a target the actor may not name, the read refuses to label one, because a stored row outlives the access that created it); an unlabelled row renders `(unavailable)`. **Consumer** (cursor `notify.consumer`, dispatcher mirrors webhooks), `_HANDLED` = `item.created/.updated` + `comment.created` + the SLA timer events (`sla.breached` → `sla_breach`, spec-69 `sla.due_soon` → `sla_due_soon`, via `plan_sla_breached(type_=…)`, detail + `remaining_seconds`) + the spec-71 approval events (wire strings, approvals loads later: `approval.requested` → `APPROVAL` to each payload-listed eligible approver [approvers deliberately NOT auto-watched]; `approval.approved`/`.declined` → the requester) + the spec-72 **`item.participant_added`** (wire string too — participants loads later and is disableable; **RADD-978**: `plan_participant_added` addresses the payload's `user` ref ONLY, so a TEAM add plans nothing personal [team rows resolve live at fan-out and are ambient by design], actor-excluded, no `watch` entry because `add_participant` already auto-watched on the write path; the recipient passes `_allowed` through the Baseline's `item.read@participant`, i.e. through the very row being announced) + `page.created`/`page.updated` (spec 118, wire strings). **First start bootstraps watch-only over the whole backlog** (watcher graph without notification spam; needs `events.offset_exists`), and each event runs in a SAVEPOINT so a bad one is logged and skipped. Planning is PURE (`planner.py`, tested in `tests/test_notify.py`): actor never self-notified, one notification per user per event (assigned/mentioned beat state_changed/commented, which beat the generic `updated`); **reporter auto-watch (spec 62)**; `Plan.follow` is the ONE way a watch is planned and it refuses `types.SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID` (RADD-996 — a wire-constant copy of `automations.types.SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID` pinned equal in `test_notify.py`; mail intake creates items AS that actor, so auto-watching the creator had put `automation@radd.system` on every emailed-in ticket and fanned `commented` rows to a robot). **Fan-out is four sets, not one list:** `planner.Audience` splits the ambient audience into `own` (assignee/reporter), `participating` (watchers ∪ direct participants ∪ participant-team members ∪ mention targets), `in_my_teams` and `subscribers`, and STAMPS each recipient's relation on the `PlannedNotification` — the fan-out that built the sets is the only place that still knows which one someone came out of. `consumer.recipient_ids(item_id)` (the spec-72 seam name, re-exported from `audience.py`) is the `participating` member: watchers ∪ CURRENT members of the item's PARTICIPANT teams (`participants.team_recipient_ids`, a deferred feature-detected import), live at delivery time. `audience.item_audience` costs three queries beyond the old flat version: `service.subscriber_ids` (ONE indexed read over `ix_notification_rules_target`, matching project + team at once — a row's channels are deliberately NOT consulted in SQL, or precedence would have a second copy), the my-teams narrowing (from the RULES side first: a `teams` row has no `scope_id` to look up, and the other direction is a query per candidate for an answer almost always empty), and the team-member expansion behind it. **`own` is a deliberate WIDENING** — assignee and reporter are in the ambient audience whether or not they watch, so Unwatch no longer silences an assignee (it removes the participating relation only; the `own` column is the off switch) and on an instance built by a silent IMPORT, which writes no auto-watch rows, assignees/reporters start receiving ambient notifications and the `commented` mail that goes with them. Pinned in `test_notify_scoped_fanout.py`. **Every recipient still passes `_allowed`**: active + `item.read` on the project + the RADD-817 relation-scoped row check + the internal-comment filter (`comment.read_internal`, team narrowing via `teams.user_team_ids`) — participation and subscription never widen visibility. `_apply` resolves the CHANNEL BEFORE `_allowed`: same rows either way, but `_allowed` is a permission resolution + relation row check per recipient and a project subscription multiplies the candidate set, so an `off` verdict should cost a dict lookup. Roughly one permission resolution per SURVIVING recipient per event is the loop's scaling shape. **`service.create_notification` is the ONE write seam** (RADD-971): it refuses the system actor, resolves the verdict (or takes the caller's prefetched `RuleSet` — `rules_by_user` returns an entry for EVERY id asked about, because a partial dict read with `.get(id, …)` is easy to get backwards), returns `None` when the verdict is `off` (the mute moved out of the row's EXISTENCE and onto its columns, which is what makes email-with-no-inbox reachable), stamps **`notifications.inbox`/`.email`** (migration `d118notifchan`; `ix_notifications_user_read` gains `inbox`; backfill `inbox=true, email=false` — the inbox half is exact, the email half unrecoverable because the relation was never recorded), and emits `notification.created` for realtime. A caller that simply picked a recipient (automations' `notify_user`) passes no relation and gets own-scope semantics, which is what addressing someone directly means. Since RADD-972 that caller also writes `item_key`/`item_title` (via `items.item_ref`) when the rule fired on an item, so the automation line links `[KEY] title` like every other item-scoped type; an itemless rule keeps `lines.entry`'s linkless degradation. `item_watchers` (auto-watch creator/assignee/commenter; manual `PUT/DELETE /items/{id}/watch` emits `item.watched/.unwatched`, `GET /items/{id}/watchers`). `GET /notifications` (envelope with `unread_count`), `POST /notifications/read`/`read-all` — personal, no extra RBAC; all three filter `inbox IS TRUE`, and **both mark-read paths are scoped to inbox rows** because `mailer._pending` skips READ rows and an email-only row can never be opened, so an inbox sweep would otherwise silently cancel a send the person asked for. **Two email loops, one decision.** The per-event **`mailer.py`** (loop `notify-mailer`, `RADD_NOTIFY_MAIL_POLL_INTERVAL` 5s / `RADD_NOTIFY_MAIL_BATCH` 100, gated on `run_workers`) reads NOTIFICATION ROWS, not events (RADD-968 — the row already passed planner precedence, the verdict and `_allowed`; re-planning would be a second copy of the policy): `email IS TRUE` ∧ `emailed_at IS NULL` ∧ unread ∧ within `notify_email_max_age_hours` ∧ not backed off. `email IS TRUE` is a COLUMN because the decision was made at the write by code that still knew the relation — RADD-686's per-recipient Python filter could not be SQL while the only thing a row remembered was its type, which is also why the mailer could never honour a scoped preference. Comment rows render via `lines` + `mailrender.comment_reply` with the FULL body (`comments.comment_body` off `event_id` → the `comment.created` event's `entity_id`; **the event-type check is load-bearing** — every event carries an `entity_id` and for the item family it is the ITEM, so without it the transport got an item uuid as a comment id and the tick died on an FK violation, RADD-978); every other type via `mailrender.notice`. Delivery is `mailintake.service.send_item_mail` through a DEFERRED feature-detected import (weak_depends; absent ⇒ env `radd.smtp`, no threading), so a notification email threads on the item and a Reply lands back on the issue. `service.mailable_user` gates both loops: inactive ∨ address-less ∨ `UserSource.SERVICE` ∨ the system actor ⇒ skip AND STAMP, because the answer is a property of the ACCOUNT and a retry can only reach it more slowly (inbox rows for service accounts are left alone; the system actor is refused a row at all). **RADD-997 — per-row send backoff**: `notifications.email_attempts`/`email_next_try` (migration `d997mailretry`, no backfill — NULL means now) + `(email_next_try IS NULL OR email_next_try <= now)` in BOTH loops' selection (in SQL, so failing rows cannot fill the batch and starve healthy ones), `retry.py` holds the ladder (1m/5m/30m, then STAMP `emailed_at` — the terminal give-up has to be the stamp, or the digest picks up an address that has refused four times), `retry.record_failure` shared so the two loops cannot drift, and `mail.failed` emitted on the FIRST failure and the TERMINAL one only via `transport.send_item_mail(emit_failure=…)`. The **`emailer.py`** digest (every `RADD_NOTIFY_EMAIL_INTERVAL`) is the slower half: `inbox IS TRUE` ∧ unstamped, grouped per user, skipping `digest_disabled_users`, sent through `mailer.send_plain` → `mailintake.send_plain_mail` (RADD-983 — it used to dial `radd.smtp` off `settings.*` and sent nothing on an instance configured through Settings → Email). `lines.py` holds the shared vocabulary (headline/actor_name/entry + `MAIL_SUBJECT_TEMPLATE`/`MAIL_REASON_TEMPLATE`) so the two channels cannot describe the same type differently — they did, four sentences for nine types, until RADD-967. **Every user-addressed email says how to stop it (RADD-985):** both loops render with `mailer.preferences_url()` (`mailrender.preferences_url(app_base_url)` → `/settings/notifications`, the spec-118 tab — NOT the profile page the issue was filed against) and send with `mailrender.unsubscribe_headers` through the transport's new `headers=` parameter (`send_item_mail` merges them UNDER the thread's own Reply-To/In-Reply-To/References; `send_plain_mail` passes them as given; the env-relay fallback carries them too). Contact-facing mail (ack, requester replies, CSAT) carries neither, by test in both directions. **Known issue (documented, not fixed):** an email-only row created while `can_send` is false for longer than `notify_email_max_age_hours` is never sent, never stamped and never digested (the digest filters `inbox IS TRUE`, so it cannot be the safety net for a row that was never in an inbox) — see `docs/specs/118-*.md`. **The wiki fans out here too.** `notify/pageevents.py` is its half of the consumer on the same three seams; `pages/watchers.notify_watchers` is DELETED (RADD-719's synchronous in-request fan-out knew about page watchers only, applied NO read gate, and `page.created` was never wired to it). Gate = `pages.refs.readable_page_ids_for_users` (space role + every restriction on the ANCESTOR PATH, RADD-948 — the wiki's answer to give, not notify's to reimplement); internal page comments gated on `comment.read_internal` at SPACE scope (the scope the WRITE gate used; the page-comment READ path still resolves it globally, a pre-RADD-791 leftover). Page events carry `page` + `page_space` SUBJECTS (RADD-923, `pages/refs.py` + `entity_refs`), so the payload has the slugs a notification links with and the space id a subscription matches on — notify may import neither `pages` (load order) nor its models (the spine rule). **RADD-1056 — page comments notified NOBODY, ever**: `comments` has been polymorphic since RADD-717 and `_handle_comment_created` did not look at `entity_type`, so every wiki comment raised KeyError inside the SAVEPOINT and was skipped, @-mentions included; it survived because `page_updated` worked, so the wiki's notifications looked alive. **Files:** `types.py`, `models.py`, `kinds.py` (vocabulary), `rules.py` (pure resolver), `planner.py` (pure planning + `Audience`), `audience.py` (who could hear it + `Subject`/`own` derivation + `actor_name_of`), `mentions.py` (candidates → real active users, needed by the item AND page comment paths), `consumer.py`, `pageevents.py` (the wiki half), `service.py`, `prefs.py` + `targets.py` + `schemas.py` + `router.py` (the preferences payload, its gate, the API), `mailer.py`, `emailer.py`, `lines.py`, `retry.py`, `dispatcher.py`. **UI:** `/settings/notifications` (own tab under Account; the Profile section is a link) — `routes/settings/notifications.tsx` + `components/settings/notifications/` (`NotificationMatrix`, `ChannelCell`, `SubscriptionList`, pure `matrix.ts`), reading `queries/notifications.ts::notificationPrefsQuery`. The cell is a five-choice MENU, not two checkboxes: two boxes can say off/inbox/email/both but cannot say UNSET, and a control that renders inheritance as "unchecked" lies about the two cases a preference exists to distinguish. A subscription's unset cell shows the SUBSCRIPTION scope's default (`off`) — the "Mine" value would describe a notification that never arrives, since for an item you have no relation to the subscription is the only applicable scope — and a new subscription is seeded with exactly one kind: `created` for a project or team, `page_created` for a space (`created` is planned from ITEM events, which carry no space). **Tests** (counts drift — run the suite): `test_notify_rules.py`, `test_notify_scoped_fanout.py`, `test_notify.py` (incl. the migration backfill replayed through alembic's operations proxy inside the test transaction), `test_notify_mailer.py`, `test_page_comments.py`, `test_event_subjects.py`. Browser proof `web/scripts/notification-matrix-proof.mjs` (both themes) — it caught the matrix's columns wobbling 10px because a DISABLED cell renders one mark and a live one renders two. `docs/specs/118-*.md`. **History, superseded (the vocabulary still appears in old commits and issues):** RADD-686 stored a preference as two per-TYPE JSONB lists on `notification_prefs` — `muted_types` (inbox = NOT muted) and `email_types` (migration `d686emailtypes`) — with email REQUIRING inbox by construction, `service.set_prefs` normalising muted entries out of `email_types`, the mailer narrowing each candidate per RECIPIENT in Python via `email_types_by_user`, and a two-checkbox `NotificationPrefsPanel.tsx` on /settings/profile.
