| `sso` | **Single sign-on — a PROVIDER REGISTRY since spec 110** (was env-single-provider OIDC, spec 40). Tables: **`sso_providers`** (`kind` `google`\|`oidc`\|`github` — every kind runs the SAME code+PKCE engine; a kind supplies its ENDPOINTS and its PROFILE STRATEGY via `KIND_DEFAULTS` [`KindDefaults`: `discovery` + `profile`], Google's issuer pinned, GitHub (spec 121, RADD-1145) fully pinned with NO discovery and NO `id_token` — `idp.py` owns the wire: `metadata()` synthesizes the endpoint document for a pinned kind, `profile()` verifies an `id_token` for OIDC kinds or calls GitHub's `/user` + `/user/emails` (subject = numeric `id`, email = the primary+verified entry → `email_verified`) and returns the same claim-shaped dict either way, so `provision()` is kind-blind; the module-level `idp.transport` is the MockTransport seam; name = the login button's label, enabled, position, issuer, client_id/`client_secret` [stored as-is, replayed on token exchange — the webhook-secret precedent; reads expose `has_client_secret`], scopes, `auto_provision`, **`allowed_signup_domains` JSONB**, `require_verified_email`, `group_claim`, `admin_groups`, `source`) + **`user_identities`** (provider FK CASCADE, user FK CASCADE, `subject`, unique (provider, subject); joins auth's `_MERGE_REPOINT` so federated logins follow a merged person). Env `RADD_OIDC_*` (+ new `RADD_OIDC_SIGNUP_DOMAINS`) is **SEED-ONLY** — `seed_from_env` runs only on an empty table (the `ai/registry.py` rule); empty list + auto-provision seeds the `"*"` wildcard, preserving spec 40 behavior. **Identity resolution:** FIRST login matches an existing account by **verified** email (this is what lands a Google login on the person's AD account — it keeps its `source`, role and history), then pins to the IdP's immutable `sub`; EVERY later login matches on subject alone, so a mailbox rename can't fork the account and a recycled address can't inherit a leaver's. `require_verified_email` (default ON) is the hinge — linking by email is exactly what an unverified address would exploit. **Signup policy** gates CREATION only (an existing account signs in from any domain): `auto_provision` off ⇒ no; `"*"` ⇒ yes; else the email domain must be listed; **EMPTY list = no signups**. Domains normalized on write (`@Radd-HQ.com`/`a@HJarrar.com` → bare lowercase). **Role sync only when `admin_groups` is set** — spec 40 wrote `instance_role` unconditionally, so a Google login (no group claim) DEMOTED the AD admin it linked to on every sign-in. API: unauthenticated `GET /auth/sso/providers` (login buttons: id/name/kind only), `GET /auth/oidc/login?provider_id=` (no id ⇒ the only configured provider), `GET /auth/oidc/callback` (ONE callback for every provider — the flow cookie carries which; **refusals REDIRECT to `/login?sso_error=…`**, since this leg runs in an address bar where a 403 JSON body would be the page), `/sso/providers` CRUD + `/sso/kinds` + `/sso/providers/{id}/test` (issuer discovery probe; failures as data) — instance-admin. Per-provider discovery/JWKS caches, invalidated on write. The `sso` capability reads a process-local snapshot (a `CapabilitySpec` check is sync). SPA: **Settings → Sign-in** + per-provider login buttons (Google's four-colour G). `docs/specs/110-*.md`. **RADD-777 — a provider's DEFAULT ROLE:** `sso_providers.default_role_id` (FK roles, ON DELETE SET NULL — a tidied role list must never break the login path; migration `cc302ccad603`) is granted in `provision()` from the ONE branch that constructs a new `User`, and nowhere else: not on the `linked` branch (an existing account gaining another door) and not on a returning login. It is a `global_role_grants` ROW rather than a field, which is what makes "never undo an admin's later change" a property of the data — nothing reconciles grants, so revoking or rescoping it is simply never re-read. Re-applying it per login would be spec 40's demotion bug in a new costume, which is why `tests/test_sso_providers.py` asserts the revoke-then-sign-in-again case explicitly. **RADD-780/781 corrected its SHAPE**: one global role could only say "everyone gets Member everywhere", so `default_role_id` is replaced by **`sso_provider_default_grants`** (provider, role, `project_id` NULL = global — the same pair `global_role_grants` carries, because these rows ARE the template copied into it) and **`sso_provider_default_teams`** (migration `e780ssotmpl` backfills the old column into a global row before dropping it). Both write full-replacement (the views/sharing idiom). A stale template never breaks a sign-in: every failure logs and is skipped, which for ROLES turns out to be unreachable (the FK refuses an unknown role and CASCADE removes a deleted one) but for TEAMS is real — a team linked to an AD group after the template was written has directory-owned membership and answers 409. Settings → Sign-in renders the same grant list the Users and Teams panels use; directory teams are not offered. **RADD-782 put a RULE between them**: `sso_provisioning_rules` (provider, name, position, `domains` JSONB) owns the grant/team rows (`rule_id`, CASCADE; migration `f782ssorules` gives every existing template one unnamed catch-all rule, so upgrade is behaviour-free). One provider serves several populations — `@acme.example` and `@partner.example` arrive through the same Google button. **EMPTY domains = matches everyone, and EVERY matching rule applies** (not first-match): grants are additive rows, so a union is the only composition that cannot surprise, and it is what makes a catch-all useful instead of forcing every rule to restate the common part — the spec-102 storage chain picks ONE host because a file lands in one place, which is the opposite problem. Matching is exact on the lowercased domain (the `allowed_signup_domains` normalization), never a regex or a subdomain wildcard: both are ways to write a rule matching more than its author believed, and this decides what a stranger gets on arrival. | events, projects, auth | `sso.login`, `sso.identity_linked` | — |
| `ldap` | **Spec 86: global scope — the workspace entity is gone; this module has no `workspace_id`, no singleton resolver.** LDAP/AD directory sign-in (spec 42) — **NO tables**. `POST /auth/ldap/login {username, password}`: **direct bind** as `<username>@<RADD_LDAP_USER_DOMAIN>` over `RADD_LDAP_URL` (`ldaps://…`) — the user's own credentials authenticate the connection, **no stored bind account** (the pipe-status pattern); blocking `ldap3` round-trip runs via `asyncio.to_thread`. On that connection: own entry read (`RADD_LDAP_EMAIL_ATTRIBUTE`/`_NAME_ATTRIBUTE`, fallbacks = UPN/username) + `RADD_LDAP_ADMIN_GROUPS` (CNs) membership via AD's **transitive matching rule** so NESTED groups count; every filter value `escape_filter_chars`-escaped, usernames gated on `[a-zA-Z0-9._-]{1,64}` pre-wire. Then exactly the spec-40 path: find-or-create SSO-only user (`password_hash NULL`), `RADD_LDAP_AUTO_PROVISION`, default-workspace role sync each login via `auth.service.sync_workspace_membership`, `radd_session` cookie. Base DN = `RADD_LDAP_BASE_DN` or derived from the domain. Login page offers an Email/Directory toggle via `login-options.ldap_enabled`. Empty `RADD_LDAP_URL` = dormant (403). **Optional service-account enumeration (spec 49)**: `search_directory_users` binds with `RADD_LDAP_BIND_DN`/`_BIND_PASSWORD` and pages the directory (`RADD_LDAP_USER_SEARCH_BASE`/`_USER_FILTER`) to bulk-provision users via `scripts/import_ad_users.py` (created member, matched by email so direct-bind login links up + syncs role); interactive login itself never stores a bind account. **Spec 84 (AD depth):** team↔group links reconciled three ways — on LDAP login (the direct-bind connection also probes each linked team's DN transitively; `groupsync.sync_login_membership`), on demand (`POST /teams/{id}/directory-sync`, team.manage, 409 sans bind account), and by the **`ldap.groupsync` PeriodicLoop** (`RADD_LDAP_GROUP_SYNC_SECONDS`, default 3600; gated `run_workers` AND `bind_account_enabled`; registered via module `on_startup/on_shutdown`). The planner (`ldap/groupsync.plan_reconcile`) is PURE: directory emails/UPNs × current rows × existing users → add/remove sets — unknown directory members are NEVER auto-provisioned by a reconcile. Group ops live in `ldap/groups.py` (service-account bind): paged `(&(objectClass=group)(cn=*q*))` search (member_count = DIRECT `member` length, display only) + transitive member resolution `(&(objectCategory=person)(objectClass=user)(memberOf:1.2.840.113556.1.4.1941:=<dn>))`. Instance-admin endpoints (all 409 without a bind account): `GET /ldap/groups?q=`, `POST /ldap/groups/import {workspace_id, group_dns, provision_members}` (create-or-link team = CN, seat transitive members, optionally provision SSO-only `source=ldap` users w/ a workspace member seat), `GET /ldap/directory-users?q=` (spec-49 enumeration narrowed over cn/sAMAccountName/mail), `POST /ldap/directory-users/import {emails}`. `users.source` set `ldap` at provision; pre-84 `unknown` rows claimed on login. **Spec 85 (Directory settings + automatic user sync):** the module's FIRST table — `directory_sync_state` (kind PK `user_sync`\|`group_sync`, `last_run_at`, JSONB `last_result`; migration `12e3da9fab36`), written by both periodic loops + the on-demand pass and served by `GET /ldap/sync-status` (instance admin, readable without a bind account). ALL directory search bases now resolve through the settings cascade: the async wrappers (`groups.search_groups/search_group_members` take a session; `service.resolved_user_base/resolved_group_base`) resolve the instance-scope keys (`ldap_user_sync_base`/`ldap_group_search_base`, empty → `base_dn` at USE time) and pass the base as a plain param into the sync ldap3 fns (`search_directory_users(q, base)` keeps a None default for the session-less spec-49 script). Group listing uses the GROUP base; transitive member resolution uses the USER base (member entries are user objects). **`ldap-usersync` PeriodicLoop** (`RADD_LDAP_USER_SYNC_SECONDS` default 3600, `sleep_first`, start-gated `run_workers` AND `bind_account_enabled`; the `ldap_user_sync_enabled` cascade value re-resolved PER TICK): search the users base → provision unknown users (spec-84 import path: SSO-only `source=ldap` + default-workspace member seat), update changed display names on ldap-source users (matching is BY EMAIL — the enumeration's dedupe key — so email changes read as leaver+joiner, not renames), and only when `ldap_user_sync_deactivate_missing` resolves true deactivate ldap-source actives missing from the directory via `auth.service.deactivate_user` (session-revoking; local/oidc never touched). `POST /ldap/sync/users` = the same pass on demand (instance admin, 409 sans bind account). Settings → Directory (`/settings/directory`) is the SPA surface (status card, user-sync card w/ Sync now, group table w/ import/link/unlink); the Users page keeps account admin only. **Spec 88 — import conflicts:** `ldap/userimport.py` — `plan_user_import` (PURE) classifies each selected directory user against the existing roster as `new`/`linked`/`conflict` with the reason for each match (`ImportMatchKind` email|username|name; Radd has no username column, so a sAMAccountName is compared against an email's LOCAL PART). `POST /ldap/directory-users/import/preview` is the dry run; the import endpoint now takes a per-entry `ImportResolution` — **overwrite** (one account adopts AD's email/name/source, `id` PRESERVED so its issues/comments/worklogs follow; 409 rather than stealing an email another account holds), **merge** (fold the look-alike into the AD-identified account via `auth.merge_users`, then apply AD's values), **create** (keep both), **skip**. Nothing auto-resolves — a name match is a heuristic and rewriting an email is not inferable. An entry with no resolution keeps the pre-88 create-or-link behavior. `adopt_directory_identity` leaves the password hash alone (no mid-migration lockout) but sets `source=ldap`, so the account becomes directory-governed (rename + deactivate-missing apply). **Spec 87 stale-group guard:** an empty transitive member search is ambiguous (genuinely empty vs. group renamed/deleted), and believing the second would revoke everything the team grants — `reconcile_team` confirms the DN still resolves before acting, raising `StaleDirectoryGroup` (409 on the on-demand endpoint, a warning + `directory_missing_since` flag in the periodic loop) and leaving the roster untouched; `sync_login_membership` holds removals while that flag is set, so the login path cannot drain the team one sign-in at a time. A broken link NEVER unlocks the team (user direction) — read-only stands while the link exists; unlinking is the deliberate way out, surfaced as an amber `AD group missing` badge + a one-click **Unlink and edit here** banner. That rests on `get_group` answering `None` ONLY for a genuine not-found and raising **`DirectoryUnreachable`** when the directory can't be asked at all (incl. the eager `auto_bind` in `service_connection`, previously outside the try) — an outage must never be reported as a missing group. | events, projects, auth, settings, teams | `ldap.login` | — | **Spec 100 — skipping disabled directory accounts.** `ldap_exclude_disabled` is a cascade-resolved instance SettingKey (Settings → Directory → User sync, alongside the sync toggles) that `base_user_filter` COMPOSES onto `ldap_user_filter` — `(!(userAccountControl:1.2.840.113556.1.4.803:=2))` — rather than baking it in, and skips itself when a deploy already hand-wrote the clause. ON by default: the shipped filter had NO account-status check, and on a real directory most entries are leavers (a live AD held roughly twice as many disabled accounts as active), so a default deploy filled Radd with dead users. `search_directory_users(q, base, exclude_disabled)` + `resolved_exclude_disabled(session)`; every caller (the import picker, the duplicate preview, the usersync loop) passes the resolved value. Verified live: the roster tripled and returned as the toggle flipped, no restart. NOTE the filter itself is still env-only; only the disabled-account decision is in the UI.
| `pages` | **RADD-708 wave (page extensions + wiki depth):** **RADD-709** — pages gain EXTENSIONS: a fenced ```radd:<name>``` block in the markdown body (still markdown to FTS, the embedder, the public surface, the API and any export, and it degrades to a labelled code block anywhere else). The kernel half is the new `PageExtensionSpec` contribution + `registries.page_extensions` (declaration only — the server never renders a body, so there is no handler); `GET /pages/extensions` (each entry carries `source` — the plugin that contributed it, read from the kernel's `page_extension_sources`, so the editor's insert menu groups by contributor without the client guessing from names it may never have seen; RADD-748) drives the editor's insert menu, so a PLUGIN's extension appears in a running Radd with no edit here and unmounting removes it. Rendering is client-side and works by SEGMENTING the body at extension fences (`splitExtensionBlocks`): page bodies go through Crepe, which owns `code_block`, so extensions render as ordinary React between prose runs rather than as a Milkdown node behind a portal. A name is declared only once the SPA can render it — `tests/test_page_extensions.py` compares the TypeScript registration list to the kernel's IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, so the insert menu can never offer an entry that lands as an "unknown extension" card. First-party set: `toc` (RADD-710, heading outline + optional subpages; heading anchors are assigned over the RENDERED document because Crepe emits none), `children`, `callout` (RADD-715, own computed per-theme token scale — NOT `--chart-*`, which encodes workflow state), `backlinks` (RADD-713), `include` (RADD-716, render-stack cycle guard), `label-list` (RADD-718). **RADD-713** — new table `page_links` (source, target; both CASCADE) maintained on the write path, only when the BODY changed; only markdown link targets and HTML hrefs count, never a path merely named in prose; `GET /pages/{id}/backlinks`, and a "Linked from" panel on every page. The migration backfills by importing the parser. **RADD-943** — the second derived index: `item_page_links.derived` (migration `d943pagerefs`) splits the table into rows a person typed (manual, untouchable by any edit) and rows the BODY owns (`pages/mentions.py`, reconciled on every body change from the issue references the text carries, so deleting the mention deletes the link). `items/mentions.py` now parses BOTH reference forms — the editor's `#[TD-1](TD-1)` token and the URL form `](/issues/TD-1)` / `](https://host/issues/TD-1)`, link targets only — which is what `chips.ts` has rendered as an issue chip since RADD-711, so the derived `mentions` links between ITEMS inherit the URL form too. A manual link on something already mentioned PROMOTES the derived row rather than 409ing; unlinking a derived row is refused (the next save would recreate it). `PageLinkedItem.derived` reaches the SPA, which drops the unlink button there. `POST /pages/reindex` (page.manage) rebuilds BOTH indexes over every live page — the backfill for content that never passed the save path, and what finally makes `backlinks.reindex_all` reachable (it had no caller; the `POST /pages/reindex-links` this row used to name did not exist). `restore_version` reindexes both, a leg RADD-713 missed. **RADD-944** — the automatic "In this section" subpage index below every page body is DELETED; children are the tree's job and the author's, via `radd:children`/`radd:toc`. **RADD-945** — the Linked-issues section starts COLLAPSED always, not just when empty; the count chip is what makes that acceptable. **RADD-948 — a page restriction runs down the TREE.** `page_access` / `readable_page_ids` now require every grant set on the ancestor path (self included) to pass, so restricting a parent closes its whole subtree in the tree, in FTS and by direct URL. RADD-792 deliberately shipped the opposite ("guessing at it would mean someone finds their subtree quietly invisible") and thereby traded a subtree quietly INVISIBLE for one quietly VISIBLE — the worse failure, and the DEFAULT one, since sensitive material is naturally a parent page with children. Deliberately EVERY ancestor rather than the nearest: nearest-wins lets a grant on the child re-open what the parent closed, which is the same hole one level down and exactly what the space rule already forbids. `page.manage` in the space still passes. The batched path resolves ancestors that are NOT in its input (a search returns matches, never their lineage) from one `(id, parent_id)` query per involved space, and both walks are loop-safe — `would_create_cycle` guards writes, but a loop already in the data must read as a finite path rather than hang the request. **RADD-718** — new table `page_labels` (page, label; both CASCADE) — the ASSOCIATION lives here, the label rows stay the `labels` module's (new `depends_on`), so a page and an issue tagged `incident` share one vocabulary. `PUT /pages/{id}/labels` (full replacement), `GET /pages/by-label/{name}?space=`; labels hydrate onto both `PageRead` and `PageSummary` in ONE query per tree. **RADD-733** — PDF export is a top-level SPA route (`/pages/<space>/<page>/print?subpages=1`), outside the app layout, rendered by the browser's own engine; no server-side change. **RADD-745 — one cascade, not three:** the kernel gains `CascadeSpec` + `registries.cascades`, and ONE consumer (`events/cascade.py`) drains the stream for every module whose rows must die with a parent. `attachments`, `comments` and (next) `access` all key children to a POLYMORPHIC parent, which cannot carry a foreign key, so `ON DELETE CASCADE` is unavailable — each had answered it separately (two head-seeded consumers plus four remember-to-call sites). A cascade is a `DELETE … WHERE parent = ?` that usually matches nothing; it does not deserve its own cursor when 18 loops already poll the instance. The manifest field is a FACTORY, because the set is derived from a binding registry other modules populate at import time and must survive `registries.clear()`. **RADD-744:** both binding registries (`attachments.ParentBinding`, `comments.CommentParent`) now carry `deleted_event`, and each module's GC derives its parent map from the registry instead of a hardcoded dict — a plugin that registers a parent gets cleanup without an edit to a module it does not own. **Specs 102/103:** registers the `page` attachment parent binding at init (wiki pages own files; global doc atoms gate them; `depends_on` gained `attachments`) and exposes `pages_for_embedding` + `pages_by_ids` for the semantic embedder/deflection. **Spec 106 → RADD-828/893:** `public.deflect_public` — the tokened form deflect's seam — is GONE with the anonymous form surface (RADD-828 deleted the tokened `/public/forms` path; the RADD-893 dead-code sweep took the seam): `search_public` stays FTS-only for the public KB box, authed deflection lives in `search/deflect.py`, and `pages_by_ids` keeps its `public_only` LIVE space re-check (the vector store's public flag is an embed-time copy, so a space flipped private since must not leak). **Spec 86: global scope — the workspace entity is gone; this module has no `workspace_id`, no singleton resolver.** Pages (spec 43; renamed from docs/wiki/KB by RADD-701) — the Confluence half. Tables `page_spaces` (workspace, unique slug), `pages` (space, self-FK parent tree, markdown body, float sibling position, `version`, archived_at), `page_versions` (prev content snapshotted on every content change; unique (page, version)), `item_page_links` (item↔page, PK pair). Spaces CRUD `/page-spaces` (read page.read, write **page.manage**; DELETE 409 unless empty or force). Pages: flat tree list per space (client builds the tree; archived subtrees pruned unless `include_archived`, manage-only), `POST/GET/PATCH/DELETE /pages` (page.write; **optimistic concurrency** — `expected_version` mismatch → 409 with current; content change bumps version + snapshots; moves cycle-guarded, don't burn versions; DELETE archives, `?hard=true` page.manage w/ live-child 409; `/unarchive` page.manage). Versions: list/fetch/`/restore` (= a NEW version). Links: `/pages/{id}/items` by item KEY (+ item.read; carries `derived` since RADD-943), reverse `GET /items/{id}/pages`. **FTS**: `GET /pages/search` over an expression GIN index (`to_tsvector(title||body)`) — queried live, no indexer (single-writer corpus). **Its declaration sits ABOVE `/pages/{page_id}`, and must (RADD-761):** Starlette matches in DECLARATION order, so a literal `/pages/<word>` written after the `{page_id}` route is answered by that route instead — `/pages/search` returned a 422 about parsing "search" as a UUID while appearing, correctly, in the OpenAPI schema and at `/docs`. `tests/test_route_shadowing.py` asserts this over the whole assembled app; reaching the routes at all means walking FastAPI's `_IncludedRouter` wrappers, whose own `path` is `None`. **Public pages (spec 74)**: `page_spaces.public` BOOL NOT NULL default false (`PageSpaceRead.public`, toggled via the existing space PATCH, page.manage; public means public INSTANCE-wide — no per-space token). Unauthenticated router `public_router.py` + service seam `public.py` (the forms public-router idiom — NO CurrentUser anywhere; unknown and non-public read as the SAME 404 so existence never leaks): `GET /public/pages/spaces` (trimmed cards `{id,name,slug,description}`), `GET /public/pages/spaces/{id}/tree` (non-archived rows `{id,parent_id,title,position}` — archived subtrees pruned by the authed visibility rules), `GET /public/pages/pages/{id}` (`{id,space_id,title,body,breadcrumb,updated_at}` — archived OR under an archived ancestor → 404; markdown renders client-side), `GET /public/pages/search?q=[&space_id=]` — `search.search_pages` grew `workspace_id: UUID | None` + keyword `public_only`/`space_id` filters (SAME statement/tsvector expression/GIN index; instance-wide when workspace_id is None; a private `space_id` pin 404s before searching). NO versions/history/links exposure. `pages/public.py` stays a plain service seam (its one-time importer, the forms' tokened deflect, is gone — RADD-828). Web: `/kb`, `/kb/$spaceId` (first page auto-selected), `/kb/$spaceId/$pageId` OUTSIDE the auth gate (public chrome + Sign-in link; composes `PageTree` [new `pageRoute` prop] + the lib/markdown renderer with queries local to `routes/public-pages.tsx`); `/settings/pages` grew the Public toggle + /kb copy-link (external-image help text); authed docs surfaces show the `PublicBadge` chip. Tests: `test_public_pages.py`. Permissions: `page.read` (viewer floor) / `page.write` (member) / `page.manage` (admins), migration backfills builtin role rows (spec-36 pattern). Frontend: sidebar Pages section, `/pages` spaces index, `/docs/$spaceId/$pageId` two-pane tree+page (inline title, MarkdownEditor body w/ stale-409 warning, History tab w/ restore, Linked-issues panel), issue-page Pages row, Cmd-K "Pages" section, `/settings/pages` (page.manage), realtime page_space/page tags, `Entity.pageSpace/page` cache tags. Yjs/pycrdt co-editing DEFERRED (no JS pkg manager for the client lib) — single editor + 409 guard + versions behind the same PATCH seam. **MCP (RADD-889/1005):** `pages/mcptools.py` contributes `get_page`, `search_pages`, `create_page`, `update_page`, `move_page` on the manifest; the per-page gate the REST router and the MCP writes share is the public `page_access.guard_page` (space atom + the page's own restriction). | events, projects, auth, items, workflow (+ deferred request-time edges, spec 106: **ai** [public-only doc candidates] + **search** [`fusion.rrf_fuse`] — both feature-detected, absent → plain FTS) | `page_space.created/.updated/.deleted`, `page.created/.updated/.deleted/.moved/.restored`, `page_link.created/.deleted` | — **RADD-1240 (radd-hq/radd#12):** the MCP receipt carries `url` — the `/pages?pageId=<number>` permalink under `app_base_url` — because an agent given only an id assembled `/pages/<uuid>` itself; the SPA's space route now RESCUES that shape (a uuid no space owns is asked of `pageByKeyQuery` and replaced by the page's address) and an unknown uuid explains the two address forms and links to the spaces. **RADD-1244:** `version` moves WITH a history row, never without one — a live autosave inside the spec-122 window writes the body only (`PageHook.BODY_AUTOSAVED`); the collab room seals a session that ends without its final save (`seal_history`: row + bump on room drop / hub shutdown), so the header's number is always the row count plus one and a stale REST client still 409s. **RADD-1248:** `refs.page_ref` carries `path` (the readable address's page part, walked up the parent chain) and the `space` ref, so a consumer holding the ref alone can name, scope and link the page. **RADD-1249:** the archive browser is a list you can WORK — search over title and path (client-side, one space's worth), multi-select, and `POST /page-spaces/{id}/archived/restore` / `…/delete` over the selection, answering per page (`done` / `skipped` with the single-page reason): restore runs ancestors-first, delete deepest-first (a child selected beside its parent is removed by its own request, not by the parent's FK cascade a moment later), a page with live children is refused per page, and a page the actor may not touch is skipped, never a whole-request 403. |
| `collab` | **Collaborative editing (spec 122, RADD-1161) — one live Yjs document per wiki page, in this process.** `POST /collab/pages/{id}/join` `{role: editor|observer}` (`CurrentUser` only — the anonymous Actor never joins; NOT in the anonymous inventory) gates through the SAME `page_access.guard_page` the PATCH uses (editor ⇒ `page.write`, observer ⇒ `page.read`) and answers `{session, role, seed, page_version}`; `seed` is true for exactly ONE editor while the room's document is empty (held until that session's first update, its disconnect, or `collab_seed_grant_seconds`) — the client that seeds the document from the page's markdown, so two simultaneous first joiners cannot double the page. `WS /collab/pages/{id}?session=` authenticates the session COOKIE exactly as `realtime` does (accept-then-close `4401`; re-validated on the `realtime_session_refresh_seconds` deadline and on inbound frames, throttled to `collab_frame_auth_seconds`), **RADD-1181** also rechecks current page/ancestor/space permission on socket connect, every outbound frame, every inbound document update and the idle deadline; loss closes `4403` and requires a fresh join. It requires the session to be THIS user's for THIS page (else `4403` — rejoin), then speaks the y-websocket protocol through pycrdt-websocket's `YRoom` (binary frames: sync step1/step2/update + awareness). `channel.py` is the seam: an OBSERVER's sync step2/update frames are dropped before the room sees them, its awareness relayed; editors' updates are applied and broadcast. **Persistence**: `page_collab_docs(page_id PK→pages CASCADE, state BYTEA, page_version, updated_at)` (migration `d1161collab`) — the full encoded state, written debounced (`collab_persist_debounce_seconds`) and when the last client leaves, tagged with the `pages.version` the room tracks; a room opening with a row at the page's CURRENT version resumes it, any other row is discarded (something that was not the room wrote the page; markdown is truth). Empty rooms drop from memory after `collab_room_idle_seconds`; shutdown persists everything. **The write guard** rides `pages`' hooks (`guard.py`, the `automations/subscribers.py` shape): `page.body_writing` refuses a body write while the room has a connected editor and `collab_session` is not one of them — 409 `being edited live by Ada, Grace` (names via `auth.users_by_ids`) — and VOUCHES for a save that is (`live_editor`), which is what skips `expected_version`; `page.version_bumped` moves the room's tracked version after a room save, and after a body write by anything else (only possible with no editor connected) RESETS the room — clients closed `4409`, stored state discarded. `is_enabled` asks the kernel registry, so a hot-disabled plugin stops guarding. Rooms are per-PROCESS (docs/deploy.md). Editor actions: `GET /ai/editor/actions` + `POST /ai/editor/stream` (Crepe AI + diff review in the SPA). **Editor-AI UX wave:** summarize became a real whole-issue digest — char-BUDGETED sections (`take_recent`: newest entries kept whole until the budget runs out; no more fixed last-12-comments) plus a **time-tracking digest** (totals + per-person split + recent entries via `timelogging.item_summary`, deferred feature-detected import — the prompt section simply never appears when the module is absent, the project has it off, or nothing is logged; the system prompt asks for an effort line only "when a Time tracking section is present"); the description's read-menu offers **Summarize issue** (this endpoint) instead of text-only. `POST /ai/similar` — similar issues for a TEXT seed (`{text, exclude_item_id?}`; the same SEMANTIC-FIRST retrieval as `/items/{id}/similar` via `similar_to_seed`, never reranked — no source issue to compare against); powers the SPA's read-mode AI menu (`AiReadMenu` on rendered comments/description/wiki: Find similar + streamed Summarize in a popover, transforms hand off to the editor as an initial whole-doc run), the editor toolbar AI button (selection-or-whole-document, `AiActionPicker`), and the **per-block diff review fork** (`web/src/components/editor/diff/` — replaces Crepe's per-chunk char-level Accept/Reject: one controls pair per changed textblock, word-boundary visual expansion, and rejects dispatched range-padded because upstream's overlap test can never match a pure deletion). Embeddings package: runtime-managed pgvector schema (`ensure_schema`, outside Base.metadata), per-model partial HNSW index, the `ai.embedder` consumer + ONE reconcile sweep (backfill+silent-events+model swaps), candidate seam for search/similar/deflect. Item vectors embed key+title+description ONLY (dogfood fix: comment text made imported boilerplate — automation nags, MR notifications — dominate the vectors, so unrelated issues ranked as neighbors; comment matches still arrive via the FTS half of /search and deflection; the recipe change stales every content hash, so the sweep re-embeds the corpus by itself). The similar rerank prompt ranks RELATEDNESS, keeping non-duplicate related issues (it originally detected duplicates only — models kept the literal twin and dropped the rest). Settings → AI (providers/roles/toggles/presets, Test button). Similar-issues is SEMANTIC-FIRST: vector neighbors ARE the pool when embeddings are up, before the optional LLM rerank; the OR-ed FTS pool runs only as the fallback when semantic is off/empty — one shared common token was enough to surface an unrelated issue as a top "text match", and its top-rank-normalized score always read 100%. NL→SLQ is dialect-aware (`items`\|`worklog` per spec 98 — the worklog prompt teaches `issue.*` delegation and validates through `compile_worklog_query`), repairs entity VALUES pre-compile (`nlrepair.py` + `fuzzy.py`: "jimmy" → the real account via the items autocomplete candidates seam; substitutions reported in the explanation — SLQ itself stays exact), carries live issue-type + work-category + WORKFLOW-STATE names in-prompt (small sets in the prompt, unbounded sets via repair) and a `Today is <date>` anchor. **RADD-1140:** the prompt teaches that fixed/closed/finished/resolved/complete/completed/shipped mean `category = done` and open/unresolved/active/pending mean `category != done` unless the user names a listed state (`types.STATE_WORDS`, one enum-backed table shared with the repair pass); in `nlrepair`, a `state`/`epic.state`/`parent.state` (+ `issue.` forms) value with NO fuzzy neighbour is rewritten to that category comparison (`RepairKind.CATEGORY`, operator flipped for the not-done words) or, outside the table, left as written and reported (`RepairKind.UNKNOWN_STATE` — the explanation says the state does not exist rather than the query returning nothing). `ai` now declares `workflow` (StateCategory). LiteLLM/Ollama/vLLM, or `anthropic`). One provider seam `complete(system, user)` (pure payload/header builders both shapes; upstream failure → clean 502). `GET /ai/status`; `POST /items/{id}/ai/summarize` (item.read; description+comments+history digest, NOT stored); `GET /items/{id}/similar` — **FTS candidates ALWAYS** (new public seam `search.similar_to_text`, RBAC-scoped) + LLM rerank when enabled (`reranked` flag; lenient JSON extraction, FTS-order fallback); `POST /slq/nl` — NL→SLQ with the frozen grammar + live registry in-prompt, **compile-validated server-side** with one error-fed retry then 422 `{slq, error}`. **Spec 119 — `ai.validate`, the second contributed automation node** (`ai/automation_node_validate.py`, a SIBLING of `automation_node.py` rather than a mode on it). `ai.classify` ROUTES — enumerated answers, so it cannot invent a branch, and it says nothing in its own words. This one WRITES: the admin gives a quality bar in prose, `complete_structured` returns `{passed, findings:[{message, field?}]}`, and each finding is a sentence the submitter reads. Gate kind, FIXED ports `pass`/`fail`/`unavailable` (unlike classify, whose ports are its answers — here what varies is what the model SAYS, not how many ways the packet can go), `arity="set"` fixed. Findings reach the collection through **`ctx.add_finding(message, field)`** — the seam `automations` exposes, so this module still imports nothing from it; on an ordinary event walk nothing is collecting, `add_finding` is a no-op, and the node is a pure router. **An AI outage must not silently block intake**: a provider failure, a dormant feature, an unresolvable gate and a node with no prompt ALL take `unavailable` and, by default, record no finding — `on_unavailable: fail` is the deliberate opt-in for an admin who would rather refuse than accept anything unchecked. Findings BEAT the model's own `passed` flag (a model that listed three problems and then ticked "fine" has told us about the problems); an unknown `field` degrades to a general finding against the live builtin + `cf.<key>` vocabulary rather than being dropped; `max_findings` is clamped into its schema's own range, and `or DEFAULT` is deliberately NOT used because a stored `0` is falsy and "report none" is not "report five". New `AiFeature.VALIDATION` + `SettingKey.AI_VALIDATION` + `config.ai_validation` + the `SettingSpec` — all four, which `test_ai_features.py` enforces. It also closed a pre-119 hole in `automations.service._validate_graph`: contributed-node schema + permission checks ran for ACTION nodes only, so every contributed GATE (`ai.classify` since spec 116) stored happily with its required params blank and took its fallback port forever. **Spec 120 — `ai.generate`, the third node** (`automation_node_generate.py`), and the one the other two make sense of by contrast: `ai.classify` ROUTES (enumerated answers ARE the ports), `ai.validate` WRITES PROSE at a person, this one FILLS IN NAMED VALUES the rest of the graph reads as `{{<node>.<field>}}`. Params: `prompt` (<=2000), the shared `include` toggles, and `fields` — up to 8 `{name, kind: text|enum, choices}`; ONE `complete_structured` call with a schema built from them, every enum field a JSON-Schema enum, so the model cannot answer outside the list (the classifier's safety property, per field instead of per node). An enum with no choices degrades to text; a field name that could never be a token is DROPPED, which keeps the write path's token check honest. `text` is always produced. **No `on_unavailable`** — unavailability IS the port: no values are published, so every token that would have read one misses and its action records a skip, and a graph that wants to react wires `unavailable`. A GATE kind because a contributed ACTION cannot name the port it leaves by (`_run_action` returns what it created and `_run_node` emits `out` unconditionally), and fixed SET arity because the packet's variable bag has one slot per node. `ai.classify` also publishes its chosen answer as an ENUM output. Pure `parsing.py`: push → branch+commit links, pull_request → upserting `pr:<repo>:<number>` link (open/merged/closed) — every id spelled by `vcs/ids.py` since RADD-1124 (a commit is `commit:<repo>:<sha>`, never a bare SHA, so the webhook, the backfill and the CI stamp share one row); same word-bounded key extraction as gitlab (deliberately duplicated — connectors stay independently disableable). Writes via `vcs.upsert_vcs_link` as SYSTEM actor; `RADD_FORGEJO_MERGE_TRANSITION_STATE` mirrors gitlab's (spec 112 replaces it with per-project settings). Admin CRUD `/forgejo/connections` + `/forgejo/repos` + `POST /forgejo/connections/{id}/test` (calls the host's `/api/v1/version`), gated on the **`vcsconn.create/update/delete`** atoms (umbrella `global.manage` — the dashboard precedent; declared here by spec 111, **moved to `vcs` by RADD-1258**). Entry date = `created` (Forgejo has no "spent on"), which the note says; author email via `GET /users/{username}` when visible, else the identity map. Everything after fetching is `vcs.timemirror.reconcile` with scope `pr:<repo>:<n>`. Tests: `test_forgejo_timelogs.py` (MockTransport; `timelogs._TRANSPORT_FOR_TESTS` hands the receiver one). Pure `plan_alerts(payload, existing)`.
