| `mailintake` | Email-to-issue + requester loop (specs 47+62). **Intake**: IMAP poller (imaplib via asyncio.to_thread; `\Seen` is ITS cursor; the loop starts on `run_workers` ALONE — **the gate is the rows**, see RADD-970 below) over UNSEEN: subject key `[TD-123]` (or bare key under Re:/Fwd:) → public comment as SYSTEM actor w/ sender noted (+ contact's `last_message_id` refreshed); else new item (title = Subject, text/plain body ≤10k, label `email`, reporter matched by sender email — **no active match = reporter NULL + a `mail_contacts` row**). **Plus-address routing (spec 62)**: To/Cc/Delivered-To/X-Original-To `anything+key@…` targets that project when it exists (case-insensitive), else `RADD_MAIL_PROJECT_KEY`. **Contacts**: `mail_contacts` — since RADD-980 an N-ARY table (`id` PK, `item_id` FK indexed, `email`, `name`, nullable `last_message_id` = that address's latest INBOUND Message-ID, `is_primary`; UNIQUE `(item_id, email)`) — a ticket has as many contacts as the conversation does; the sender who WRITES first becomes primary, an address already on the item is never overwritten (a second sender on the thread gets its own row; re-mailing from an existing one only advances that row's `last_message_id`). Threading itself does not run off `last_message_id` — see RADD-954 below (In-Reply-To → References → subject key → new issue). Public seams `service.upsert_contact`/`contact_for_item`/`send_ack` (consumed by forms' public submits, the csat sender's recipient resolution [spec 65], and the automation send_email action's `contact` role [spec 66]). `GET /items/{item_id}/mail-contact` (item.read, 404 when none) → the issue rail's External-requester chip. **Ack**: an intake-created (or public-form) item that captured a contact gets `[KEY] title`, sent POST-COMMIT through `transport.send_item_mail` (RADD-970 — see below); `RADD_MAIL_SEND_ACK` (default true). **Outbound replies** (`outbound.py`, consumer offset `mailintake.outbound`, loop always started under `run_workers`): `comment.created` events passing the pure `should_reply` gate (has contact ∧ PUBLIC ∧ actor ≠ SYSTEM — the SYSTEM gate stops inbound-mail/automation comments echoing back) → email the contact the FULL comment body (`comments.comment_body`) as `Re: [KEY] title` with In-Reply-To/References from `last_message_id`. Runs on the shared `events.runner.run_head_seeded` scaffold: first start seeds the cursor at the stream HEAD (never mail the backlog); the cursor commits BEFORE sending (at-most-once: a dropped reply beats a duplicate); SMTP unset = advance silently (plans nothing), so enabling SMTP later replays nothing. **RADD-951 wave — the email service desk.** `mail_messages` (RADD-952) is the Message-ID store threading depends on: unique `message_id` doubles as the 7-day dedup key and the reply→item lookup, because nothing previously recorded what Radd SENT (`mail_contacts.last_message_id` is one INBOUND id, overwritten every reply) so threading fell through to the subject key. **Threading order is now In-Reply-To → References (newest first) → subject key → new issue** (RADD-954); sub-addressing is deliberately NOT the mechanism (corporate systems and lists strip `+`). `intake.accept` (RADD-951) is the provider-agnostic core every transport calls — guards, dedup, threading, quote stripping, attachments, item-or-comment — proved real by the IMAP poller dropping from 242 to 115 lines to use it. `POST /integrations/email` (RADD-953) is HMAC-verified over the raw body (empty secret rejects everything) with a status contract that becomes an SMTP outcome: 4xx bounces, 5xx retries, so everything past authentication answers 503 — a `ConflictError` from an unset project key mapped to 409 and would have bounced customers' mail for OUR misconfiguration. Parsing (RADD-956): HTML-only mail falls back to markup-STRIPPED text (`html_body.py` — no allow-list to get wrong, no new dependency), quoted history is cut conservatively (`quoting.py`, never returns nothing), attachments ride the spec-102 seam. Loop guards (RADD-957): `Auto-Submitted` ≠ `no`, self-addressed mail, and an in-process per-sender rate limiter; every drop is 202, because bouncing at a loop is a message into the loop. Outbound (RADD-955) mails the item's PARTICIPANTS over notify's watcher set (it previously mailed one contact) and **stores the Message-ID the SENDER REPORTS**, not the one composed — SMTP honours a client-set id and the Gmail API replaces it, so storing the intended value unthreads every reply with nothing raised. `radd/smtp.py` gained `SmtpConfig` + a return value for exactly this. **Events (RADD-960):** `mail.received` / `.sent` / `.failed` / `.dropped`, item-scoped except `dropped`, carrying sender/sender_domain/subject and **never the body** (an event payload is readable by anything that can read the stream). They need no edit to `automations` — `catalog.TRIGGERS` derives from the kernel event registry. **Configuration is ROWS (RADD-958/961):** `mail_sources` (webhook\|imap\|**google\|outlook**, address, secret, default project), `mail_senders` (smtp\|**google\|outlook**; `is_default`, from/reply-to), `mail_rules` (an ORDERED chain per source: `recipient` = alias→project, `sender` = address or @domain, `subject` = substring, `llm` = classify the CONTENT via `ai_client.complete_choice` behind `AiFeature.MAIL_ROUTING` + the `ai_mail_routing` setting, every failure falling through). Migration `d958mailcfg` (the preset kinds need none — `kind` is already a string column); `seeding.py` creates the first rows from env ONCE (the spec-101 rule) and is never consulted again — and it seeds `smtp`/`imap`, never a preset kind, because an env var that NAMES a host is by definition the custom kind. **Gmail/Outlook presets, on the spec-110 pattern (RADD-969):** `types.KIND_DEFAULTS` maps a kind → `MailKindPreset` (display name, summary, smtp host/port/starttls, imap host/port, app-password guidance + help URL); a preset kind is a DEFAULT SUPPLIER, not a transport — google/outlook sources are polled by the same IMAP poller and google/outlook senders send through the same `SmtpSender`. **The row stores BLANK where the preset answers** and `resolve.py` resolves at READ time (`sender_host/port/starttls/username`, `source_host/port/username`, `source_pollable`), so upgrading a preset upgrades every existing row; an explicit row value always wins (`starttls` excepted — a bool has no "unset" and the form hides it). Username falls back to the from-address/address, which is what Gmail's login IS. `registry.save_*` refuses a hand-configured imap/smtp row with no host (ConflictError) while a preset row saves with nothing, and `default_sender`/`outbound_configured` read the RESOLVED host — a hostless Gmail row that the test button sent through but the transport called "nowhere to send from" is the exact bug the 968/969 seam could have shipped; `senders.sender_for` is the ONE kind→implementation dispatch (`transport.py` and the settings test-send both call it — two copies is how the test button came to answer "no implementation" for a kind that sent fine, and the RADD-968 transport is where the consumer's copy went). `GET /mail/kinds` (the `GET /sso/kinds` shape) feeds both dialogs: kind, name, summary, host/port/starttls, guidance, help_url, and `preset` — the flag the form branches on to HIDE the connection fields rather than pre-fill them, since a pre-filled `imap.gmail.com` gets saved and then outlives the preset. **The classifier is reachable, declining is a verdict, and a broken rule says so (RADD-989):** `AiFeature.MAIL_ROUTING` was in `ai.types` and in neither `ai.features` table, so `feature_enabled` raised KeyError at `routing.py`'s call site, the chain's per-rule `except Exception` logged "rule raised, skipped", and every llm rule fell through on every message since RADD-961 — the tests passed because they pinned only the fall-through side. Three things changed with the wiring. `NO_MATCH_ANSWER` ("None of these") is APPENDED to the choices at ask time (never stored as an answer row), so off-topic mail reaches the source default as a DECISION instead of being cornered into a wrong category — the default was previously reachable only by failure. Every rule the walk consults leaves a `RuleOutcome` (`MailRuleStatus` matched\|declined\|errored) on `RoutingDecision.outcomes`, surfaced additively as `RoutingPreviewResult.outcomes` and rendered by `PreviewDialog` — surviving every failure means a CRASHED rule and a declining one left identical traces, so the dry run called both "no rule matched — source default" and sent the admin off to rewrite a correct rule. And `feature_enabled` is called OUTSIDE `_match_llm`'s guarded region deliberately: a missing dispatch entry is a wiring bug, not a classification failure, so it propagates to `decide`'s per-rule `except` (message still safe) and records the rule as ERRORED instead of being filed under "fell through (unexpected)" beside every provider hiccup. **The trace is the WHOLE chain, and a decline reads as one (RADD-994):** `decide` stopped filtering `enabled` in SQL and records what it skipped and what it never reached, so `MailRuleStatus` is `matched|declined|errored|disabled|not_reached` — a rule switched off, a rule below the winner and a rule that was DELETED used to arrive as the same silence, and "why didn't my rule fire" is the question the dry run exists to answer. A disabled rule below the winner reads DISABLED, not NOT_REACHED (being off is unconditional; reordering it would change nothing), which leaves NOT_REACHED meaning exactly one thing. `RoutingDecision.reason` is now always accurate and `rules_router` passes it through VERBATIM — recomputing it from `project_id` flattened a classifier that DECLINED, and a rule that matched while naming no project, back into "no rule matched", so the dry run disagreed with the line the live path logs. `PreviewDialog` numbers the chain as the chain dialog numbers it and puts each rule's detail on its own WRAPPING line (it was a `truncate` span with no title: the most informative field on the panel, clipped inside a modal); `RuleDialog` names the automatic `NO_MATCH_ANSWER` row in the category list itself, since an admin who cannot see the choice the model is given either fights it in the prompt or duplicates it. Proof: `web/scripts/mail-routing-trace-proof.mjs` (seeds a five-outcome chain, reads the statuses back off `data-rule-status`, deletes its sources). Routing runs ONLY in `_create`, so a reply resolved by threading never reaches a classifier — first-message-only comes free from pipeline order. `EmailPlan.recipients` exists because aliases share a mailbox: `pipeline@` vs `help@` is visible only in To/Cc/Delivered-To, never in the IMAP connection. **Settings → Email** (`/mail/*`, global.manage — `config_router.py` = kinds/sources/senders, `rules_router.py` = the chain + dry run, both gating on `require_mail_admin`) with a **test send** reporting the relay's actual Message-ID and a **routing dry run** naming the rule that captured a message — the Storage lesson, since an ordered chain nobody can dry-run makes "why did this land there" unanswerable. The SPA page is a thin route over `web/src/components/settings/email/` (`SourcesPanel`/`SourceDialog`/`RuleChainDialog`/`RuleDialog`/`PreviewDialog`/`SendersPanel`/`SenderDialog`/`TestDialog`), mirroring the sign-in and storage splits; reads go through `lib/queries/mail-admin.ts` and the connection line shows the RESOLVED host/port/username, since a preset row stores none of them. **Rendering (RADD-967):** the reply used to ship the raw comment body — no author, no key, no link, plain text — and had no tests at all. `reply.py` now holds WHO (`recipients_for`) and WHAT (`render`, per recipient) while `outbound.py` stays the consumer; the body comes from `radd.mailrender` as text+html carrying the author (`payload["author"]["name"]`, the ref the event always had), `[KEY] Title`, the comment, a View-issue link and a footer keyed by `MailRecipientKind` (watcher → "you are watching KEY"; requester → "you contacted us about KEY" — `REPLY_REASON_TEMPLATES`), which is why the body is composed per address. Subjects (`ACK_SUBJECT_TEMPLATE`/`REPLY_SUBJECT_TEMPLATE`) stay wire constants in `types.py` because the bracketed key threads replies; `ACK_BODY_TEMPLATE` moved into `mailrender.ACK_BODY`. `OutboundMessage.html_body` carries the html part through the sender seam. **Transport + narrowing (RADD-968):** `transport.py` (re-exported as `service.send_item_mail` / `service.outbound_configured`) is now the ONE way anything mails a person about an issue — resolves the `mail_senders` row through `senders.sender_for` (falling back to the env relay as `send_ack` always did, so a seed-era instance still mails — `_EnvSender` carries `kind="smtp"` so resolution treats it as the custom kind it is), builds `Reply-To` + `In-Reply-To`/`References` from `mail_messages` and prefers the thread's STORED subject (`Re: …`) over the caller's `[KEY] Title` opener, sends, records the id the sender REPORTS, and emits `mail.sent`/`mail.failed` **per message** (payload shape unchanged — `recipients` is a one-element list). It never raises; `session=None` opens and commits its own. **`emit_failure=False` (RADD-997)** suppresses the `mail.failed` event for one send and has exactly one caller — notify's mailer, on the attempts between its first failure and its last. The default answers for everyone else and is right: a reply and an ack are sent once, so their failure is final when it happens; a RETRYING caller re-answering "did this person hear from us" every five seconds is noise in a stream every consumer reads. `outbound.py` is now purely the consumer and `reply.recipients_for(session, item_id)` returns the external `mail_contact` ALONE — the watcher loop is deleted (notify mails users, off its permission-gated rows), and a contact whose address belongs to an ACTIVE user is skipped so a staff member who once mailed in is not sent the same comment twice. `OutboundReply` lost `in_reply_to`/`references`: threading is resolved at SEND time, so nothing can go stale between planning and delivery. **`notify` left `depends_on`** — the edge now runs the other way, as notify's weak deferred reach into this transport. **Finishing the env→row split (RADD-970):** two env references outlived RADD-958 and each cancelled half of it. **(1) The poll LOOP** was gated `run_workers and bool(settings.mail_imap_host)`, so a mailbox added in Settings → Email on an instance with no mail env vars was a complete row with a lit capability pill that nothing ever polled — silently. The loop now starts on `run_workers` alone (the outbound consumer's posture all along) and **`poller.run_once` returns 0 early when `registry.polled_sources` is empty**: the per-tick row check IS the gate, because `PeriodicLoop.enabled` is SYNC and cannot await a session. `mail_imap_host` survives for `seeding` only. The empty tick costs exactly one SELECT — the early return precedes the `own_addresses` read, which a test traps. **(2) `service.send_ack`** dialled `radd.smtp` off the environment with hand-built In-Reply-To headers; it now goes through `transport.send_item_mail`, so it sends from the **default `mail_senders` row** (env relay still the fallback), reads In-Reply-To/References out of `mail_messages` (intake records the inbound id in the transaction BOTH callers commit before acking — the poller and `POST /integrations/email`), **records the ack's own outbound Message-ID** so a reply to the receipt threads on a header instead of the subject key, and reports a failure as `mail.failed` rather than a log line. `AckPlan` swapped `message_id` for `item_id`; `send_ack` takes an optional leading `session` like the transport. **`pin_subject`** is the one new transport knob: `send_item_mail` normally prefers the thread's STORED subject (so a rename cannot split a conversation), but the ack must keep `ACK_SUBJECT_TEMPLATE`'s `[KEY]` — that bracket is the subject-line threading fallback (`parsing.extract_reply_key`) and the stored subject at ack time is the requester's own, which carries no key. Pinning touches the subject LINE only; Reply-To/In-Reply-To/References still come from the store, and the reply consumer is unpinned and unchanged. **`loops.own_addresses` is DELETED** — an env-only duplicate of `registry.own_addresses` (rows ∪ env), which is the definition every transport already called; two answers to "which addresses are us" is how the self-loop guard stops firing on one path with nothing raised. **The ack carries the issue URL (RADD-977):** RADD-967 deliberately omitted it ("the requester can't log in"), which is stale twice over — intake PROVISIONS an account for an unknown sender (RADD-828) and on an SSO instance the sender is often already signed in — so `mailrender.acknowledgement` now renders it in BOTH parts (a text line + an html button, `ACK_LINK_LABEL`) with reply-by-email still the PRIMARY, first wording and the `[KEY]` subject mechanics untouched. **Each source binds the sender that answers for it (RADD-979):** `mail_sources.sender_id` ("Send replies from", NULL = the default sender) + `mail_messages.source_id` = the item's mail ORIGIN, written on INBOUND rows only (`threading.record(source_id=…)` from `intake._create`/`_append`; outbound rows never carry one) — migration `d979mailbind`, both FKs `ON DELETE SET NULL`. **Resolution order in `transport._sender(session, item_id)`:** the sender bound to the item's origin source (via `threading.origin_source_id` — the EARLIEST inbound row naming one, so a mailbox CC'd in later cannot take over the identity, and `registry.bound_sender`, which falls through when that sender is disabled/deleted/hostless) → `registry.default_sender` → the env relay. ONE resolution point, so the reply, the ack and notify's per-event mail all inherit it — they all ride `send_item_mail`. Identity is a property of the ADDRESS a conversation arrived at, not of the instance: before this, a ticket raised at `help@` was answered by `agent@`. `outbound_configured` gained a second half (`registry.any_bound_sender`) because two enabled relays each bound to their own source leave `default_sender` ambiguous — answering "nowhere to send from" there would silence every message that had somewhere to go. `SourceDialog` gains the "Send replies from" select (enabled senders + the currently-bound one even when paused; blank = default) for ALL source kinds, round-tripped through `MailSourceRead`/`MailSourceWrite.sender_id`. **A ticket has as many contacts as the conversation does (RADD-980):** `mail_contacts` is reshaped in place by `d980contacts` — `(id PK, item_id FK indexed, email, name, last_message_id, is_primary)` UNIQUE `(item_id, email)`, every v1 row becoming its item's primary — because `item_id` was the PRIMARY KEY, i.e. "one external contact per ticket, ever", and real desk mail is not that shape: `parsing.extract_recipients` had harvested To/Cc since RADD-958 and only ROUTING read it, so a customer who copied two colleagues raised a ticket that knew one of the three and was answered to one of the three. **Capture:** `intake._capture_contacts` on CREATE records the SENDER first (which is what makes them primary) then every To/Cc address minus `own_addresses` (`_is_ours` also strips a plus tag, since spec 62's `support+td@` routing convention IS expected To-line traffic while `registry.own_addresses` holds the mailbox — a literal comparison files our own alias as a requester and mails it every reply), minus the sender, minus any address holding a REAL account (`_is_real_account` = active ∧ `source != EMAIL`) — a copied-in colleague is a user reached by notify, and recording them here would be RADD-968's duplicate fan-out again; on APPEND, `_touch_contact` records a NEW external sender (Cc capture is deliberately NOT repeated: a reply's To line carries whoever the client kept). **Primary:** `upsert_contact(copied_in=)` can only WITHHOLD the badge, never award or move it — the first person who WRITES is the requester, so an agent-raised issue gains one when a customer mails in, while an issue a colleague raised BY EMAIL with a customer copied in gains none at all and CSAT/`send_email`'s `contact` role fall through to the reporter. `contact_for_item` therefore FILTERS on `is_primary` (a bystander must not inherit a satisfaction survey) while new `contacts_for_item` returns all, ordered primary → oldest → **address** (contacts captured from one message share a transaction timestamp, so an id tiebreak would reshuffle the rail on every read — the trap `forms.requests._comment_signals` documents). `last_message_id` is per contact. `reply.recipients_for` returns them ALL and applies the active-user skip PER ADDRESS (it used to `return ()`, so one staff contact silenced the whole reply). `GET /items/{id}/mail-contacts` (list, `[]` when none) beside the unchanged 404-quiet singular; `MailContactRead` gains `is_primary`; the `ui/` remote chip lists every address with the primary starred and first. **The mailed reply is attributed to the person who sent it (RADD-981):** `_append` resolves the sender through `_sender_user` and a REAL account becomes the comment's AUTHOR with the body verbatim; a `UserSource.EMAIL` or unresolvable sender keeps SYSTEM + the `Email reply from …:` prefix. One fact, four broken seams: the issue read as if a robot spoke, `outbound.should_reply`'s SYSTEM gate refused to relay an agent's emailed answer to the requester, `slas.evaluation`'s first-response feed skipped it as a non-answer, and notify (which excludes the ACTOR) notified the agent of their own comment. **Attribution is not authorisation** — `From:` is forgeable, so the write goes through `comments.create_comment` (that person's own `comment.write`), and a `ForbiddenError` falls back to SYSTEM rather than dropping the mail. Two SYSTEM divergences went with it: `forms.requests._comment_signals` excludes `SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID` from `awaiting_requester` (a requester's own emailed reply lit their own row with "somebody answered you"; still COUNTED, mirroring the SLA path's judgment), and `_resolve_thread`'s SUBJECT-KEY leg is gated by `_may_thread_by_subject_key` — the sender must be an item contact, the reporter, or ANY active real account, else the message opens a NEW routed issue. Header threading stays open: an `In-Reply-To` names an id Radd generated and told one person, `[PROJ-412]` is a guessable string in a text field and keys are sequential. **The ack is a receipt for the EMAIL (RADD-995):** `intake._ack_plan` plans it for the SENDER on every mail-born CREATE, user or contact alike. It used to be planned only on the branch that captured a contact, so a recognised OIDC colleague became the reporter of a real ticket and heard nothing — caught live, silent because every other part of that path worked. Refusals are an absent `From:` and one of our OWN addresses (the loop guard already dropped that message; restating it at the composing site keeps the widened ack from becoming the one path that re-opens the loop). Still `mail_send_ack`-gated, still CREATE-only, still through `transport.send_item_mail(pin_subject=True)`. Tests (counts drift with every wave — run the suite for the current number, not this line): `test_mail_render.py`, `test_mail_threading.py`, `test_mail_routing.py`, `test_mail_config.py`, `test_mail_events.py`, `test_mail_ingest_endpoint.py`, `test_notify_mailer.py`, `test_mail_transport_reach.py` (RADD-983 — every sender rides the one transport), plus `test_sla_depth.py` (an agent's emailed reply meets the response target) and `test_requester_view.py` (a SYSTEM comment does not flip `awaiting_requester`). **RADD-983 — the ONE outbound seam widens to every mailer, not just item replies.** `send_item_mail`/the new **`send_plain_mail`** (no item, e.g. a digest) / `outbound_configured` / `mail_health` are re-exported from `transport.py` as `mailintake.service`'s public surface — notify (per-event mail + the digest), csat (the survey) and the automation `send_email` action all call THIS, not `radd.smtp` directly, so there is no mail leaving the instance by any other route; a caller looks for a module's public functions here, not in a file named after the implementation. **`service.mailable_user(user)` lives here too** (inactive ∨ address-less ∨ a spec-113 SERVICE account ∨ the system actor ⇒ not a person to mail) — CSAT and `send_email` are the two callers that previously had no such check and mailed whatever a resolved role (reporter/assignee) happened to be; notify carries an identical copy since it loads before mailintake and can only reach it deferred. **RADD-1032/1033 — mail intake hardening (migration `d1044mailhard`).** Sender authentication demotes a forged staff `From:`. `mail_sources.trusted_authserv_id` names the `Authentication-Results` authserv-id the source's own gateway stamps (e.g. its MX); a message whose trusted-gateway verdict for `From:` is fail-or-absent is DEMOTED before it ever reaches `_reply_author` — it cannot be attributed to the staff account it claims, so it lands as SYSTEM + the `Email reply from …:` prefix instead of impersonating them (layer 1). Layer 2 narrows the SUBJECT-KEY threading gate itself (`_may_thread_by_subject_key`): a bracketed `[PROJ-412]` is a guessable string in a text field and item keys are sequential, so a sender must be an item contact, the reporter, or ANY active real account before a subject-key match opens (or re-opens) an existing item on their behalf — an unqualified `comment.write` on the project used to be enough. **RADD-1033 — raw retention.** The inbound bytes ride the spec-102 blob seam, kept per `MAIL_RAW_RETENTION_DAYS` (NULL = off) regardless of how parsing/quoting mangled the rendered body, behind `GET /mail/messages/{id}/raw`. **RADD-1035 — a drop leaves a trace.** A per-message attachment cap that swallows parts leaves a note on the item (`types.py`'s dropped-attachment message) instead of silently shrinking the mail; a message the poller cannot even parse now emits `mail.dropped` keyed off the Message-ID (correlatable) rather than being flagged `\Seen` and vanishing. **RADD-1036 — mail health surfaces on Settings → Monitoring.** `transport.mail_health(window_hours)` aggregates recent `mail.sent`/`.failed` into a card (`monitoring` weak-depends this module, deferred + feature-detected, so the card just isn't there with mailintake disabled — see the `monitoring` row); `MailFailureReport` widened to THREE answers (error / given-up / neither) so a terminal failure reads differently from one still retrying. **RADD-1045 — the ack's body is an admin-editable instance setting** (`mail_ack_body`, Settings → Email — a service desk's wording is instance policy, not per-project) resolved through the generic scoped-settings cascade; `mailrender.acknowledgement` renders the stored (or shipped-default) prose rather than a hardcoded template, so `body` arrives pre-rendered and the renderer only wraps it. Pure `parse_email`/`extract_project_key`/`should_reply` tested in `test_connectors.py`. | projects, auth, items, comments, automations (also `intake.suppressed()` around `_create` — spec 119: the poller marks the message Seen, so a required check refusing a mail would drop the request with no issue and no bounce), events, attachments, settings (RADD-1045's `mail_ack_body` scoped setting) (weak: ai) | `mail.received`, `mail.sent`, `mail.failed`, `mail.dropped` | `on_startup`/`on_shutdown` (poller + outbound loops); consumer-offset pattern **RADD-1247:** `_plan_reply` returns None for a comment event whose parent is a page (`item: None`) — there is no requester to mail — instead of a TypeError per page comment. |
| `csat` | CSAT satisfaction surveys (spec 65) — the service-desk feedback loop's last mile. Table `csat_surveys` (item_id FK CASCADE **unique — one survey per item LIFETIME**, `token` String(64) unique `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`, nullable smallint `rating` 1–5, `comment` Text, `sent_at`, nullable `responded_at`). **Sender** (`sender.py`, consumer offset `csat.sender`, loop gated on `run_workers`, poll `RADD_CSAT_POLL_SECONDS`): `item.updated` events whose `changes` diff touched `state` AND whose state embed is done-category (`moved_to_done`, pure) → guards in order: `csat_enabled` resolves TRUE for the item's project (scalar cascade — per-project opt-in), outbound mail is configured (`mailintake.service.outbound_configured` — **since RADD-983**, replacing a bare `settings.smtp_host` check that silently never fired on a rows-only Settings → Email instance, radd-hq.com's own shape), no survey row yet (reopen→re-resolve never resends), recipient resolves (**mail contact preferred** via `mailintake.contact_for_item`, else the reporter when `mailintake.service.mailable_user` says there's a mailbox behind the account) → creates the row + emits `csat.requested`, then sends through the ONE transport (`mailintake.service.send_item_mail`, RADD-983 — per-source sender identity [RADD-979], threading onto the item's own conversation, `mail.sent`/`.failed` instead of a silent SMTP call) "How did we do?" with five links `{app_base_url}/public/csat/{token}?rating=N` (the SPA page POSTs — a mail-scanner prefetch never records). Cursor idiom = the shared `events.runner.run_head_seeded` scaffold: first start seeds AT THE STREAM HEAD; survey rows + csat.requested + cursor COMMIT BEFORE sending (at-most-once — planning writes ride the runner's session); SMTP/setting off = advance silently. **Public API** (spec-62 idiom, own unauthed router — the token is the credential): `GET /public/csat/{token}` → `{item_key, item_title, rating, responded_at}` (404 unknown); `POST` `{rating: 1..5, comment?≤2000}` → latest-wins update, `responded_at` stamped ONCE, emits `csat.responded`. `GET /items/{item_id}/csat` (item.read) → `{rating, comment, responded_at}`, **404 until answered** (the rail chip is 404-quiet). Both events carry entity_type=item (History feed) + `actor_id=None` (requester has no user; also lets automation rules fire — the engine only skips the SYSTEM actor). `report.py` = the models-only read seam reporting folds into `/reports/sla` (csat_avg/csat_count). Frontend: public SPA route `/public/csat/$token` (star row preselected from `?rating`, optional comment, thanks state, revise-allowed), amber stars chip in the issue rail once responded, CSAT tile + weekly avg-rating trend on the Service-desk report card. | workspace, auth, items, settings, events, mailintake (contact + transport seam, RADD-983) | `csat.requested`, `csat.responded` (payload: item_id, key [+ rating, comment excerpt ≤200 on responded]) | `on_startup`/`on_shutdown` (sender loop); consumer-offset pattern |
| `approvals` | **Approvals on workflow transitions (spec 71; per-entry rules since spec 107)** — JSM-style approvals built INTO the spec-61 machinery: `require_approval` is just another `TransitionCheck` (params `{approvers: [{kind: user\|team, id, name, required?}]}`, JSONB on `workflow_transitions` — no schema change there; write-validated in workflow with server-snapshotted display names). This module owns the lifecycle: `approval_requests` (item FK CASCADE, transition FK SET NULL, the rule's `approvers` entries SNAPSHOTTED verbatim, to_state FK states, requested_by, note, `ApprovalStatus` pending\|approved\|declined\|canceled\|applied; ONE live [pending\|approved] request per (item, to_state) = app-level 409) + `approval_votes` (request FK CASCADE, user, `approve\|decline`, note, unique (request, user) — revote REPLACES while pending). **Per-entry satisfaction (spec 107)**: each entry's electorate resolves live (a user entry is that person; a team entry is the team's CURRENT members — a deleted/shrunk-below-required team makes the entry unsatisfiable, deliberately: removing people never quietly lowers an approval bar); a request approves only when EVERY entry is satisfied (user entries need that person's own approve vote; team entries need `required` approve votes from current members) + **auto-apply**: the service calls `items.update_item(state_id=to_state)` AS THE FINAL APPROVER (real actor for audit) inside a SAVEPOINT — other guards failing rolls it back and BANKS the unlock (request stays approved; the errors ride the vote response for the UI toast); any decline → `declined` (terminal). Guard seams (deferred, feature-detected on the workflow/items side): `approved_target_state_ids(item_id)` → the string state ids `require_approval` passes for; `consume(item_id, to_state_id)` — called by `items.update_item` after a successful state change, flips approved → `applied` (one approval unlocks ONE move). API: `GET /items/{id}/approvals` (item.read → `{live, history, requestable_to_states}` — requestable computed server-side from the rules, live requests carry the resolved electorate + votes), `POST /items/{id}/approvals {to_state_id, note?}` (item.update; 409 no matching rule / live request exists; mode deliberately not consulted — requesting is meaningful whenever a rule exists), `POST /approvals/{id}/vote` (eligible approvers only → 403), `DELETE /approvals/{id}` (requester or project.manage → canceled), `GET /approvals/pending` (MY queue — powers the My Work card). Frontend: Require-approval config on the transitions editor (entry rows + SubjectPicker add, per-team required stepper — spec 107), the issue rail's Approvals card (per-entry progress chips "DevOps 1/2 · Hussein ✓", Approve/Decline/Cancel, Request-approval affordances — the federated remote's `entries` render), My Work "Awaiting my approval". `ApprovalRequestRead` carries `entries` ([{kind,id,name,required,approved_count,satisfied}]) beside the flat resolved electorate. | events, projects, auth, teams, workflow, items | `approval.requested/.voted/.approved/.declined/.canceled` — ALL entity_type=**item** (History feed, realtime item invalidation, item-scoped automations under the Service-desk trigger group; payload: item_id, to_state name, requester, approved_count, `approvers_summary` ["Hussein Jarrar; 2 of DevOps" — notifications render it verbatim] [+ voter/verdict; requested also carries `eligible_user_ids` for the notify fan-out]) | — |
| `participants` | **Request participants (spec 72; RADD-844 made it a RELATION)** — users + whole TEAMS following an item, riding the existing watcher + notify machinery. **Since RADD-844 a participant IS an access relation**: the module registers `@participant` on the ITEM resource (`ITEM_PARTICIPANT` in `__init__` — where-form: `id IN (SELECT item_id FROM item_participants WHERE user=me OR team IN mine)`; `holds=None`+`expensive=True`, gated via `authz.relation_holds_row_async`'s one-row EXISTS since membership lives in its OWN table, sync resolvers fail CLOSED), and the Baseline/Requester floors carry `item.read@participant` + (Baseline) `comment.write@own`/`@participant` (migration `d844partrel`) — the **second-reporter model**: a share means the person can OPEN that one item, COMMENT on it, and get notified, nothing wider. `comment.write`'s qualifier resolves against the ITEM via the kernel's **relation-domain** declaration (`register_relation_domain("comment.write", "item")` in comments — validation + the comments item-binding gate agree). Self-leave is an IDENTITY operation (no readability gate — a participant who lost project access can still stop the notifications). `item_participants` (item FK `work_items` CASCADE ix; EXACTLY-ONE-OF `user_id`/`team_id` — DB CHECK `ck_item_participants_one_subject` [the `view_shares` idiom, here DB-enforced], both FK CASCADE ix; `added_by` FK users; unique (item,user) + (item,team); user-merge folds rows via auth `_MERGE_DEDUPE` + an `added_by` repoint). API: `GET /items/{id}/participants` (item.read) → `{users, teams, rows:[{id, user\|team, added_by, created_at}], can_manage}` — `can_manage` computed per ACTOR server-side so the client stays dumb; `POST /items/{id}/participants {user_id\|team_id}` + `DELETE .../{participant_id}` gated **item.update OR the item's REPORTER** (an IDENTITY check, not a permission — THE point: a requester shares their own ticket), and a direct USER participant may always remove THEMSELF (leave); subject validation follows the approvals rule-write precedent (active user + workspace member or instance admin / same-workspace team, else 409; dupe 409; exactly-one-of enforced at the schema → 422). Semantics: adding a direct USER **auto-watches** them (`notify.add_watchers` — one fan-out mechanism, no parallel path); TEAM rows stay **LIVE** — the notify consumer's `recipient_ids` union resolves CURRENT team members at fan-out time via the seam `team_recipient_ids(item_id)` (deferred feature-detected import on notify's side), so joining a team joins its shared tickets and leaving stops delivery without cleanup rows; recipients still pass notify's item.read + internal-comment filters. **The add itself now tells the person (RADD-978):** notify consumes `item.participant_added` and writes a `participant_added` notification to the added USER (inbox + email by default — it is in `DEFAULT_EMAIL_TYPES`; "«Actor» added you to the issue"). A TEAM add notifies nobody personally, and a self-add is silent. Auto-watch only ever covered what happened NEXT, so being shared into an issue was the one moment the person had no idea it existed. No event/payload change here — notify reads the existing one by wire string. Frontend: the issue rail's Participants section (avatar + team chips with ×/"Leave", add user/team selects shown whenever `can_manage`), History verbs for both events, `participantsQuery` (`Entity.item` tag — participant events ride the item entity, realtime invalidation free). Tests: `server/tests/test_participants.py`. | events, projects, auth, teams, items, notify | `item.participant_added`/`item.participant_removed` — entity_type=**item** (History feed with NO `RELATED_EVENT_TYPES` entry [csat/approvals precedent], realtime item invalidation, item-scoped automations under the Service-desk trigger group; payload: item_id, `user`/`team` display refs + a scalar `participant` name lifted by history `_DETAIL_KEYS`) | — **RADD-1236 (radd-hq/radd#8): the roster over MCP** — `list_participants` / `add_participant` / `remove_participant` (`mcptools.py`), item by KEY, a person by EMAIL, a team by NAME (case-insensitive), never a row id; `kernel_enforced=False` because the gate is the service's own (item.update OR the reporter — a blanket atom would refuse the reporter the feature exists for); `item.update` is the catalog floor. `can_manage` rides the listing. |
| `dashboards` | **Spec 86: global scope — the workspace entity is gone; this module has no `workspace_id`, no singleton resolver.** **Composable dashboards (spec 75)** — user-assembled widget grids over data surfaces that ALREADY exist; the server side is deliberately thin (layout + sharing), every widget FETCHES through the ordinary read APIs at render time so RBAC/visibility is inherited, never reimplemented. Tables: `dashboards` (workspace FK, name, description, `owner_id`, `workspace_access` ShareLevel\|NULL — never 'owner' → 409, position), (sharing lives in `access_grants`, see below — `dashboard_shares` is GONE), `dashboard_widgets` (dashboard FK CASCADE ix, `WidgetType` StrEnum, nullable `title` override, `width` grid-thirds 1..3, position, `config` JSONB). **Sharing = the spec-92 access framework** (adopted, migration `ef4be162afa1`): dashboards register a `ResourceSpec` (resource_type=`dashboard`, accesses viewer/editor/owner, hierarchical, `default_open=False`, `project_scoped=False`, subjects user/team, `can_manage` = owner-or-co-owner) and per-subject shares are `access_grants` rows managed through the generic `/grants` API — spec 75 had copied `view_shares` verbatim into a `dashboard_shares` table, and that copy outlived the original (spec 92 deleted `view_shares` but missed dashboards). `owner_id` + `global_access` stay on the Dashboard row. Visibility = owner ∪ grantees ∪ active users when global_access set, non-visible → 404 admins included; editors change definition + widgets; owner/co-owners share/delete/transfer; `PUT /dashboards/{id}/sharing` now carries ONLY `global_access` (per-subject shares go to `/grants`, exactly as views), `POST /{id}/transfer` (previous owner kept as an editor GRANT); broadcast gate = the **`dashboard.create`** atom. User-merge/delete needs no dashboard-specific case any more — `_repoint_user_access_grants` covers every resource type generically. **Widget validation (widgets.py):** create bodies are a discriminated union on `widget_type` (the automations Action idiom → unknown type/shape 422); PATCH configs revalidate against the stored type (`WidgetConfigError` → 422); semantics per type — SLQ compiles via `items.validate_slq` (SlqError → 422 {detail, position}), referenced project/cycle/view must exist, live in the dashboard's workspace, and be visible/readable to the WRITER → 409 (view visibility via the views seam `visible_view`). `WidgetType`: report_throughput\|report_cfd\|report_time_in_state\|report_velocity\|report_burnup\|report_sla (the /reports/* endpoints), slq_count (`GET /items/count`), slq_list (`GET /items?q=&limit≤20`), view_count (`POST /views/counts` — per-viewer omission = the "Unavailable" signal). API: `GET /dashboards?workspace_id=` (visibility-filtered, position→name), `POST` (member floor item.read; workspace_access needs dashboard.create), `GET/PATCH/DELETE /dashboards/{id}` (reads carry widgets + per-actor can_edit/can_manage), widget `POST/PATCH/DELETE .../widgets[/{wid}]` (edit-gated; widget writes return the full DashboardRead and emit dashboard.updated). Frontend: sidebar Dashboards section (collapsible, + New dashboard), `/dashboards/$dashboardId` — 3-column CSS grid, widgets span their width, report widgets reuse the SHARED `components/reports/*` cards (new pin props: TimeInStateCard `initialKind`, VelocityCard `initialLast/initialMeasure`, BurnupCard `fixedCycleId/initialMeasure`, SlaCard `initialWeeks`), slq_count/view_count big-number cards, slq_list compact peek rows; a widget whose fetch 403/404s renders an Unavailable card — the dashboard never dies; Add-widget modal (type picker + per-type form with live SLQ validation), hover toolbar (edit/width-cycle/move/remove), Sharing dialog = the generalized `ViewSharingEditor` (`noun` prop). Widgets are ordinary entity-tagged queries (`Entity.item` on counts/lists → realtime keeps them live; report queries staleTime 60s). Migration `9176930be113` (+ backfills the three dashboard.* atoms into stored builtin admin rows). | `attachments` addendum | **RADD-790 — attaching a file is its own authority.** The item binding's `require_write` gated on `item.update`, conflating "may edit this issue's fields" with "may add a file to it": a role of `item.read` + `comment.write` — someone who may discuss an issue without editing it — posted a comment fine (201) and 403'd the instant the editor uploaded, so it was reported as "commenting is broken". Two new project-scoped atoms, **`attachment.create`** / **`attachment.delete`**, both riding `project.manage`; deleting someone ELSE's attachment is still `project.manage`. **No data migration**: `IMPLIED_PERMISSIONS[item.update]` contains both, so every role that could attach still can, and a role created tomorrow inherits the rule — a backfill would have fixed only the rows that existed the day it ran. SPA: `Permission.attachmentCreate` gates `AttachmentsSection` (the item page's last bare `item.update` check; everything else there goes through `useItemWritability`). The PAGE binding still uses `page.write` and moves onto the space scope in RADD-791 — a page attachment is governed by its space, not by a project. | attachments, auth, items | — | — |
| `pages` addendum | **RADD-791 — a wiki space is a grant SCOPE, the way a project is.** Every `page.*` atom was checked at GLOBAL scope because a page had none, which made per-space access inexpressible AND killed page commenting: `comments_binding` resolved `comment.write` (a PROJECT-scoped atom) with `project=None`, so a project-scoped grant never reached it and the gate behaved like a refusal for everyone but an admin. `global_role_grants` gains **`space_id`** (migration `307a8b156894`, FK + `ck_global_role_grants_one_scope`: at most one scope per row; the uniqueness keys gained the column, and `_MERGE_DEDUPE` with them — `test_merge_coverage.py` caught that omission). `PermissionScope.SPACE` + `GrantScopeKind`; `authz.effective_permissions(space_id=)` / `require(space_id=)` resolve through the SAME `grants.granted_role_ids`, so a space grant is the same kind of fact as a project grant with no second set of rules. A typed column with an FK rather than a polymorphic `(scope_type, scope_id)` pair — polymorphic cannot carry one, and trading referential integrity for an unrequested third scope is dev rule 5. **`pages/access.py`** is the pages-side seam: `space_permissions` / `permissions_by_space` (batched — the nav resolves every space at once) / `readable_spaces` (the wiki's `readable_projects`; list surfaces answer EMPTY, never 403). One edit to `_page_guard` rescoped ~20 endpoints; space-level writes, `list_pages`, `create_page`, export and templates pass `space_id`; cross-space reads (`/pages/search`, `/pages/by-label`, `/pages/extensions`, `by-path`) take the any-space floor and filter rows — FTS and label listing constrain by space BEFORE the limit, so unreadable hits cannot eat the result budget. Both bindings follow: comments and attachments resolve against `page.space_id`. `search`'s KB deflection and semantic doc results filter by readable spaces too (the deflection path is the one page surface an ISSUE reader reaches). Grant API + the ONE Grant Role dialog take `space_ids` beside `project_ids`; the grant row renders a third chip (a space grant used to fall through and render "Global"). Tests: `test_space_scope.py` (8), Baseline emptied throughout so nothing passes on a permissive floor. NOT yet space-aware: SSO provisioning rules (RADD-782) still grant project scope only. **SPEC 118 — the wiki's notification fan-out LEFT this module.** `watchers.notify_watchers` is deleted; the table and its four accessors stay, and `update_page` still auto-watches the editor on the write path. RADD-719 shipped that fan-out synchronously inside the save request on the argument that a consumer would be "a second delivery path to keep correct for a volume that does not need one" — the volume was never the point, it WAS the second path: it knew about page watchers only, it applied NO read gate (an edit told whoever had once clicked Watch, whatever the space had decided since), and `page.created` was never wired to it. Fan-out now runs in `notify/pageevents.py` off the stream. **New `pages/refs.py`** — the module's outward seam for a consumer that may import neither this package (load order) nor its models (the spine rule): `page_ref` / `space_ref` (registered as the plugin's `entity_refs`, so `_emit_page` passes `subjects={"page", "page_space"}` and the KERNEL writes the shapes, RADD-923), `space_of_page_ref` (a `comment.created` on a page carries an id and no space, and a space is what a wiki subscription is scoped to), `page_watcher_ids`, and `readable_page_ids_for_users` — the delivery gate, batched per page, because a page's visibility is a space role PLUS every restriction on the ancestor path (RADD-948) and that is this module's answer to give. Every page EventTypeSpec DECLARES both subjects, so the loader refuses to boot if a ref goes missing; `page.created`'s payload lost its bare `space_id` string to the ref. | pages, auth, comments, attachments, search | — | — |
| `pages` addendum 2 | **RADD-792 — a page can be restricted individually, the way a FIELD can.** `pages/page_access.py` registers `page` as a spec-92 `ResourceSpec` (`default_open=True`, accesses read/write, `implied_by` write→read, subjects user|team|role, `project_scoped=False`, `can_manage` = `page.manage` IN THE PAGE'S SPACE) — a registration, not a new mechanism, because "open until someone is named, then closed to everyone else" is exactly what custom fields already do. **The two layers compose in one direction only**: the SPACE decides whether you are in the room (RADD-791's role grant), the PAGE grant can only NARROW within it — a page grant naming someone with no space access grants them nothing, or "restrict this page" would double as a way to hand out access to a space you were never given (asserted). `page_access` (one page) and `readable_page_ids` (batched, one query per layer) are the seams; `_page_guard` applies it after the space check and 404s rather than 403ing so a restricted page's EXISTENCE stays private. Filtered on every list surface: the page tree (`list_pages(actor=)`), FTS (`drop_restricted_results`) and the label index (`drop_restricted_labelled`) — a title is usually the sensitive part, so a search hit would defeat the restriction on its own. Role-subject grants resolve against roles held IN THAT SPACE. `hierarchical` on the spec means ordered access LEVELS, NOT the page tree: child pages do NOT inherit a parent's restriction, and subtree inheritance stays unbuilt rather than guessed at. Tests: `test_page_restriction.py` (7), Baseline emptied. **RADD-793 — the screens.** `GET /role-grants?space_id=` answers the question an admin actually asks ("who has access to THIS space") — deliberately NOT folding in the instance-wide grants that also apply, since a Revoke button beside one would take access away everywhere. `SpaceAccessPanel` hangs off each row in Settings → Pages (who holds what + Grant role, users/teams only — a role-subject grant here would be the grant itself); the unified Grant Role dialog gained a Wiki-spaces picker beside Projects, so ONE dialog still covers every scope. "Restrict page…" is a padlock on the page itself over the generic `<AccessGrantsEditor>` — the decision is about that page and gets made while reading it. Proofs: `web/scripts/space-access-proof.mjs` (14 checks, both halves — the panel lists the real grant, and a granted member sees exactly one of two spaces with the other's pages 403ing and its titles absent from search) and `scoped-member-proof.mjs` (the project half). | pages, access, auth | — | — |
| `pages` addendum 3 | **Spec 122 — the body write has HOOKS (`pages/hooks.py`), and a collaborative save has a history WINDOW.** `update_page` dispatches `PageHook.BODY_WRITING` (`PageBodyWriting`: page, actor, `collab_session`, `body_changes`, and the handler's answer `live_editor`) before a body write and `PageHook.VERSION_BUMPED` (`PageVersionBumped`) once `version` moved — the spec-119 inversion again: `pages` never imports `collab`, with no subscriber both are no-ops. `PageUpdate` gains `collab_session` (a session id from `POST /collab/pages/{id}/join`) and `final`. When a handler vouches for the session (`live_editor`) the `expected_version` check is SKIPPED — the live document is the current version — and the snapshot rule changes: `version` still bumps on every content change, but the `page_versions` row is written only when the page's newest row is older than the new instance-level scalar `page_collab_version_window_seconds` (`SettingKey.PAGE_COLLAB_VERSION_WINDOW_SECONDS`, default 300, section `pages`) or `final` is set — a person's history is a list of work sessions, not of pauses in typing. | `pages` addendum 5 | **RADD-1233 — pages are KEYED by id and ADDRESSED by path; slugs are unique among live siblings.** The slug had been acting as the page's identity (unique per space across live AND archived rows, frozen after create, `-2` suffixes, archived pages squatting on names). Now: `pages.number` (BigInteger from `pages_number_seq`, `uq_pages_number`) is the human key and the PERMALINK (`/pages?pageId=12402`; `GET /pages/by-number/{n}`); `uq_pages_space_slug` is DROPPED for the partial expression index `uq_pages_live_sibling_slug` (space, coalesce(parent), slug WHERE archived_at IS NULL), so two parents may each have an `onboarding` and an archived page frees its name; the canonical URL is `/pages/<space>/<slug>/<slug>/…` (`GET /pages/by-path/{space}/{path:path}`), and `PageRead`/`PageSummary`/`PageBreadcrumb`/`PageBacklink`/`PageLabelled` carry `number` + `path`, folded over rows already loaded (`core.page_paths`) — nothing stored. **Resolution (`pages/paths.py`) is one query**: live pages whose slug is in the segment set, walked from the root in memory (`core.walk_path`; archived rows as a second pass, so an archived page is reachable when no live page holds its path); then an EXACT lookup in **`page_path_history`** (page_id, space_id, path — written by `_remember_paths` for the page AND every descendant whose address changed on rename, move or restore), so a stale link lands on the page it named, never a namesake; and nothing else — the migration SEEDED the history with every nested page's pre-1233 single-slug address and wrote `page_number` into old notification payloads, so no resolver or renderer carries a legacy branch (RADD-1234). `_free_slug`/`_reslug` are sibling-scoped; a move decides its slug BEFORE the parent changes (autoflush would otherwise flush the collision). Server-emitted links use the permalink (`mailrender.page_url(base, number)`; notify payloads carry `page_number`); export/backlinks parse paths and permalinks. SPA: `/pages/$spaceSlug/$` (splat), `/print/pages/$spaceSlug/$` (nothing may hang a literal after a path), `?pageId=` on the index resolves and REPLACES to the path; `lib/page-links.ts` (`pageLink`/`pagePermalink`/`pageHref`/`pagePrintHref`) is the ONE place a page URL is assembled — every former slug-built link moved onto it. `radd:include` takes `page` = a path (or number), `space:path` for another space. | `forms` addendum | **RADD-795 wave — the requester surfaces.** A request could be filed and then nothing more done with it. **RADD-796** adds `forms/requests.py`: a requester view of an issue scoped by RELATIONSHIP, never `item.read` — `visible_condition` (`reporter_id == actor OR team_id IN actor's teams`) is the ONE admission rule, shared by the list, `GET /portal/requests/{key}` and `POST …/comments`. **404, never 403**, on a request you cannot see: a refusal confirms the key names a real issue. The reply needed a seam, so `comments` grew **`create_authorized_comment`** — a NAMED function for callers that authorise by another rule, not a `bypass_authz` flag, and handed an EMPTY permission set so the internal-visibility check still refuses. Public comments filtered in the QUERY: the COUNT leaks as much as the list. Fixed a real determinism bug on the way — `created_at` is `func.now()` (TRANSACTION time), so same-transaction comments tie and `DISTINCT ON … ORDER BY created_at` picked arbitrarily (the importer writes many per transaction); the reply marker compares newest-by-others against newest-by-me STRICTLY, so a tie resolves conservatively. **RADD-797** — rows carry assignee (directory NAME), release, public comment count, `awaiting_requester`; one query, not 2N. **RADD-981** — `SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID` is in NEITHER bucket: an inbound-mail comment from a person with no account is SYSTEM-authored, so a requester emailing the desk lit their own row with "somebody answered you" pointing at the message they had just sent (an automation's public comment is an acknowledgement, not a reply, for the same reason `slas.evaluation` skips the id on the first-response timer). It is still COUNTED — a count that disagrees with the visible conversation is its own bug — and the module's `weak_depends` on `automations` is what carries the import. **RADD-798** — a per-form team picker (`team_picker_enabled`, migration `c1bfec45331b`, OFF by default) offering only the SUBMITTER's teams, writing `item.team_id`; membership re-checked server-side, and a form with the picker off refuses a team outright. **RADD-799** — `components/requests/` holds `ListSection` + `RequestRow` + `RequestPeek`, rendered by BOTH `/portal` and My Work; `components/forms/MyRequests.tsx` deleted. Cleanup emits `form.staging.deleted` per AREA so the spec-102 cascade does the work; `trigger=False`, so the 67-trigger parity oracle holds. Anonymous public-form uploads remain RADD-802. | `auth` addendum | **RADD-672:** `authz.require_anywhere(session, user, permission)` — the cross-project gate. Returns the per-project permission map for the projects where the atom holds; ForbiddenError only when it holds NOWHERE (globally included, so a member on a zero-project instance gets an empty map, not a 403). Exists because `require(permission)` with no project asks for the GLOBAL atom, which a spec-113 key scoped to one project never holds — so GET /projects, cross-project `list_items` (+ `bulk._visible_ids_query`) and the MCP read tools refused exactly the principals the spec-114 catalog was built for. Cross-project item listing now also constrains its query to the readable project ids UP FRONT, fixing pagination (LIMIT used to run before the visibility post-filter). **RADD-788 finished the job**: the ~25 remaining member-floor gates were NOT deliberate — they were vacuous. `item.read` sat in the hardcoded `MEMBER_FLOOR`, so the global check could not fail; RADD-773 made the floor an editable role and the first admin to empty it turned every one of them into a 403 for people whose access is project-scoped (the normal shape of a grant). `GET /views` was the one that decided the experience — specs 61–67 deleted the builtin board/list/planning pages, so a refused view list leaves a project containing nothing but Reports. Two named seams replace the pattern: **`authz.readable_projects(session, user)`** (memoised per request like the baseline; LIST endpoints return `[]` when it is empty) and **`authz.require_member(session, user)`** (same floor, raises — for single-resource reads, where emptiness is not an available answer). Callers with project-scoped rows filter to the returned ids. Converted: views (list/counts/card-presets/`_require_scope`/`transfer_ownership`), cycles, labels, roles+permissions catalog, teams, fields, dashboards, events, canned, work categories, timesheet, semantic search, AI NL→SLQ, SLQ suggest/validate, automations runnable. `views.PERSONAL_VIEW_PERMISSION`/`dashboards.PERSONAL_DASHBOARD_PERMISSION` named a scope that was wrong, so the latter is deleted rather than repointed. **SPA half:** `usePermissions().anyProject(atom)` + `useCan(atom, {anyProject: true})` mirror `require_anywhere` on the client — the seam that was MISSING, which is why all-projects gates reached for `global` (view drag-to-rank, ViewModal sharing, the sidebar New-view affordance). `/settings` redirects to Profile, not Fields — the index used to land every non-admin on a page gated by `field.manage`. `tests/test_scoped_member.py` walks the HTTP surface as a project-scoped member against an EMPTY Baseline and asserts each answer, so the class cannot return silently. |
| `groups`/`auth` addendum | **RADD-833 — the Groups admin screen, and the inspector shows the PATH.** The directory mirror, read-only (a Settings → Groups tab of its own until RADD-931 folded it into Settings → Directory, where the live browse already was, and gave each row the per-group role grants the standalone page never had) — name/dn, the TRANSITIVE member count beside the direct one (the number a grant resolves to; direct under-sells nested groups), nesting both directions by name (`groups.service.nesting_edges`, one query), and the spec-87 health chip ("missing in AD" — grants kept, removals held). **The inspector reports the CHAIN, not just the fact:** `grants.attributed_rows_for_user` splits every grant channel by its carrying subject (direct → team → group, most legible first), `permission_sources` channels carry `via="group"` + `via_group` + `group_path` — computed by `groups.service.membership_path` (BFS down the child edges, depth-limited, shortest chain wins; empty = direct member) — and the SPA renders "granted to group Studio ← VFX All ← Render Wranglers" with the hop count in the hover. Pinned by the three-level-chain test in `test_group_grants.py`. |
| `auth`/`teams`/`settings` addendum (RADD-927 — the settings & access cleanup wave) | **One grant model (RADD-929).** `project_members` and `project_teams` are DROPPED (migration `d929grants`, rows folded into `global_role_grants` as project-scoped grants; deduped with `WHERE NOT EXISTS`, not `ON CONFLICT` — the unique key includes a NULL `space_id` and Postgres treats NULLs as distinct, so `ON CONFLICT` silently inserts the duplicate). Three tables expressed one fact and `authz_core._granted_role_ids` unioned all three, so a team's entitlement on a project appeared twice in the Teams panel under two names. Now: subject (user\|team\|group) × role × scope (project\|space\|none), one table, one resolution path — the batch resolver `permissions_for_projects` lost two queries with it. Authorization is SCOPE-derived and already existed (RADD-826): `member.create`/`member.delete` on the project for project-scoped grants, global `role.update` for anything wider. **Endpoints gone**: `/projects/{id}/members` and `/projects/{id}/teams`; the read is `GET /role-grants?project_id=`, new beside the existing subject/space directions. `teams.delete_team`'s guard counts the team's project-scoped grants (it counted attachments, so a team entitled only by grant deleted silently). SPA: project → Access is ONE list with a user/team/**group** subject picker (group grants were fully implemented server-side since RADD-832 and unreachable from the UI — `roleGrantsQuery`'s `enabled` guard had dropped `groupId`, disabling that query permanently); the Teams panel's Projects section is deleted. **Settings declare where they live (RADD-930).** `SettingSpec.section` — a dotted path whose ROOT names the surface and whose tail names a card within it (`directory.connection`) — carried through `ScopedSettingRead`. Each surface renders `ScopedSettingsEditor section=…`; a General page renders the REMAINDER (`homed=…`), so a setting with a departed or misspelt section degrades to "appears on General" rather than "unreachable". This retires the hand-maintained key arrays (`DIRECTORY_*_KEYS`, `AI_FEATURE_SETTING_KEYS`) and the inline `key !== WORKFLOW_TRANSITION_MODE_KEY` exclude. Placements: release states → Releases, working week → Time logging, CSAT → SLAs, workflow mode → Workflow. **Plugin-owned settings tabs withdraw with their plugin (RADD-928).** `SettingsNavItem.plugin` names the owning `core=False` plugin and the layout gates on the capabilities manifest's `plugins` (a field that had existed since spec 93 and was read by nothing) via the new `usePluginEnabled` hook — disabling Forgejo used to leave a tab whose every fetch 404'd against the unmounted router. Settings → Plugins links each plugin to its tab, derived from the same table. **Two tabs retired.** Settings → Groups folds into Settings → Directory, which becomes a real declared tab and gains per-group role grants (RADD-931); Settings → Holidays folds into Settings → Time logging, renamed from "Work categories", which now holds every instance-scope time policy (RADD-932). Both old paths redirect. | `ai` addendum | **RADD-1273 — Radd states its request preferences; the provider's chat template is never edited for Radd's sake.** `ai_providers.reasoning` (bool, OFF for every new and every existing row — migration `d1273aireason`) and `ai_providers.request_params` (JSONB object). Qwen3-style templates think unless the request opts out, and the closed payload builder had no way to say so: on mark015 (sglang, `qwen3.8-27b`) every call reasoned first inside the same 1024-token budget the answer needs — a two-way structured choice spent ~1000 chars of reasoning and 2.6 s where 0.7 s does. `provider.finish_payload` is the last word on every chat payload (`complete`/`complete_structured`/`complete_choice`/`stream`/the Test probe; embeddings untouched): `with_reasoning` speaks the shape's dialect — OpenAI OFF sends `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking=false` (vLLM/sglang), ON sends nothing (the model's default IS "on"); Anthropic OFF is silence (the API default), ON adds `thinking` with `config.ai_reasoning_budget_tokens` and lifts `max_tokens` by it, except under a forced tool call, which the API refuses to combine — then `deep_merge` lays `request_params` LAST so an admin overrides anything Radd chose. `RESERVED_REQUEST_PARAMS` (`messages`, `stream`) are refused on write: they are the request, not a tuning of it. `ResolvedModel` carries both so no caller names them. Trade-off, stated in the form: api.openai.com rejects the unknown key, so an OpenAI-hosted provider sets reasoning ON; the Test button surfaces the 400 in one click. Settings → AI provider form: a Reasoning checkbox + "Extra request parameters (JSON)" textarea (client-side parsed, same reserved-key rule), a Reasoning column on the table. Tests: `test_ai.py` (composition), `test_ai_registry.py` (defaults + round trip). | ai | — | — |

| `items` (SLQ) addendum | **RADD-1176 — `ORDER BY state` / `ORDER BY category`.** Both were filterable but not sortable; a list could not be read in workflow order even though every state carries a `position` (the order states are dragged into on Settings → Workflow, which the board already follows). `state` sorts by `states.position` then `states.name` (the tiebreak for all-projects views, where workflows share positions); `category` by `state_categories.position`, then state position, then name. **Compiled as a JOIN, never a per-row subquery**: `order_clause` now returns a TUPLE of columns and `order_joins(terms)` the `(target, on-clause)` pairs the terms reach through; `CompiledQuery.joins` carries them and the two statement builders (`service/listing.py`, `service/scope.py`) apply `.join()` before ordering. Measured on the 503k-item seed, 101k-item project, 200-row page: created 54 ms, priority 53 ms, state-via-join 57 ms, category 73 ms, state-via-scalar-subquery 261 ms (the shape the test forbids by asserting no SELECT inside the order columns); all-projects priority 80 ms vs state 122 ms. No new index — expression sorts cannot use one and priority never had one. Multi-key sorts were already one top-N sort (`ORDER BY state, updated DESC`).
