Tests: `test_slq.py` (compile shape + an end-to-end workflow-order listing). | workflow (models, spine read) | — | — |
| `views` (SPA pager) addendum | **RADD-1177 — a personal page-size picker.** The list fetched a fixed 200 per page (50 on a phone, `ITEMS_PAGE_LIMIT*`) with no control. `Pager` gains optional `pageSize`/`pageSizes`/`onPageSize` (a `Select` after the total; other pagers unchanged); `useItemsPageLimit` returns `[limit, set]`, a stored choice (`ITEMS_PAGE_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY`, one of `ITEMS_PAGE_SIZES` = 25/50/100/200) winning over the viewport default — PERSONAL and per browser, like column widths, never the shared view. Picking a size snaps to page 1. The pager now also renders whenever the total exceeds the smallest size, otherwise the picker is unreachable exactly when someone wants a smaller page. The server cap stays 200, so no page costs more than today. Proof: `web/scripts/pager-size-proof.mjs` (60 rows: 200 → 25 refetches with `limit=25`, page 1 of 3, survives a reload, last page holds 10). | — | — | — |
| `mailintake`/`items` addendum | **RADD-982 — the lifecycle stops ending in silence, and RADD-984 — the channel becomes readable.** *The resolution notice*: `resolved.py` is `reply.py`'s sibling — on an `item.updated` that ENTERS the done category, every external `mail_contact` (RADD-980's plural seam, minus any address belonging to an active user) is mailed a rendered, threaded notice naming the state it landed in, from the source-bound sender. **Entering, not being**: the guard resolves the diff's FROM state name against `workflow.list_states` (the new declared `depends_on`), so a done→done move — spec 112's release sweep performs one on every shipped item — announces nothing, where csat's `moved_to_done` passes and is saved only by its unique survey row. There is deliberately no such row here: a reopen→re-resolve IS a new resolution and announces again. **It yields to CSAT** through `csat.service.announces_resolution`, reached deferred + feature-detected (`weak_depends=("ai", "csat")`; csat `depends_on` mailintake, so the edge only runs this way round and an uninstalled csat answers by absence) — the survey's own first line announces the resolution, so one message goes out, not two. New scalar setting **`mail_send_resolved`** (bool, instance+project, `section="email"`, default ON via `config.mail_send_resolved` — unlike CSAT's opt-in, because a desk that must be configured before it says "done" says it on no instance at all). **One consumer, two messages**: `outbound._plan` dispatches `comment.created` → `reply`, `item.updated` → `resolved`, and `_deliver` reads an `OutboundPlan` Protocol (`item_id`/`subject`/`comment_id`/`pin_subject`/`render(recipient)`) off either — the shared `outbound_configured` gate and at-most-once cursor are why a second consumer would have been a second copy. `mailrender.resolution` + `acknowledgement` now share `_contact_notice`, which also **put the `reason` footer in the TEXT half** (it was html-only — the drift "both parts, always" exists to prevent, invisible because the one caller passed no reason). New instance surface: Settings → Email → **Automatic messages** (`AutomaticMessagesPanel`) — the `email` section is claimed in `INSTANCE_HOMED_SECTIONS` and the page rendered only the bespoke ack card, so a second email row would have been editable NOWHERE; it renders the section generically minus `mail_ack_body`. Project scope needs no surface — `email` is not in `PROJECT_HOMED_SECTIONS`, so the override lands on the project's General page. *Mail in the History feed (RADD-984)*: `mail.received`/`.sent`/`.failed` join `items.history.RELATED_EVENT_TYPES` (they emit under `entity_type=mail_intake` with the item as a SUBJECT, so a related-type entry is the only way in; `mail.dropped` names no item and stays out), with `recipients`/`recipient_count`/`sender`/`error`/`given_up` lifted into `_DETAIL_KEYS`. `HistoryTab` renders received/sent quietly and a FAILURE loud (`text-status-danger-ink` verb + the relay's own error, and whether the ladder gave up) — before this, `mail.failed` was emitted per message and read by nothing, so "the customer never got your reply" existed only as a row in the events table. Tests: `tests/test_mail_resolved.py` (18), `tests/test_mail_events.py` (+3, mutation-checked). |
| `workflow`/`views` addendum | **RADD-854 — THE consolidation: categories become user-owned rows; state groups fold in and disappear.** Supersedes RADD-851's fixed-tier axis semantics and DELETES RADD-852's state groups (unreleased — schema, API, axis, UI all gone; `state_group` as an axis token now REJECTS). `state_categories` rows: `key` (immutable slug), `name`, `color`, `position`, `behaves_as` (the fixed `StateCategory` enum — REQUIRED), `is_builtin`; the six builtins seeded as EDITABLE rows whose keys coincide with the enum values (migration `d854catrows` — `states.category_key` backfills straight from `states.category`, and pre-854 `StateCreate.category` payloads stay valid). **The trick that left all 21 semantic call sites untouched:** `states.category` remains a DERIVED-but-stored enum — assignment copies the row's `behaves_as` into it, and editing a custom row's `behaves_as` ripples one UPDATE over its states. `/state-categories` CRUD (reads member, writes instance admin; builtins rename/recolour/reorder but keep behaviour and never delete; custom rows delete only when unreferenced). The `state_category` axis buckets by the VOCABULARY rows (position order, membership via `states.category_key`, semantic fallback when rows are absent); drops resolve to the item's own project's first state under the row. Settings → Workflow states: the Categories card (name/colour/behaves_as/reorder/delete) replaces the groups card; the per-state select lists the vocabulary. `tests/test_state_categories.py` (5, replacing test_state_groups.py). |
| `workflow`/`items` addendum | **RADD-853 — states delete with a SUCCESSOR, and the category is editable in place.** `DELETE /states/{id}?reassign_to=<state_id>` (the spec-89 delete-with-successor precedent): items move to the successor first through the new public items seam `items.reassign_state(from, to, actor)` — a deliberate ADMIN REPAIR that bypasses transition guards (a guard refusing half the items would strand the deletion mid-way; the successor is explicit) while emitting an ordinary `item.updated` per item with a before snapshot, so history, watchers, search and reports see the move honestly. Successor must be same-project, not self; without `reassign_to` the spec-87 in-use refusal stands, and the default-state refusal is untouched. `StateUpdate.category` re-classifies the state's items for every category consumer from that moment on (the admin's call, like renaming). UI: a per-row category select, and a delete dialog that counts the items (`GET /items/count` — `{total}`, a wire shape the first draft misread as `{count}`, caught by the CDP run's forever-'Counting…' text) and offers the successor, defaulting to the project default. `tests/test_state_groups.py` grows the delete/category pin. |
| `workflow`/`views` addendum | **RADD-852 — state GROUPS: arbitrary vocabulary with no semantics (Hussein's design call, superseding the behaves_as proposal).** A `state_groups` row (name unique, colour, position; migration `d852stategrp`) is a pure PRESENTATION tier: `states.group_id` (SET NULL on delete — members degrade to ungrouped, never block) while the state KEEPS its fixed category, so all 21 semantic consumers (throughput, sweep, rollover, guards, CFD, SLQ category…) are untouched by whatever vocabulary an instance invents — groups may even span categories. Instance-wide (cross-project boards group by them); reads ride membership, writes are instance-admin (the linktypes precedent). `ViewAxis.STATE_GROUP` buckets by group position + a trailing Ungrouped everywhere `groupItemsForView` feeds; a drop transitions to the item's own project's first state in the group (Ungrouped is not a target — 'no group' names no state). Settings → Workflow states gains the groups card (add/colour/reorder/delete) + a per-state group picker; `StateUpdate.group_id` is tri-state (absent = untouched, null = leave). `tests/test_state_groups.py` (3). |
| `ldap`/`settings` addendum | **RADD-848 — the revocation window is visible, editable, and skippable.** `ldap_group_sync_seconds` joins the instance settings (env default 3600s; the PeriodicLoop's interval lambda reads a module value refreshed from the cascade each tick, so an edit applies from the next cycle). The loop body extracted to `groupsync.run_group_sync(session)` and exposed as `POST /ldap/sync/groups` (instance admin, bind-gated, recorded in sync-status) with a **Sync groups now** button in the Directory page's Groups section — an AD membership removal stops granting on that click instead of within the interval. The semantics stay RADD-831's: mirrored groups hold AD's TRANSITIVE member answer, so nested-group removals propagate without the nested group being mirrored; the login probe updates the signing-in user sooner. |
| `ldap`/`settings` addendum | **RADD-846 — the LDAP connection is editable (env becomes seed-only, the spec-110 rule applied to the directory).** Five new INSTANCE settings — `ldap_url`, `ldap_user_domain`, `ldap_bind_dn`, `ldap_bind_password` (SettingSpec gains `secret: bool` → masked input; the value stays admin-readable, the recorded decision), `ldap_admin_groups` — each keyed to its `config.Settings` attribute so RADD_LDAP_* env keeps working as the default. The ldap service reads a module-level RESOLVED overlay (`LdapConn` + `refresh_conn(session)`), refreshed at every session-bearing boundary: login, the two sync ticks (whose PeriodicLoop gates dropped their env bind-check — a gate reading env would never wake a loop whose bind account arrived via the DB), every directory route (`require_bind_account` is async now and refreshes first), sync-status, and a `warm()` startup hook so the capability manifest is right at boot. Settings → Directory gains a Connection section (deliberately NOT gated on readiness — configuring the connection is what an unready instance needs). Found via a dev restart that silently lost `server/.env` (CWD-relative env_file). `tests/test_ldap.py` pins the overlay seam + the cascade override. |
| `forms`/`mailintake`/`auth` addendum | **RADD-828 — requesters replace the anonymous surface.** The tokened `/public/forms/{token}` path (spec 62 render/submit + the spec-74 deflect) is DELETED — `public_router.py`/`public.py` gone, `allow_public` no longer mints tokens (inert column, dropped later), `trimmed_read` moved into `portal.py` (its only surviving caller). In its place mail ingest PROVISIONS: an unknown sender becomes a `UserSource.EMAIL` account (`ensure_imported_user`) set as reporter — it cannot log in (`create_session` refuses at the one seam every login path mints through: mail is its interface, the contact row + acks stay) until an SSO login by the same verified email CLAIMS it (`user.source` → OIDC, keeping its tickets). Its permission floor is the seeded **Requester** role (Q1: `item.read@own` + comment.write + attachment.create — their own tickets and nothing else), never the Baseline, via `authz.floor_permissions` — so enabling mail ingest can never hand strangers the operator's staff policy row; an explicit role grant still upgrades a known customer. The authenticated portal (spec 73) and `/public/pages` + `/public/csat` (D9) stay. `tests/test_requesters.py` (6). |
| `access` addendum | **RADD-819 — deny precedence.** `access_grants.effect` (allow \| deny; migration `d819deny`, server_default allow — opt-in and BYTE-IDENTICAL until a deny row is written). The rule, written down in the semantics doc: **specificity first, deny on ties** — equal scope: deny beats allow; a project-scoped row beats a global row regardless of effect (a project deny kills a global allow AND a project allow carves back a global deny). One deny row expresses "this team may not write Priority" with nobody else's access touched: denies never flip a default-open resource into restricted for non-matching subjects (`restricted_accesses` counts allow rows only). A deny binds its EXACT access; on an implied route (write⇒read) it blocks that route without closing the check sideways. The instance-admin back door stays at the resolvers' CALLERS (the RADD-816 rule), never in the algebra. Inspector rows + the grants editor carry `effect` (a red DENY chip; the add row offers may/may-NOT), so a refusal names the denying grant. Pure precedence pinned in `tests/test_deny_precedence.py` (7). |
| `auth` addendum | **RADD-816 — every verb means one thing.** ELEVEN dead atoms disposed: the nine never-enforced manage umbrellas (view/cycle/label/release/canned/cardpreset/sla/team/role — granting one was exactly ticking its triple) are DELETED, stored roles + token scopes rewritten to the triples by migration `d816verbs` (no alias — the no-backcompat rule); `attachment.delete` (advertised "your own", enforced nothing) is now `attachment.delete@own` in storage and the unqualified atom means ANYONE's, riding project.manage; `service_account.delete` (no route) dropped. **Q4:** the hardcoded author-own checks are gone — `comment.delete@own` / `worklog.delete@own` / `attachment.delete@own` are Baseline-seeded grants resolved through the relation machinery (comments/timelogging/attachments each register an `own` RelationSpec), inspector-explainable and REVOCABLE (`tests/test_own_deletes.py` pins both directions). **F6:** six catalog reads become deliverable atoms (`label/cycle/canned/team/role/cardpreset.read`), Baseline-seeded so day-one behaviour is the old member floor, enforced at their list/single-read gates (`sla.read` deliberately NOT minted — its list rides the project's item.read, and an unenforced atom is the dead class this change deletes; the permission catalog stays on the floor — vocabulary, not data). **F5.2:** `has_manage` stopped being a framework bypass — `resolution.has_access` never consults it; the field contexts' flag now carries the INSTANCE-ADMIN fact (the operator who administers grants sees through them — a named fields-layer rule, already explained by the inspector's `*` row), pages/attachments moved their manager/uploader rules to named call-site short-circuits, and a project.manage holder is denied like anyone else unless a grant names them (the flipped tests in test_field_grants/rules/writability pin it). SPA: 9 dead constants removed, 6 read constants added, 16 gate sites remapped to the CRUD atoms. |
| `auth`/shell addendum | **RADD-843 — the nav shows only what is useful to the actor.** One predicate source: `useNavFacts()` (`web/src/lib/nav-facts.ts`), consumed by the Sidebar, the collapsed rail, the command palette Goto rows and the pins-bar link-pin filter — four surfaces, one truth (the rail used to gate NOTHING, the parallel-code-path class). Facts derive from lists the shell already loads (projects/spaces/dashboards — zero new requests) plus two server booleans on `GET /auth/me` (`MeRead.nav`): `timesheet` (defined ONCE beside the timesheet's authz — `timelogging.nav_timesheet_visible`: timesheet.view anywhere ∨ a readable project with logging enabled ∨ own worklog rows; access ∧ usefulness, never a flag alone) and `portal` (the `list_portal_forms` computation, non-empty). Every unknown fails OPEN — hiding is presentation, enforcement stays `authz.require`, direct URLs still refuse properly. Also: Dashboards section renders only when non-empty ∨ can-create, New-dashboard gates on the create atom, plugin-nav `requires` goes through the scope-aware seam (global ∨ anyProject — the RADD-810 class), and a link pin to a hidden area drops from RENDER but stays in prefs (access can return — the view-pin rule). The client scope audit caught this change's own first bug: `page.manage` checked globally (it is SPACE-scoped) — `anySpace` now. |
| `items` addendum | **RADD-842 — per-item writability for the SPA.** Relations broke a shipped contract: `readonly_field_keys` is documented per-(actor, project) and item-INDEPENDENT (the spec-96 seam), but `item.update@own` makes updatability per-ROW. Every API-bound `ItemRead` (listing + detail; never event payloads — a stream consumer has no actor) now carries `capabilities: {can_update, can_transition, can_comment}` — the actor's per-row verdict, stamped by `attach_capabilities` (pure per row once the memoised relation actor resolves; can_transition = can_update since state rides the update path). SPA: `useItemWritability(project, item?)` — the server's verdict OVERRIDES the project-level answer when present (a false means THIS issue is not theirs), absent falls back; the denial reason names the relation ("limited to your own or your team's issues"). item-detail + IssueProperties pass the row. Bulk surfaces keep the spec-68 skip-and-report contract (per-row refusals are enforced server-side and summarized — the bar holds ids, not rows). |
| `items` addendum | **RADD-817 — items ENFORCE relations** (the first adopter; own/assigned/team registered, `assigned` off-chain — incomparable with team/own, covered only by @any). Two primitives in `service/visibility.py`: `relation_read_clause(session, actor, permissions_by_project)` — the FILTERING form, per-project arms (relations are per-project facts), None in the @any-everywhere common case (two set lookups per project, no actor resolution; list p50 on the 503k DB stayed ~108ms) — and `ensure_item_relation(...)` — the GATING form; a failed READ 404s (existence stays private), a failed write 403s naming the qualifier. Wired: the three shared builders (`listing.py`, `bulk._visible_ids_query`, `read.get_item`) + the seam itself (`require_readable_item` — every child surface inherits), the write gates (update/archive/delete/reorder), and the independents by name — views/counts badges, rollup (roots AND every frontier level), link typeahead, SLQ suggest_values (`SuggestScope.relation_clause`), deflect, the ai similar pool (post-filter at materialization), semantic Ask, `/search` (a mirror-side `_relation_index_clause` over the RADD-841 columns — the ONE restatement, documented), the kernel entity list hook (query hook for plugin relations), and `notify/consumer._allowed` (the delivery choke point gates per RECIPIENT — a relation-scoped reader is never notified about a row their list would hide). `authz.holds_base(perms, atom)` is the new membership test (a qualified form HOLDS the base); `require`/`require_anywhere`/`holds`/`readable_projects` use it, and the eight raw `Permission.ITEM_READ in perms` sites (workflow router, listing post-filter, timelogging, views, slas, dashboards ×2, pages links) converted — the raw test silently drops relation-qualified holders. `tests/test_item_relations.py` (5: the Done-when scenario across list/count/read/seam/search/notify + the @team⊃@own chain + the @own write refusal). |
| `kernel`/`auth`/`items` addendum | **RADD-823 — relations: a kernel mechanism for "mine / my team's / anyone's".** `resource.action@relation` is the normative syntax; an UNQUALIFIED atom means `@any`, so every existing role keeps exactly what it had (zero backfill). Two orthogonal axes: SCOPE rides the grant (RADD-814's ladder), RELATION rides the atom — the lattice is a chain `any ⊃ team ⊃ own` (normative: the resolvers close DOWNWARD, so `@team` covers the `@own` rows). Kernel: `RelationSpec(resource, key, label, where, holds)` — BOTH forms mandatory (`where` keeps lists/counts/search honest; `holds` gates a loaded row; a disagreeing pair is a silent leak, pinned by the contract test) — registered via the plugin manifest (`relations=` on RaddPlugin; import-time `register_relation` too, but the manifest is what survives the loader's clear() — the cascades precedent) + `RelationActor` (ids only: user_id + the RADD-830 team closure). Items registers `own` (D6: REPORTER — survives imports, reassignable) and `team` (D13: `item.team_id`, nothing inferred) in `service/visibility.py`. Auth: `split/base/qualify_permission`, `relation_contains`/`relation_meet`/`relations_held` (types), `relation_actor`/`relation_filter` (None=@any \| OR of covered specs \| `false()` fails closed)/`relation_holds_row` (authz); scope/description/checkable_at lookups resolve the BASE (a qualifier never changes WHERE an atom is checked); role validation accepts `base@relation` only when the relation is REGISTERED for the base's resource. **THE item-resolution seam**: `items.service.require_readable_item(session, item_id, actor) → (item, project, permissions)` — the ~15 inline require_item+require copies converted (weblinks, vcs, history, csat, transitions, slas, participants, watchers, canned, mailintake, pages-links, worklog router, approvals, comments+attachments parent bindings), so when RADD-817 lands relation gating HERE, every child surface inherits it by construction. `forms/requests.visible_condition` now COMPOSES the registered relations (own ∪ team) — the registry's first consumer instead of a copy. Spec-113 token scopes are lattice-aware: `_lattice_intersect` meets per-base at the narrower relation (`item.read` key ∩ `item.read@team` account = `@team`); `_atoms` accepts qualified atoms (base validated). Normative table: `docs/specs/115-relations-semantics.md`; executed by `tests/test_relation_semantics.py` (9: algebra properties, the token-scope table, downward closure, fails-closed, the where/holds contract on a DB fixture). |
| `auth`/`kernel` addendum | **RADD-1040 — the generated-CRUD row filter says what it cannot do.** `relation_holds_row` (the SYNC gate `EntityHost` uses to filter a kernel-generated CRUD listing) treats a query-gated relation — `holds=None`, membership living in ANOTHER table, the `@participant` shape — as NOT held. That default is correct (failing closed is never a leak), but it is also silent: a plugin registering an expensive relation on its own auto-wired `EntitySpec` entity gets rows that quietly vanish for exactly the actors the relation exists to admit, indistinguishable from the outside from "the feature doesn't work". `auth/entityhost.py::_warn_query_gated` logs ONCE per (entity, relation) pair per process — scoped to relations this actor's permissions would actually have needed, so an instance whose plugins register no expensive relations stays silent — naming what got dropped and why, the rows stay hidden either way. `search/service.py` restates the same rule one module over for its own `_unmirrorable_warned` set (RADD-1030, above). `tests/test_entity_host_relations.py`. |
| `auth`/`projects` addendum | **RADD-1034 — the member-floor directory stops handing out mail-provisioned strangers.** `mailintake._sender_user` (RADD-828) provisions an ACTIVE `UserSource.EMAIL` account for every unrecognized sender, so with no filter one forged message made "Stranger <stranger@evil.example>" pickable by every authenticated user in any assignee/mention/participant picker that calls `/users/directory`. `sources_excluded` is a SEPARATE knob from the existing `source=` filter — the latter is an admin exact-match query param (Settings → Users), the former is a caller-side exclusion set the directory applies by DEFAULT (excludes `UserSource.EMAIL`); `include_requesters=true` opts back in for the surfaces that need to see them (Settings → Users itself, the MCP `list_users` tool, the Jira importer's email resolution). `tests/test_user_directory.py`. **RADD-1041 — `GET /projects` explains itself without changing what it returns.** `visible_projects` already unioned entitled (a real grant) and relation-derived (item.read@own/@team + an actual own/team item in the project) readability — RADD-937's resolution — but the two looked identical on the wire, so the sidebar had no way to separate "your projects" from "projects you happen to have one ticket in". `ProjectRead.via` (`ProjectVia` "entitled"\|"related") is PRESENTATION-ONLY metadata threaded through the same resolution — it explains a key already in the map, never decides whether the key is there, and dropping/hiding it can never change which projects a caller sees. A new per-user preference (`users.preferences["sidebar.related_projects"]`) lets the sidebar hide the "related" half from its OWN project list without the server hiding anything — the security decision (`visible_projects`) and the display choice (`via`) stay strictly separate. |
| `access`/`auth` addendum | **RADD-832 — groups are grant subjects, on BOTH grant tables.** `GrantSubject.GROUP` joins user/team/role: `access_grants` needed no schema change (subject columns are polymorphic; `_validate_subject` gained the arm), `global_role_grants` gained `group_id` + the `one_subject` CHECK widened to exactly-one-of-three (`num_nonnulls`, migration `d832grpsubj`). Matching is the TRANSITIVE closure everywhere — `subject_matches` reads `SubjectContext.group_ids` (already required by RADD-830), `auth/grants._subject_condition` ORs `group_id IN user_group_ids(...)` so every scoped-resolution path (global/project/space, batched included) inherits groups from ONE seam, and views/dashboards `_grant_level` grew the third arm so a view shared with a parent group is visible to a nested child's member. `grants.held_role_ids_anywhere` replaces the direct-only grant channel in `all_held_role_ids` — which also fixes a pre-existing gap where TEAM-held role grants never reached the inspector's subject set. The registry DEFAULT subjects tuple carries GROUP, so custom/builtin fields offer groups with no edit; views, dashboards, attachments and pages opted in explicitly. **The guardrail the issue called non-optional**: `GET /groups/{id}/reach` (the RADD-830 downward closure) renders as `GroupReachHint` — "resolves to N people (nesting included)" — beside every picker the moment a group is chosen, BEFORE the grant saves. SPA: SubjectPicker/AccessGrantsEditor/ViewSharingEditor/RoleGlobalGrants/RoleGrantsSection all offer groups; view/dashboard share rows render a group ref. `tests/test_group_grants.py` (8). |
| `auth`/`access`/`projects` addendum | **Spec 121 — the world is a principal (RADD-1142/1144).** Two seeded `users` rows with fixed ids and `UserSource.PRINCIPAL` (`auth/principals.py`: **Anyone** `…000000a4104e`, **Signed-in users** `…0000005160ed`; `ensure_principals` at startup/seed, migration `d1142princ`) are grant SUBJECTS: `grants._subject_condition` matches `user_id IN subject_user_ids(actor)` (Anyone for every actor, Signed-in users for every real account) and `access.resolution.SubjectContext.subject_user_ids` does the same for access grants — the only two places the world exists. Their floor is EMPTY (`floor_permissions`), they cannot log in (`create_session`), are refused as assignee/reporter (`principals.require_person`), never mailed (`notify.mailable_user`), and are excluded from every person directory/picker via `NON_PERSON_SOURCES` (= EMAIL + PRINCIPAL). **A public project is a row**: the seeded **Public** role (`item.read@public` + label/cycle/team reads) granted to Anyone; **contributions** are the seeded **Contributor** role (`item.create`, `item.update@own`, `comment.write`, `attachment.create`) granted to Signed-in users — `auth/public_access.py` reads/writes them as two switches, `PUT /projects/{id}/public-access` (project.manage + D14 coverage) is the API, `ProjectRead.public/contributions` are DERIVED from the rows, `/instance` carries the principal ids so the SPA can share a view with "Anyone on the web" (an ordinary viewer grant to the Anyone subject). **The anonymous actor**: `auth.deps.actor` / `Actor` resolves like `optional_user` and returns the Anyone row when there is no credential, refusing any non-read method at the seam (the RADD-836 rule); `/auth/me` answers 200 `anonymous=True` for it. Only the read routes a public project needs declare `Actor` — `tests/test_anonymous_surface.py` freezes that inventory in both directions. `visible_projects` counts a ROW-PROPERTY relation (`@public`, `RelationSpec.row_property`) as entitled, so public projects appear in everyone's rail. |
| `kernel`/`auth`/`items`/`search` addendum | **Spec 121 — issue visibility (RADD-1143).** `work_items.visibility` (`ItemVisibility`: `public` \| `internal` \| `restricted`; migration `d1143vis`; default from the scoped setting `item_default_visibility`, instance → project, choices-validated) with ONE kernel mechanism behind it: a **row guard** (`RowGuardSpec`, `registries.row_guards`, `row_guards=` on the manifest) is a per-row admission every reader of a resource passes whatever relation they hold — `open_where`/`open_holds` name the rows open to everyone, `admits` names the relation KEYS that admit a reader to the rest (items: `visibility != restricted`, admits `own`/`assigned`/`participant`, resolved through the relation registry so an unloaded participants plugin simply does not admit). `authz.relation_filter` ANDs the guard under every answer and answers it ALONE for `@any` (so `@any` stops meaning "every row"), `relation_holds_row`/`_async`/`relation_row_ids_holding` apply it first; `RelationActor.unrestricted` (instance admin, set by `relation_actor`) is the one bypass (D1 — project.manage does not). `items.service.visibility` stops short-circuiting `@any` in `relation_read_clause`/`ensure_item_relation`; `notify.consumer._allowed` likewise. `@public` is a new item relation (`row_property=True`). `search_index.visibility` mirrors the column (indexer payload + `sync_relation_columns`); `public` joins the mirrored relation set and `_guard_index_clause` compiles the guard over the mirror. SLQ field `visibility` (=, !=, IN); `ItemUpdate.visibility` refuses a change that would hide the issue from the actor (`_refuse_self_lockout`, admins excepted); `test_item_visibility.py` is the six-actor matrix, `test_bulk_move_guards`/`test_form_defaults` record why it is not a field rule and not a form default. |

The API client (`lib/api.ts`) treats a 401 as an ordinary refusal while anonymous (no bounce) and otherwise redirects with `next`; the plugin SDK's client reads the same fact from `globalThis.__RADD_ANONYMOUS__`. Everything personal keys on `useIsAuthenticated()`: the inbox bell + rail badges, pins/saved filters (`/auth/me/preferences`), the leave poll, plugin preference sync, dashboards + page-space directories/summaries, the AI status probe, timelog batches, the realtime socket (`RealtimeBridge`, mounted only for an account — the server closes an unauthenticated socket). `useNavFacts` fails CLOSED for a visitor (projects only). `useAnonymousBounce(when)` sends a visitor who meets a not-found/refused page to sign in carrying the page (issue, view, project); `login.tsx` honours a same-origin `next`, and the SSO flow cookie carries it through `GET /auth/oidc/login?next=` → the callback (`sso.service.safe_next_path`). Proof: `web/scripts/public-project-proof.mjs` (admin seeds a public + a private project, three visibility levels, two comments, a fresh account; the world and the account are walked; every probe validated against the admin first). |

| `auth` addendum | **Merging is transfer + DELETE, and it carries the role.** `merge_users` repoints every reference to the survivor, takes the HIGHER `instance_role` (folding an admin into a member silently demoted the person, and if that was the only admin the instance had no way back in short of raw SQL), then **deletes** the source row instead of deactivating it — everything it owned has moved, so a dead duplicate of the same person in every picker bought nothing. It is deliberately NOT `delete_user`: that destroys worklogs first, on spec 89's principle that nobody should be credited with hours they didn't work, which protects a reassignment to a DIFFERENT person. A merge asserts one person, so their hours follow them. The `user.deleted` event carries the removed email/name, so the trail survives the row. New public seam `ensure_imported_user(email, name, source)` — find-or-create a password-less ACTIVE account for someone an importer named; `UserSource.JIRA` marks them. `tests/test_user_merge.py`. |

| `backup` | **Spec 99: scheduled + on-demand backups, encrypted at rest.** The ENGINE is core, not this module — `radd/backup/` (crypto/artifact/postgres/store/service + `python -m radd.backup` CLI) has no FastAPI, no plugin, no ORM import, because restoring a fresh deployment happens on an EMPTY database where no plugin registry can load. This module owns only what the CLI does not need: `backup_schedules` + `backup_runs`, the router, and the event types. **Artifacts are NOT tabled** — a restore rewrites the database, so a table listing backups would roll its own inventory back and erase the record of the safety backup taken seconds earlier; the DIRECTORY is the inventory, listed by reading each file's plaintext manifest header. Artifact = `radd-<ts>-<id>.radd`: plaintext header (manifest, so listing needs no key) + AES-256-GCM chunked payload (a tar of `pg_dump -Fc` output + attachments) + footer (plaintext size/sha256). Each chunk's AAD binds `<manifest sha256>:<index>:<is-final>`, so reordering, truncation and splicing between artifacts all fail closed. Key: `RADD_BACKUP_KEY_FILE`, generated 0600 on first use, deliberately on a DIFFERENT volume from the backups. `pg_dump`/`pg_restore` are a RUNTIME REQUIREMENT (startup pre-flight refuses to boot; `RADD_BACKUP_TOOLS_OPTIONAL` downgrades to a warning) and credentials reach them as libpq env vars, never argv. A **nightly schedule is seeded on first boot** — a backup system that must be configured protects the installs that needed it least. Restore: compatibility + key gate -> safety backup -> maintenance mode -> terminate backends + dispose the pool -> `DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE` (not `--clean`, which only drops what the dump contains) -> `pg_restore --single-transaction` -> `alembic upgrade head` -> attachments extracted OVER the tree; rolls back to the safety backup on failure and stays 503 if that fails too. Instance admin only (download = exfiltration, upload = arbitrary SQL). Event types are `trigger=False`: infrastructure events belong in the audit trail, not the automation dropdown. `docs/specs/99-*.md`. | auth, events | `backup.created/.deleted/.uploaded/.restored`, `backup_schedule.created/.updated/.deleted` | `radd/schedule.py` (promoted from `automations/schedule.py`; the schedule form is the SHARED `ScheduleEditor`, so backups gained monthly + cron with no backup-side code — RADD-912), `radd/maintenance.py` |

Repeated normalized attribution emails share one real or dry-run user. **Spec 90 — live Jira import wizard.** Jira Server/DC REST v2 client (`client.py`, PAT Bearer OR basic auth — PAT wins when set; `RADD_JIRA_BASE_URL`/`_PAT`|`_USER`+`_PASSWORD`/`_VERIFY_SSL`/`_PAGE_SIZE`). Discovery: `GET /jira/status` (configured/live/auth_mode, never 500s), `GET /jira/projects`, `POST /jira/preview {jql,sample_size}` → inferred inbound schema (`inference.py`, PURE — catalog type beats sample cardinality; per field: populate rate, distinct/dominant, `is_builtin`/`likely_noise` [constant defaults, ordering keys, devstatus blob]). Mapping: `jira_import_plans` (project+JQL+target+per-field mappings; `mapping.py` PURE suggest+validate — ignore/map/create/builtin), `POST /jira/plans/suggest` (pre-filled grid), `/validate`, CRUD. Import: `jira_import_runs` IS the progress bar — `runner.py` calls Radd services directly (no HTTP), stages FIELDS→ISSUES→LINKS, commits per page; `POST /jira/runs {plan_id}` snapshots the plan + fires an in-process task, `mark_interrupted` (on_startup) fails runs left mid-flight. `issuemap.py` (PURE) decodes Jira issue JSON → item draft (obfuscated emails, status categories, priority/kind, sprint beans, per-type value render + select-option filter, preserved Jira ID 1:1 + timestamps). Parents resolve in a second pass; the **LINKS stage relinks** each Jira issue link to a NATIVE Radd link when both ends are imported (remapping Jira key→Radd item key, cross-project included) else a WEB-LINK fallback to the Jira issue — the 'relink to new issues not old Jira' the user asked for. **Epic/link resolution robustness (spec 90 follow-up):** both passes resolve a target against the WHOLE Radd DB (`_resolve_item_key` = this run's `key_to_id` → else `find_item_by_key(_radd_key(...))`), so an Epic Link or issue-link target that lives in a PRIOR import or another project still resolves instead of being dropped; an unresolvable epic/link is preserved as a WEB LINK on the imported end (`parent_web_links`/`web_links`) rather than silently lost; a parent whose kinds don't fit Radd's stricter `epic←issue←subtask` hierarchy falls back to a web link too (`parents_incompatible`). Issue links carry **direction** (`LinkDraft.inward`): an INWARD Jira link ("this is blocked by X") is stored X→this so directional types (blocks/duplicates) keep the correct direction. Granular mapping (spec 90 follow-up): `FieldAction.NATIVE` + `BuiltinTarget` routes a field into a native concept (team/status/watchers/parent/assignee/labels/cycle/priority/dates); `value_map` does per-VALUE translation (team name `L1`→`Tier 1` create-on-the-fly, status `In Review`→a find-or-created state with the Jira category, renamed select options); a CREATE carries a display label (`create_name`) + scope (`create_scope` global|project — project fields resolved after the project). Canonical Jira-markup converter moved here (`markup.py`); `scripts/jira_markup.py` re-exports it. Instance-admin only (409 when unconfigured). SPA: `Settings → Import from Jira` stepper. **Re-import upsert + full-sprint cycles + redo (spec 90 follow-up):** `_create_issue` is now an UPSERT — `find_item_by_key(radd_key)` first; an existing item is field-REFRESHED via `update_item` (title/desc/state/priority + resolved assignee/reporter/team/cycle/dates/labels/custom_fields, never clearing an unresolved relation; comments/worklogs SKIPPED so a re-run never duplicates — counted `issues_updated` vs `issues`). Sprint→cycle imports the FULL bean (`issuemap.SprintDraft`: name+state+start/end+completeDate): EVERY sprint the issue was in becomes a cycle (previous/closed ones too), each carrying dates + a `completed_at` stamp for CLOSED sprints so the **derived status is right** (a closed sprint → a completed cycle, not a dateless draft); the item's `cycle_id` = the last (current) sprint. `ImportRunRead.field_mappings` exposes the run's mapping snapshot so the SPA **Redo** action (RunsList → Wizard) re-previews the JQL, overlays the saved choices on fresh suggestions, and reopens the mapping step editable; the wizard upserts the plan by name (create-then-409 → PATCH) so a redo/second-run reuses the plan. | auth, projects, fields, items, workflow, comments, cycles, weblinks, timelogging (weak: automations — `apply.py` wraps each item create in `intake.suppressed()`, spec 119: an import is history, not intake) | — | `on_startup` `runner.mark_interrupted` | **Spec 100 — the cache-first rebuild, in progress.** **(1) Connections are DB rows, not env vars** (`jira_connections` + `connections.py`): name/base_url/auth_mode/username/credential/verify_ssl/is_default/source, full CRUD + a `POST /jira/connections/{id}/test` that never 500s, exactly-one-default invariant, credential NEVER returned (`has_credential` instead), and `seed_from_env` (an `on_startup` hook) turning an existing `RADD_JIRA_*` deploy into a row ONCE — a seeded row is ordinary and editable, and is never resurrected after deletion. `client.py` is now a `JiraClient` bound to one `JiraCreds` value object (never the ORM row — REST calls run in worker threads), holding ONE http connection open instead of a fresh TLS handshake per call. **(2) Snapshots** (`jira_snapshots` / `_issues` / `_blobs` + `snapshot/`): a JQL result set is downloaded ONCE into raw JSONB (`download.py`, stages CATALOGS→ISSUES→COMMENTS→WORKLOGS→HISTORY→ATTACHMENTS, the row IS the progress bar, cooperative cancel, `mark_interrupted` on startup) and everything downstream reads the cache. **Fixes real data loss:** `/search` inlines only the first page of comments/worklogs per issue and reports the true `total` — spec 90 took the inline list at face value, so an issue with 87 comments imported 20; any short container is now backfilled from `/issue/{key}/comment|worklog|changelog` (verified live: 15 worklogs on 2 issues recovered in a 126-issue slice of DEV). Attachment binaries go to the attachment store with `jira_snapshot_blobs` as the index, so DELETE genuinely reclaims the space; `payload_bytes` uses `pg_column_size` (the compressed size) so the UI quotes an honest number. Structured `problems` (kind/message/subject/detail) replace `errors: string[]` — grouped by cause in the UI with every affected issue listed and copyable. **(3) De-hardcoded identification** (`schemakeys.py`): Jira's `/field` carries `schema.custom`, a plugin type key IDENTICAL on every instance. `client.field_catalog` now captures it, and `NOISE_JIRA_FIELDS` (six literal `customfield_*` ids), `NOISE_FIELD_NAMES` (English names) and the unconditional `fields.get("customfield_10002")` sprint read are DELETED in favour of `find_by_schema_key`/`native_target`/`noise_reason`. Verified against the live instance: `gh-sprint`→customfield_10002, `gh-epic-link`→10003, `gh-lexo-rank`→10007 AND 13701, `devsummary`→13400 — every id the blocklist hardcoded, discovered instead of assumed (and customfield_51604 was no longer present at all: the list had gone stale against its own instance). Noise now carries a human REASON shown in the UI. the hardcoded `FALLBACK_EMAIL_DOMAIN` is gone too — `connections.placeholder_email_domain` derives it from the connection host (or `RADD_JIRA_PLACEHOLDER_EMAIL_DOMAIN`), and with no domain `person_email` invents NOTHING. **(4) Unused is hidden AND ignored** (`FieldBand`: `in_use`/`noise`/`unused`/`builtin`, replacing spec 90's `is_builtin`+`likely_noise` booleans, which could not express "unused" at all — so a field no issue has ever filled in scored as ordinary data and sat at the TOP of the grid). Only IN_USE is expanded; the rest collapse AND default to `ignore`, each carrying a human `band_reason` shown verbatim so hiding is a judgement you can overrule rather than a disappearance. Measured on the live instance: **320 catalog fields → 9 rows visible**, 254 unused / 29 machinery / 28 native. SPA: `Settings → Import from Jira` gains a Connections panel and a Downloads panel (size + X to delete + live progress), `web/src/lib/queries/jira.ts` (the wizard previously had no queries module and inlined ad-hoc keys), and `ProblemList` (grouped by cause). Tests: `test_jira_connections.py`, `test_jira_snapshot.py`. **(5) The plan + the pipeline.** `profile/` (PURE, one streaming pass over the cache) counts every field and vocabulary; `plan/` holds NINE editable mapping tables (fields, issue types, statuses, priorities, link types, users, sprints, versions, components) in one JSONB doc, pre-filled by `suggest.py` from STABLE signals only — Jira's `statusCategory.key`, its priority `id` ORDER (so P1/P2/P3 and Urgent/Normal both work), its link-type outward phrasing, an exact email match. `provision.py` creates the real targets FIRST (project/fields/states/issue types/link types/placeholder users), idempotently and ledgered — spec 90 only ever *validated* a create, so there was nothing to look at before committing. `runs.py` is ONE pipeline with a `commit` flag: a dry run resolves everything and writes nothing, the import does identical work and writes, so they cannot disagree. Stage order ITEMS→PARENTS→LINKS→RELINK is what makes links work. **Comment visibility (RADD-1180):** a Jira comment carrying `jsdPublic: false` (a Service Desk internal note) imports as an INTERNAL Radd comment — `issuemap.comment_is_internal` is the one rule, carried on both comment drafts and written through `CommentCreate.visibility`. **RADD-1182 supersedes the role/group fallback:** the original restriction rides both drafts; role/group-restricted comments are OMITTED with a `comment_restricted` problem in dry-run and import, pending explicit audience mapping. JSM-only internal notes remain internal. Existing rows are never silently repaired by reimport. `python -m radd.modules.jiraimport.audit_comments` produces a PostgreSQL-enforced READ ONLY, body-free JSONL audit over exact run/snapshot/comment provenance; missing source is unknown. It reads audience metadata through `comments.service.comment_audiences`. The sample-file CLI also retains internal visibility and refuses unresolved restrictions. `jira_import_records` is the provenance ledger (created rows + before-images of updated ones) that `rollback.py` replays in reverse — with a pre-flight naming anything a human edited since, which is then left alone (and the project too, rather than deleting it out from under them). `jira_pending_refs` + `relink.py` park an unresolvable cross-project reference behind a stand-in web link and resolve it when its target is finally imported — automatically after every import and on demand. **Verified live on a 126-issue DEV slice: 126/126 imported with Jira numbers and dates preserved, 248 events of which 0 not silent, 0 notifications, 256 comments across 18 real authors, 47 pending refs grouped by target project, 784 ledger rows, rollback removing 744 cleanly.** The run found three real bugs now fixed: issue types need a colour; a MAPPED field scoped to other projects is now widened into scope (it was failing 38 issues with "unknown field"); and a select value outside the target's options is now dropped-and-reported rather than killing the issue. SPA: `Settings → Import from Jira` = Connections · Downloads · Import plans · a tabbed mapping editor (incl. the **People** step with per-person actions + bulk "create placeholders for all" / "attribute all to…" / "leave unattributed") · Runs with Undo and Relink. `docs/specs/100-*.md`. **RADD-892 — rollback deletes only what registered hooks name.** `_PROJECT_CHILDREN`, a seven-entry tuple of OTHER modules' table names (`views`, `forms`, `releases`, `work_items`, `issue_types`, `states`, `project_timelogging`), is gone; `_delete` now walks `registries.project_purge_tables()`. Each owner declares its own: `views`/`forms`/`timelogging` (order 20), `releases` (30), `items` `work_items` (50), `workflow` `(workflow_transitions, states)` (70), `itemtypes` `issue_types` (75), plus every project-scoped kernel entity (order 10, auto). The hardcoded list was already WRONG in two ways a registry cannot be: it never knew about `milestones` (a plugin table it could not have anticipated), and it named `states` without `workflow_transitions`, whose un-cascaded `to_state_id` FK meant a project with any transition row could not be purged at all — reproduced and fixed (the old tuple raises ForeignKeyViolation on a seeded project; the registered list deletes it). `tests/test_project_purge.py` is the ratchet: coverage never drops below the old seven, every project-scoped table whose FK carries no ON DELETE action must be claimed by some spec, and a registered name must be a real table with a real `project_id`.
| `confluenceimport` | **Spec 117 — the Confluence importer**, the wiki half of the migration and the last named gap below. Spec 100's machine pointed at Confluence and writing into `pages` instead of `items`: **connections** (`confluence_connections`, the `JiraConnection` shape verbatim — exactly-one-default, redacted credential, `seed_from_env` once) → a **snapshot** downloaded ONCE (`confluence_snapshots`/`_pages`/`_comments`/`_attachments`; the row IS the progress bar) → a **plan** of editable mappings → a staged, silent, reversible **run**. **Server/DC only** — `viewpage.action?pageId=` is the Server/DC URL shape, so the REST base is `/rest/api`, auth is PAT or basic, and bodies arrive as **storage format** (the XHTML dialect with `ac:`/`ri:` elements) rather than Cloud's ADF; Cloud would be a second `client.py` behind the same `service.py`, deliberately not designed around. **One scope, three selections**: `ScopeKind` SPACE\|SUBTREE\|PAGES all resolve to one page-id list before the download starts, so the downloader has a single input shape; a hand-picked set keeps each page's real parent and the run attaches to the nearest IMPORTED ancestor and reports the gap, because silently flattening to the space root would look like it worked. **The converter** (`storage/`, PURE — no DB, no network, so it re-runs over the cache every time a mapping changes) is a ~90-line tree over stdlib `html.parser`: storage format is machine-generated XHTML, and a LENIENT HTML parser degrades on a decade of pasted junk where a strict XML one raises — `lxml` would buy speed we do not need for a C extension in the image. Five of the seven pre-existing `radd:*` extensions already covered the common vocabulary (`toc`, `children`/`pagetree`, `info`/`note`/`tip`/`warning`/`panel`→`callout`, `include`/`excerpt-include`, `contentbylabel`); `code` is a plain fence and `ac:task-list` a markdown checklist. **A real page taught it three things a fixture never would**: Confluence writes images BOTH ways (the editor emits `<ac:image><ri:attachment>`, a paste and every older editor emit a bare `<img>` with an absolute `/download/attachments/` URL — handling only the first silently dropped most images on most pages); `status` is INLINE and lives in table cells, so a block-level fence there ends the table (it degrades to inline code, losing the colour — there is no inline extension seam and inventing one for a lozenge is not proportionate); and `details`/`excerpt` are containers whose body IS the content. A fourth arrived from the live import: a filename with spaces makes `![a](b c.png)` not-a-link, so every emitted target goes through `_md_url` angle-bracket escaping. **The census** is the plan's point — a regex pass over the cached bodies counts every macro by real use, with a count-0 tail for what Radd already renders, collapsed and ignored (the spec-100 treatment that turned 337 Jira fields into 14 decisions). An unmapped macro becomes `radd:unsupported-macro` carrying its name and parameters, NEVER stripped: a page whose content WAS the macro would import empty, and building the renderer later upgrades every instance in place on the next conversion. **Restrictions map AD groups to THEMSELVES, by DN** — `groups` already mirrors AD with nesting and `GrantSubject.GROUP` is already a subject `pages`' `_PAGE_SPEC` accepts, so this is identity, not translation; the plan's `groups` table is the FALLBACK for principals that do not resolve (with bulk map-all-to-X / map-all-unknown-to-Y), and an unresolved principal with no fallback **fails its page** rather than importing it open, resolved BEFORE the page is created so there is no window where it exists and is public. **Version history is supported and OFF by default** (a live page in the corpus sits at version 206); revisions are written straight into `page_versions` via the new `write_version` seam rather than replayed through `update_page`, which would fire N events, N watcher fan-outs and 2N reindex passes to rebuild a history that is already final. Three new extensions ship with it — `unsupported-macro`, `expand`, and `items` (a Confluence `jiraissues` becomes an **SLQ** table; an untranslatable JQL keeps its original text and says so rather than running a guess that returns plausible wrong rows). A `jira` macro whose key spec 100 imported becomes a real issue mention, so the imported wiki stops pointing at the system being replaced. Dry run shares the write path via one `commit` flag; the pipeline runs inside `events.quiet`; `confluence_import_records` (BigInteger Identity, so undo is the exact inverse of write order) makes it rollback-able. Instance-admin only, no new atoms. SPA: `Settings → Import from Confluence` — one scrolling page, reusing the census/used-unused split and the "Fix in Macros → drawio" jump. Proof: `web/scripts/confluence-import-proof.mjs`. `docs/specs/117-confluence-import.md`. | auth, events, pages, attachments, comments, labels, access, groups, teams, items, projects | — | `on_startup` `connections.seed_from_env`, `snapshot.download.mark_interrupted`, `runs.mark_interrupted` |

| `leave` | **Leave + team holidays (timesheet wave).** `leave_periods` — ONE subject per row (`user_id` XOR `team_id`, check-constrained): personal leave vs team HOLIDAY (regional public holidays; different teams differ — the point). Holidays expand to CURRENT members at read time, never materialized. Authz: everyone records their own; a team steward covers members (`teams.is_team_steward`); admins record anyone's + holidays. `POST/DELETE /leave`, `/leave/mine`, `/leave/holidays`, **`/leave/current`** (who is away today — ONE cached query feeds the app-wide indicator: `Avatar` dims + palm badge + "until" tooltip on EVERY surface rendering people), `/leave/calendar?start&end` (spans pre-expanded per member — the timesheet joins them onto its day grid: palm cells, exempt from outlier flags). **Timesheet outlier flags** (settings `timesheet_day_min_hours`/`_max_hours`, instance scope, defaults 6/10; `Timesheet` payload carries resolved bounds + `work_days`): person-view day cells tint amber under-min (completed workdays only, leave-exempt) / red over-max, tooltips explain. User merge REPOINTS leave (one person), hard delete DESTROYS it like worklogs (a successor must not inherit vacations). SPA (settings reorg): my leave lives ON `Settings → Profile`, holidays admin CRUD on `Settings → Time logging` beside the working week they interrupt (RADD-932; `/settings/leave` and `/settings/holidays` both redirect); timesheet person rows get avatars. **RADD-1031 — leave provides the kernel's `NON_WORKING_DAYS` socket** (`holidays.py`: `non_working_dates(session, start, end)` unions every HOLIDAY-subject period's dates in the window — the instance's own studio calendar, never a person's personal leave, since the socket answers "does nobody work today", a question with no room for one person's absence); `slas` is the first consumer (its SLA clock pauses on these dates too, alongside business hours) and reaches it ONLY through the socket, never a direct import — a second calendar-holding plugin unions in with no edit to either module. Tests: `test_leave.py`, `test_sla_holidays.py` (the consumer side, in `slas`). | auth, teams, events | `leave.created`, `leave.deleted` | — |
