| `scripts` | **Admin-authored Python, run from automations (RADD-1269).** A `core=False` bootstrap plugin (in `config.modules`, on by default, disableable). `interpreter.py` owns ONE uv-built venv per instance under `RADD_SCRIPTS_DIR` (`uv venv --python X`, then the Radd SDK client and every `script_packages` row via `uv pip install`; `uv pip show` reports what resolved). **RADD-1277 — it builds with no network:** `resolver_args` puts every existing WHEELHOUSE first (`RADD_SCRIPTS_FIND_LINKS` — the image's `/app/wheels`, holding the SDK wheel + its closure built by `pip wheel` at image build — then `<scripts_dir>/wheels` for an operator's own), then the admin's `index_url`, then `--offline` when the row says so; the SDK install is ALWAYS offline when the SDK is a bundled wheel (`sdk_is_bundled`), and `--seed` is gone (pip came from PyPI and nothing needs it). `script_interpreter.index_url`/`offline` are the admin's (migration `d1277scriptidx`; `PUT /scripts/interpreter`, event `script_interpreter.updated` with a diff, the URL's password masked everywhere — `masked_url`). `RADD_SCRIPTS_API_URL` is what `ctx.client` calls (the chart sets the in-cluster Service). `runner.py` runs ONE script: a fresh temp dir holding the bundled `harness.py` (the child-side program, never imported by the server) and the body as `radd_script.py`, the packet as JSON on stdin, a MINIMAL environment (no server secrets), the result on the last stdout line after a sentinel, killed at the timeout, stdout/stderr tail-capped. The child's `ctx` carries `event`, `items` (full read models loaded AS the automation's actor, capped by `scripts_max_items`), `vars`, `params`, `actor` and a lazy `ctx.client` (`radd_sdk.RaddClient`) bound to a SHORT-LIVED UNSCOPED key `auth.service_tokens.mint_ephemeral_token` mints for the actor and `run_body` discards after — a key can never exceed its account (spec 113), so a script cannot exceed the automation. `run_body` COMMITS around the run (the child's request arrives on another connection). Nodes: `script.run` (action; set|item arity; `outputs` param declares the dict keys published as tokens; a failure RAISES so the executor records it — `executor._run_contributed_action` now rewrites the plan entry `resolves=False` on an exception, for every contributed action) and `script.decide` (gate; `ports` param + `unavailable`; its plan IS the run, like `ai.classify`). **RADD-1272: the script's BODY lives on the node** (`params.body`, a fresh node arrives with the author contract as its default; `check` refuses a body without `main(ctx)`), so the graph is the script and the graph's versions (RADD-1268) are its history — the one-day script library (`scripts`, `script_versions`) is gone, and the unreleased `d1269scripts` migration was edited rather than followed by a drop. `script_packages` (spec validated to name+extras+specifiers — no URLs, paths or options), `script_interpreter` (one row). `POST /scripts/run` (the inspector's Test box: a body as typed, an optional seed item by key, as the caller — a dry run never applies an action, so this is how a script is tried), `/scripts/interpreter` + `/rebuild`, `/scripts/packages` (+ DELETE). Atom `script.manage` (global, implied by `global.manage`; mirrored in `auth.types.Permission`). Events `script.created/updated(changes, body hidden)/deleted`, `script_package.installed/removed`, `script_interpreter.rebuilt` — audited, never triggers. SPA: Settings → Scripts (`routes/settings/scripts.tsx`: interpreter card, packages, the author contract) under the SERVER nav group beside Automations (both moved there from Issues, RADD-1272); `ScriptNodeFields` in the automation inspector holds the CodeMirror `PythonEditor`, outputs/ports, timeout and the Test box; dynamic ports/outputs in `node-visuals`/`automation-outputs`. Tests `tests/test_scripts.py` (this process's interpreter stands in for the venv); proof `web/scripts/scripts-plugin-proof.mjs` (a real uv build, a real package, a real comment through the SDK). | auth, events, projects, items | `script.*`, `script_package.*`, `script_interpreter.rebuilt` | — |
| every module (RBAC) addendum | **RADD-890 — a module declares the permission atoms it enforces.** `auth/types.py::Permission` centrally enumerated all 88 atoms of every feature module (`item.*`, `page.*`, `worklog.*`, `sla.*`, `vcsconn.*`, …) while the kernel permissions registry built for exactly that (spec 93/A2, `PermissionSpec` + `CrudResourceSpec`) had ONE client — `milestones`. Two parallel systems: plugins contributed, core modules edited auth. Now every atom is declared on the manifest of the plugin that refuses on it, and the registry is what `all_permission_keys` / `permission_scope_of` / `permission_description_of` / `implied_map` / role + token-scope validation / `GET /permissions` compose from — no core-vs-plugin branch anywhere. **Who contributes what:** `items` item.read/create/update/delete · `comments` comment.write/read_internal/delete · `timelogging` worklog.write/delete + timesheet.view · `pages` page.read/write/manage/delete (SPACE-scoped) · `attachments` attachment.create/delete · `workflow` state.manage + the state triple · `fields` field.manage + the field triple · `forms` form.manage + the form triple · `webhooks` webhook.manage + the webhook triple · `automations` automation.manage + the automation triple · `releases` release.* · `views` view.* + cardpreset.* · `labels` label.* · `canned` canned.* · `cycles` cycle.* · `slas` sla.* · `teams` team.* · `itemtypes` issue_type.* · `dashboards` dashboard.create · `vcs` vcsconn.* (forgejo's until RADD-1258) · **`auth` keeps governance only**: `global.manage`, `project.create`, `project.manage`, and the user/role/member/service_account resources whose tables and routers live there (`auth/permissions.py`). **What auth still knows, and why:** the `Permission` enum survives as the TYPED ALIAS SURFACE (~1000 call sites pass `Permission.ITEM_READ`, and `PROJECT_PERMISSIONS` — hence the seeded Admin builtin role — is computed when auth is IMPORTED, which is before any feature manifest exists; a registry-built enum would make the admin role a function of import order), together with one scope token per atom (`_PROJECT_SCOPED`/`_SPACE_SCOPED`). Both copies are ratcheted by `tests/test_permission_ownership.py`: registry ⊇ enum, registry ⊆ enum, scopes agree, one owner per atom, and auth's manifest == the governance set. **Implication edges moved with the atoms**: a `CrudResourceSpec.manage` umbrella, `PermissionSpec.implied_by` (declared in spec 93, read by NOTHING until now — a plugin atom could not ride an umbrella at all), and a new `PermissionSpec.implies` for the one thing `implied_by` cannot express — a relation-qualified form that is not itself a catalog atom (`item.update` → `attachment.delete@own`, RADD-790/816). `auth.types.IMPLIED_PERMISSIONS`, `CRUD_RESOURCES`, `CRUD_MATRIX` and `ResourceSpec` are GONE; `PERMISSION_DESCRIPTIONS` is a live Mapping over the registry. Behaviour-identical: the 92-row permission catalog (scope, prose, resource, action, order) hashes the same before and after. Two deliberate consequences of an atom having an owner at last — a token scope may now name a plugin atom (`scopes._atoms` validates against the live catalog, not the enum, so the spec-113 intersection no longer silently drops one), and uninstalling a `core=False` module sweeps ITS atoms from stored roles (RADD-818, which previously had nothing to sweep). |
| every module (settings) addendum | **RADD-891 — a module declares the scalar settings it reads.** `settings/types.py::SettingKey` centrally enumerated every feature module's tunables (`ai_*` ×7, `ldap_*` ×11, `csat_enabled`, `release_*` ×2, the four timesheet/timelog keys, `workflow_transition_mode`, `estimation_points` — 27 in all) in one hardcoded `SETTINGS_REGISTRY` dict, while the kernel fields built for exactly that (spec 93, `RaddPlugin.settings_keys` + `SettingSectionSpec`) were read by NOTHING — the dead-code audit's SettingKey finding (`research/audit-2026-08/01-architecture-kernel-plugin.md` §5). Mirrors RADD-890's fix for `auth.types.Permission`, one registry over: every key is now declared via `settings_keys=(SettingSpec(...),),)` on the manifest of the module that RESOLVES it (`settings.service.resolve`/`.set_value`/`.list_for_scope`), and `settings` validates against the kernel registry instead of hardcoding every feature's keys. **Who contributes what:** `timelogging` work_week_days + timelog_hours_per_day + timesheet_day_min/max_hours (the timesheet's four tunables — `work_week_days` is also read by `slas`/`projects`, but timelogging is where the timesheet grid and its outlier flags live) · `workflow` workflow_transition_mode · `csat` csat_enabled · `items` estimation_points (resolved directly by the SPA via `GET /scoped-settings/resolve` — `usePointsEnabled` — not by server code, but items is still the true owner: it gates the `estimate_points` column) · `ldap` the eleven `ldap_*` connection/sync keys · `ai` the seven `ai_*` feature toggles · `releases` release_waiting_state + release_shipped_state · **`settings` declares none of its own** — it is pure cascade mechanism (asserted by `tests/test_setting_ownership.py`). **What `settings` still knows, and why:** `SettingKey` survives as the TYPED ALIAS SURFACE — it types a FastAPI query param (`GET/DELETE /scoped-settings`) and a pydantic field (`ScopedSettingWrite.key`), both fixed at `settings`'s own import time, which happens at a FIXED, early position in `config.Settings.modules` (right after `auth`) — long before `ai`/`ldap`/`releases`/`timelogging`/`workflow`/`items`/`csat` have registered theirs. A registry-built `SettingKey` would make its membership a function of `RADD_MODULES` order. Ratcheted by `tests/test_setting_ownership.py`: registry ⊇ enum, registry ⊆ enum, one owner per key, every key's label+description present, type/scopes are known values, and every `SettingSpec.default` resolves without raising. **Unlike `Permission`**, nothing in `settings` computes eagerly from the full key set at ITS OWN import time — there is no settings equivalent of auth's `PROJECT_PERMISSIONS` — so there is no static per-key scope/type table to keep in sync; `setting_spec(key)` reads type/scopes/prose/default LIVE off `radd.kernel.registries.settings` on every call, and `types.SETTINGS_REGISTRY` is a live `Mapping` over that registry (was a dict literal) so `SETTINGS_REGISTRY[key].default`-shaped call sites (incl. `tests/test_scoped_settings.py`) keep working unchanged. `SettingSpec` itself moved OUT to the kernel (`kernel/specs.py`) — plain `str` `type`/`scopes` rather than `settings.types`'s enums (kernel purity forbids importing a module's enum), with `.default` as a kernel property doing the `getattr(config.Settings, config_attr or key)` dynamic dispatch (kernel's own domain per its charter: "config/loader/lifecycle… the settings platform"). Behaviour-identical: booting `create_app()` and diffing the resolved catalog (every key's type/scopes/label/description/choices/secret/config_attr/default) before and after RADD-891 hashes the same. `RaddPlugin.settings_sections` (kernel `SettingSectionSpec`) remains declared-but-unwired — no consumer groups the generic settings editor by section yet; the SPA's per-page key lists (`web/src/lib/types/settings.ts`) still hand-maintain which keys appear on Settings → AI/Directory/etc, unchanged by this issue. |
| every module (fact providers) addendum | **RADD-892 — the aggregation inversion: a module declares the facts about ITSELF, and the consumer iterates.** Three registries with one shape, closing the audit's `research/audit-2026-08/01-architecture.md` §1/§2/§4 findings. **`nav_facts` (`NavFactSpec{key, resolve(session,user)->bool}`)** — auth's `_nav_facts` imported `timelogging.service` and `forms.portal` behind try/except ImportError to answer RADD-843's two area-visibility booleans, which inverted the load order (both modules load AFTER auth); now `timelogging` contributes `timesheet` (`service.nav_timesheet_visible`) and `forms` contributes `portal` (`portal.nav_portal_visible`, new, beside `list_portal_forms` so the nav answer and the page cannot disagree), and `MeRead.nav` is a `dict[str, bool]` built from the registry at REQUEST time (an unloaded module leaves its key ABSENT, which the SPA's `known(v) => v !== false` already reads as visible — same fail-open, no `NavFacts` schema class). **`grant_scopes` (`GrantScopeSpec{key, labels, exists, reach?}`)** — what a role grant may be BOUND to: `projects` contributes `project` (`service.project_keys` + new `service.project_exists`), `pages` contributes `space` (`pages/grantscope.py`: names, existence, and `reach` → `(readable_spaces, total_spaces)`). This killed the LAST entry in `tests/test_module_contracts.py::MODEL_IMPORT_ALLOWLIST` — auth reached into `pages.models.PageSpace` from `authz_explain._space_names` (now the generic `authz_explain.scope_labels(session, kind, ids)`), from `router.user_resource_access`'s space counts, and from `grants.create_grant`'s scope check (now `_check_scope`, which validates whichever scope column is set against the registry). `reach` is OPTIONAL and `project` does not supply it: how many projects someone can read is an atom question auth answers with `require_anywhere`, while space readability is a per-space ACL only pages can compute. **The set of scopes stays CLOSED** — `key` names the `<key>_id` column on `global_role_grants`, so a third kind is still a migration in auth; the registry inverts the KNOWLEDGE, not the schema. **`project_purges` (`ProjectPurgeSpec{name, tables, order}`)** — see the jiraimport row. **What did NOT invert, deliberately:** teams and groups as grant SUBJECTS stay direct `weak_depends` service calls (`teams.user_team_ids`, `groups.user_group_ids`, `*_by_ids`), because `global_role_grants` carries a `team_id` and a `group_id` COLUMN — the subject set is closed by auth's own schema, so a subject registry would advertise an extensibility no column list can honour. auth's `weak_depends` is now `(access, groups, teams)`, down from six. |
| every module (audit diffs) addendum | **Spec 123 — every `*.updated` emitter says WHAT changed.** `kernel/changes.py` (`snapshot`/`column_fields`/`diff`/`diff_object`/`collection_change`/`hidden_change`; exported as `radd.sdk.changes`) is the one shape — `{field, from, to}`, `{field, added, removed}`, `{field}` for a hidden value — resolved to NAMES at write time (owner → user name, grant → subject + scope, field scope → project keys, worklog → category name, page parent → title). Secrets (client secrets, API keys, S3 credentials, PATs, webhook secrets, mail passwords) and bodies (comment, page, canned response, automation graph, form fields, card layout) are `hidden`. `tests/test_event_changes.py` holds two EMPTY burn-down contracts: every registered `*.updated`/`*.changed` type declares `has_changes=True`, and every event type a module's `*Event` enum can emit is registered (48 were not when the contract landed). New audited writes: `setting.changed`, `project.public_access_changed`, `page_space.public_access_changed`, `sso_provider.*`, `ai_provider.*`/`ai_role.changed`/`ai_preset.*`, `storage_host.*`/`storage_rule.*`, `forgejo_`/`github_`/`jira_`/`confluence_connection.*` (+ repos), `mail_source.*`/`mail_sender.*`/`mail_rule.*`, `work_category.*`, `project.timelogging_changed`, `state_category.updated`, `plugin.contributions_changed`, a comment's `resolved`. None is an automation trigger (`trigger=False`); the trigger snapshot stays at 75. | — | — | — |
| `kernel` (entity host) addendum | **RADD-892 — `kernel/entities.py` imports no module, at load OR at request.** The generated CRUD handlers reached auth/projects/events through deferred imports (`from radd.modules.auth import authz` inside the handler), which made the kernel depend on three plugins it sits underneath. `kernel/hosts.py` defines **`EntityHost`** — four operations, because those are the four decisions the kernel must not make: `current_user(request, session)`, `require(session, user, atom, project_id=)`, `visible_rows(session, user, entity_key, rows)` (the item.read-per-project pass + the RADD-817 relation narrowing) and `emit(...)`. `auth/entityhost.py` implements it and installs it at auth's import (`hosts.set_entity_host`); the slot is module state rather than a `KernelRegistries` field precisely because `registries.clear()` would wipe it with no import left to replay it. Resolution is per REQUEST: the entity routers are BUILT while plugins are still loading, so the current-user dependency is a kernel-owned `_acting_user(request, session)` that defers to the host — a build-time `Depends(CurrentUser)` would have re-imported the very module this removes. `register_entity` also auto-registers a `ProjectPurgeSpec` for a project-scoped entity, which is what makes the north-star promise hold in both directions: declare an entity, write no model code AND no teardown code. |
| `auth`/`items`/`comments` addendum | **RADD-1148 — a per-account write throttle (spec 121 §9).** `auth/throttle.py` generalised: one `_SlidingWindow` (bounded keys + bounded history) now sits under both `LoginThrottle` and the new `WriteThrottle`, keyed `(WriteBucket, user id)` — `item.create` / `comment.create`. `check_write(user, bucket)` is the seam the routers consult AFTER authentication and BEFORE the service call (`POST /items`, `POST /items/{id}/comments`, `POST /{entity_type}/{id}/comments`); it exempts instance admins and the automation actor (a literal uuid, the notify idiom, pinned to `automations.types.SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID` by test). Refusal is 429 + `Retry-After`. Tunables `RADD_WRITE_WINDOW_SECONDS` / `RADD_ITEM_CREATES_PER_WINDOW` / `RADD_COMMENTS_PER_WINDOW` / `RADD_WRITE_THROTTLE_BUCKET_LIMIT` (`docs/deploy.md`). Service-level creation (mail intake, forms, automations, MCP) is deliberately NOT throttled — the window guards the open HTTP routes, and per-process counters carry the login throttle's single-replica caveat. |
| `web` (frontend, `web/`) | React 19 + Vite SPA — Phase 1: login, app shell, projects index. Phase 2: board `/p/$key` (columns from states, native-DnD card moves with optimistic update + rollback), list `/p/$key/list` (client-side filters: kind/category/assignee/team/label), item detail — a single **canonical key-addressed issue page** `/issues/$itemKey` (Jira-style, spec 21; `routes/item-page.tsx` → `components/items/ItemDetailBody`), resolved server-side via `GET /items/by-key/$itemKey` (`useItemByKey`/`itemByKeyQuery` — resolves the item then its project, current workspace first then fanning out across the caller's workspaces for the duplicate-key edge; replaces the old client number→id list scan). EVERY internal issue link now points here (`params: { itemKey: item.key }`): list rows, board cards, roadmap bars + unscheduled tray, saved-view board/list/swimlane cards, the detail's parent + dependency links, and the form-submit success link. The old board side-panel route `/p/$key/i/$number` (`ItemDetailPanel`) and page route `/p/$key/issue/$number` are RETIRED (as are `ItemDetailPanel`/`useItemByNumber`) — a board card now navigates to the full page (no separate side panel), and the page's back link targets the item's project board (resolved from the loaded item) else the projects index — inline title/description, state/priority/assignee/team pickers, label chips, custom-fields form generated from the fields registry, comments thread; New-item modal (kind/parent/assignee/team/labels/custom fields, registry 422s rendered per field). Phase 3: settings suite under `/settings/*` (secondary-nav layout, `/settings` → fields): `fields` (table + create-only New-field modal with options editor and scope; new fields start default-open — grants are edited afterwards, see Phase B), `states` (project picker, ordered list, add, inline rename via PATCH), `labels` (list with color swatches + create with color picker), `teams` (create, expand → members add/remove, attach to project with role, attachments collected across projects), `members` (workspace memberships joined with `/users`, add with role, 403 → admin-only notice), `tokens` (PAT create with once-shown token panel + copy, list with prefix/last-used, two-step revoke). Spec 09 Phase A: saved views — sidebar per-project sections (Board, List, then that project's views; personal views marked) plus a workspace "Views" section for `project_id: null` views; view pages `/p/$key/v/$viewId` + `/v/$viewId` fetch ONE `GET /items?` + the view's `query_string` verbatim (zero client-side filtering or sorting — SLQ ORDER BY arrives pre-applied) and bucket client-side for presentation (`lib/view-utils.ts` — axis tokens `state|assignee|priority|kind|team|cf.<key>`, columns/sections incl. always-present Unassigned/No-team/None buckets; boards without group_by fall back to state columns); header shows Shared/Personal badge, axis summary, the view's SLQ text, Edit/Delete (owner, or view.manage for shared). Spec 11 (spec 10 consumer — SLQ + swimlanes): the chip filter builder is DELETED — `ViewModal` edits views as SLQ text (`components/views/SlqEditor`: monospace textarea + debounced live validation via `GET /items?<scope>&q=<draft>` at the page cap — match count on success, 422 `{detail, position}` parse errors rendered as message + caret under the offending spot, helpers in `lib/slq.ts`), with a collapsible syntax cheat sheet (`components/views/SlqCheatSheet` — grammar constants in `lib/slq.ts` mirroring spec 10's frozen grammar + custom-field rows live from the registry) and axis pickers: Columns (`group_by`) and Swimlanes (`swimlane_by`, boards only, must differ, gated on Columns) over the builtin axes + every SELECT-type registry field (`cf.<key>`, labeled by field name); boards with `swimlane_by` render the swimlane matrix (`components/views/ViewSwimlanes`): sticky column-header row × sticky collapsible lane headers (bucket name + count; collapsed lanes persisted per view in localStorage), cells = lane×column intersections with empty cells slim; the project list route gains an ad-hoc SLQ bar (same editor, compact) that swaps the list's base set to the server-filtered/-ordered `q=` result — `useSlqProbe` (`lib/hooks.ts`) owns debounce + fetch, so the validation count and the rendered rows are one shared request. Spec 13 (spec 12 consumer — SLQ autocomplete): the same `SlqEditor` hosts a Jira-style suggestion dropdown (`components/views/SlqSuggestDropdown`, controller `useSlqAutocomplete` in `lib/hooks.ts`, fetch/splice helpers in `lib/slq-suggest.ts`) — typing (debounced 150ms) or Ctrl+Space calls `GET /items/slq/suggest?q=&cursor=&workspace_id=[&project_id=]`, showing field/operator/value/keyword suggestions with a context header; ↑/↓ navigate (hints skipped), Enter/Tab/click accept (splices `insert` over `[replace_from, cursor)` + a trailing space so the caret advances to the next context — field→operator→value→keyword chaining), Esc closes; both the ViewModal editor and the ad-hoc list bar pass `suggestScope` (workspace_id + optional project_id, so values are scoped to the project). Admin panel: `/settings/roles` (role list with builtin badges, expandable permission matrix from `GET /permissions` grouped by scope with descriptions — editable + deletable for custom roles, New-role modal with auto-slug key), `/settings/projects` (per-project direct members: add user+role from `GET /roles`, inline role change, remove; team attachments: attach/change-role/detach), `/settings/members` gains inline workspace-role change + remove (self-row locked), teams panel gains data-driven role selects + detach. Spec 09 Phase B (spec 07 consumer): `/settings/fields` grows a per-field expandable Permissions editor (`components/settings/FieldPermissionsEditor`) — grant rows (role/team picker from `GET /roles`+`GET /teams` × read/write, remove, add-grant row) saved as a full-list replace via `PUT /fields/{id}/permissions` with optimistic fields-cache update + rollback and inline 409 rendering; fields with any grant show an amber "Restricted" badge in the table (no grants = "Open"), default-open semantics explained inline; item-detail custom-field inputs stay enabled (grants + the caller's subjects aren't exposed client-side) and a fields-grant 403 (`no permission to write custom fields: <keys>`, parsed by `deniedCustomFieldKeys` in `lib/api.ts`) surfaces inline on the named controls; unreadable fields never arrive so their controls simply render empty. Comments: composer gains a Public reply / Internal note toggle shown only with `comment.read_internal` on the project (`usePermissions().project`), internal comments render with an amber Internal badge + tinted background; `GET` already filters server-side, so no client hiding. Permission gating via `usePermissions()` (`global(p)` — global scope, from `/auth/me` membership `permissions` (renamed from `workspace(p)` in the spec-67 follow-up); `project(project, p)` from `ProjectRead.permissions`; instance admin/anonymous-dev pass everything) — replaces `useIsWorkspaceAdmin`; gates New-item (board/list/sidebar, incl. the `c` hotkey), New-view (view.manage in scope), New-project (project.create), and every settings-create affordance; global 403 toast (`lib/toast.ts` + `<Toaster/>`, deduped) fired from the api client stays as backstop; settings lists have skeletons + empty states. Typed client `web/src/lib/api.ts` → `/api/v1` (cookie auth, 401 → /login); route/query-key/API-path consts in `web/src/lib/constants.ts`; enum display metadata in `web/src/lib/meta.ts`. Degrades: `/auth/me` 404 → anonymous dev mode (settings affordances stay visible); comments 404 → "unavailable" placeholder; absent spec-02 item fields (kind/parent/assignee/team/counts) → optional-chained, kind defaults to issue. Spec 18 (cycles, releases, item planning fields): `ItemDetailBody` (panel + full page) gains a planning row (`components/items/PlanningFields`) — a **Cycle** select (`GET /cycles?workspace_id`, options + the selected cycle's derived-status dot), a **Release** select (`GET /releases?project_id`, options + status dot), and native **Start/Target date** inputs (empty clears via PATCH `null`) — plus a **Dependencies** section (`components/items/DependenciesSection`) listing outgoing+incoming `item.links` grouped by type/direction ("Blocks"/"Blocked by"/"Relates to"/"Duplicates"/"Duplicated by"), each a link to the far item's issue page + remove (×), and an add-link row (type select + number/key input → `POST /items/{id}/links`, 409 conflicts surfaced inline; both ends refresh since the POST returns the updated source and every `["item"]` detail cache is invalidated — `useAddItemLink`/`useRemoveItemLink` in `lib/item-mutations.ts`). Cards show cycle+release chips (`CycleChip`/`ReleaseChip` in `components/items/ItemBadges`), the New-item modal gains optional cycle/release/dates. Sidebar grows a workspace **Cycles** section (active cycle badged, ⚙ → `/settings/cycles` when `cycle.manage`); a cycle page `/cycles/$cycleId` (`routes/cycle.tsx`) fetches every project's items and client-filters by `item.cycle?.id` (GET /items has no cycle param), grouping by state category with a done/total progress bar. Settings `/settings/cycles` (create/edit/delete via modal, `cycle.manage`) and `/settings/releases` (per-project picker, create/edit, Mark-released PATCH `status→released`, delete, that project's `project.manage`). Types/queries/paths for Cycle/Release/ItemLink live in `lib/{types,queries,constants,meta}.ts` (`cyclesQuery`/`cycleQuery`/`releasesQuery`). Spec 19 (roadmap/timeline/Gantt + reporting dashboards): **Roadmap views** (spec 79: `ViewType.roadmap` saved views rendered by `routes/view.tsx` → `components/roadmap/RoadmapSurface.tsx` — SLQ query, quick filters, sharing, Edit view all inherited from the view page; the legacy `/p/$key/roadmap` route redirects to the project's first roadmap-type view and `ProjectNav`/the sidebar dropped their fixed Roadmap entries) lay dated items (`start_date`+`target_date`) as bars on a hand-built week/month time axis — pure layout in `components/roadmap/roadmap-model.ts` (week-snapped domain, epic grouping, unscheduled split), rendered by `RoadmapTimeline` (positioned bars over gridlines + a today line, per-state-category bar colours from `CATEGORY_CHART_COLORS`); epics render as parent rows with their scheduled children indented under them (`kind`+`parent`), a `blocks` **incoming** link marks the dependent bar (⛔ blocking key), bars link to the issue page, and items missing either date park in an `UnscheduledTray`. **Spec 77 made the roadmap an EDITOR** (item.update actors; read-only otherwise): pointer-event bar move/resize with day snapping (optimistic date PATCHes; a child exceeding its parent epic pairs an outward-only epic-stretch PATCH — one toast/rollback for the pair), tray drag-in scheduling (drop day + `ROADMAP_LEAF/EPIC_SPAN_DAYS`), ○-handle drag → typed link via a drop-point popover (spec 78: Blocks default/Enter, Relates, Duplicates, Esc cancels) with SVG elbow `Connectors` between visible bars (header toggle; chip fallback for filtered-out endpoints; blocks red, relates/duplicates zinc, VIOLATED blocks — dependent starts on/before the blocker's end — dashed amber; wide invisible hit paths make connectors clickable → same popover retypes DELETE-then-POST or removes the link), right-click verbs via `RoadmapContextMenu` (epics: Fit to children / **Auto-schedule children** (spec 78 — replaces week-stacked Bring-children: Kahn topo over intra-epic blocks edges w/ rank tiebreak + cycle fallback, duration = ceil(estimate/hours-per-day) via `POST /items/timelog/batch` + `useDurationConfig` else `ROADMAP_LEAF_SPAN_DAYS`, same-assignee items serialize while others parallelize, epic fit inward+outward, one optimistic unit + "Scheduled N items" toast) / expand-collapse / Clear dates / Open; leaves: Clear dates / Open / Flag; every bar: a Dependencies submenu listing manual links with per-link Remove), expandable epic rows (collapse per-VIEW in localStorage since spec 79), zoom (6/9/14 px day widths) + Today button. **Spec 78 dependency cascade**: a commit that changes a bar's target date BFS-pushes its loaded `blocks` dependents so start = blocker.target+1d (duration preserved, recursive, push-forward only, cap 50 CASCADED items → cascade skipped + warning, Alt-drop skips) inside the SAME optimistic unit, with union-deduped parent-epic outward stretches for every moved item (`epicStretchPatches`); **spec 81 epic container drag**: a BODY move of a non-derived epic shifts the epic + every LOADED scheduled child by the same delta (`epicMovePlan`), the whole moved set seeding the now MULTI-SEED cascade planner (`cascadePlanMulti` — seeds deduped, never re-pushed, cap counts cascaded items only) in ONE optimistic unit; child bars translate live with the ghost during the drag (connectors lag until commit), resize doesn't touch children, and Alt = "just this bar" (skips children AND cascade); **spec 78 timeline extension**: the domain pads `ROADMAP_DOMAIN_PAD_DAYS` at BOTH ends, slim "+1 month" caps at each axis end grow session-state `{extendBeforeDays, extendAfterDays}` (additive `buildRoadmapModel` params), and a drag held within 40px of the scroll pane's edge auto-scrolls (rAF loop in `useBarDrag`, scroll-compensated deltas) then auto-extends once pinned — a before-extension shifts the live drag's indices via `shiftDomain` so the ghost keeps its dates; all date math stays pure in `roadmap-model.ts` (`dayFromX`/`moveSpan`/`resizeSpan`/`epicStretchPatch(es)`/`cascadePlan`/`cascadePlanMulti`/`epicMovePlan`/`autoSchedulePlan`/`intraEpicBlocksEdges`/`durationDaysFromEstimate`/`isBlocksViolation`, both-end-padded domain, derived spans for date-less epics), gestures in `useBarDrag.ts`/`useRoadmapEditing.ts`, the paired mutation in `lib/item-mutations.ts` `useRoadmapItemPatch` (view-keyed since spec 79 — paints the paged `viewItems` cache). **Spec 79**: roadmap views auto-fetch EVERY page (no Load-more; streaming count), the `UnscheduledTray` gains persisted per-view **All | Epics** chips and lists the full unscheduled set, and workspace-spanning roadmaps (project null) span readable projects — auto-schedule simply attempts the estimates batch and degrades quietly, cross-project ○-drops surface the server's 409 toast. A public-shaped **submit page** `/p/$projectKey/forms/$formId` (`routes/form-submit.tsx`) renders `GET /forms/{id}` as a title input (labelled by `title_prompt`) + one control per exposed field by registry type (reusing `CustomFieldControl` with the form's label/required overrides + help text), submits `POST /forms/{id}/submit`, shows the created item's key linking to its issue page, and renders 422s per field (`customFieldErrors` — form-required overrides + registry validation share the `{errors:["key: message"]}` shape); the sidebar per-project section gains **New-from-form** links (enabled forms, `form.manage`). Settings `/settings/timelogging` (`routes/settings/timelogging.tsx`): per-project enable toggles (`project.manage`) + work-category add/rename/archive (`workspace.manage`). Types/queries/paths live in `lib/{types,queries,constants}.ts` (`Worklog`/`ItemTimeSummary`/`WorkCategory`/`Timesheet` shapes, `itemTimelogQuery`/`workCategoriesQuery`/`projectTimeloggingQuery`/`timesheetQuery`, `apiItem{Worklogs,Timelog,Estimate}Path`/`apiWorklogPath`/`apiWorkCategoryPath`/`apiProjectTimeloggingPath`, `TimesheetPeriod`/`TimesheetGroupBy`, `Permission.worklogWrite`/`timesheetView`). Spec 24 (saved-view interaction — slices 1–3 shipped): saved views are no longer read-only. **Cross-bucket drag** — `lib/axis-dnd.ts` (`dragEnabledForAxis`, `bucketMovePlan` → `{patch,optimistic}`) maps a drop onto a bucket to the axis's field (backlog→cycle `cycle_id`, medium→high `priority`, state/assignee/team/select-cf; `kind` not draggable (it converts via its own endpoint, RADD-1089, never by drag); state needs project scope), applied via `useUpdateItemInView(view)` (optimistic merge into `viewItemsQuery`, reconcile on success, invalidate on settle, rollback on error); native HTML5 DnD in `ViewList` sections, `ViewBoard` columns, `ViewSwimlanes` cells (cell drop sets BOTH axes); gated on `item.update`. **Right-click menu** — generic `components/ContextMenu.tsx` (positioned, flyout submenus, outside/Esc/scroll close) + `components/items/ItemContextMenu.tsx` (Open, Set priority ▸/state ▸, Assign to me/Unassign, Move to cycle ▸ incl. Backlog, Flag/Unflag, Copy key/link; mutating rows need `item.update`). **Flag is a first-class `flagged` BOOLEAN** (`work_items.flagged`, `Item.flagged`, SLQ `flagged = true`) — NOT a label; a `FlagBadge` (amber) shows on cards/list rows/detail, the issue detail header has a flag toggle, and `Item.flagged`/`ItemUpdate.flagged`/`ItemCreate.flagged` are in `lib/types.ts`. **Multi-select + bulk** (list views) — `routes/view.tsx` owns a `Set<itemId>` (row hover checkbox, click toggles+anchors, shift-click range), `components/views/BulkActionBar.tsx` floats when ≥1 selected (set priority / move-to-cycle / assign-to-me / flag=true / clear, fan-out PATCHes). `lib/toast.ts` gained a `success` kind. **Manual rank** (slice 4) — `ViewList` enables within-section drag-to-reorder (top/bottom insertion indicator) whenever the view is **rank-ordered** (no explicit non-rank `ORDER BY` — the default) + `item.update`; `useReorderItem(view)` optimistically splices the array and `PATCH /items/{id}/rank` sets the midpoint; cross-section drag still changes the axis field (`item_rank_step` config = 1024). **Personal star** (slice 5) — `StarButton`/`StarBadge` in `ItemBadges` (row hover-toggle, card/list indicator, detail-header toggle), a Star/Unstar context-menu row (needs only `item.read`), `useToggleStar(view)` (optimistic) + `useToggleStarOnItem()` (detail), `Item.starred` in types, `apiItemStarPath`/`apiItemRankPath`; filter with SLQ `starred = true`. `flagged`/`starred` added to the SLQ cheat sheet (`lib/slq.ts`). Spec 25 (issue side panel + back nav): a `?peek=<itemKey>` search param on the pathless `appLayoutRoute` (`validateSearch`, inherited by all in-app routes) drives a right-side drawer rendered once in `AppLayout` (`components/items/IssuePanel.tsx`, reusing `ItemDetailBody` via `useItemByKey`) — **clicking an issue opens the panel over the current view** (`usePeek().open` → `navigate({to:".", search})`, so Back closes it and `?peek=` URLs are shareable) with Close (×/backdrop/Esc) + **Open full page** (⤢ → `expand` navigates to `/issues/$key` with `replace:true`). Wired at every item-list surface — `BoardCard` (board + view boards/swimlanes), `ViewList` rows, the ad-hoc `ListPage` table, the cycle page rows, roadmap bars + `UnscheduledTray`, and timesheet issue rows; only the context-menu Open, form-submit success link, and in-detail parent/dependency links stay full-page. The full issue page's **Back** now uses `useCanGoBack()` + `router.history.back()` (returns to where you came from) instead of a hardcoded link to the project board (fallback: project board / projects index on a cold load). This partially revives the side-panel idea retired in spec 21, but as a global search-param drawer, not a nested route. **Live-update invariant** (bug fix): items are cached in FIVE separately-keyed families (board/list/cycle, saved views, ad-hoc SLQ list, detail, side-panel by-key). Rather than hardcode that list, invalidation is **by entity** (`lib/cache.ts`): each item query self-declares `meta: entityMeta(Entity.item)` and every item mutation calls `invalidateEntities(qc, Entity.item)` on settle (via the `invalidateItemCaches` wrapper) + seeds the returned item into `item`+`itemByKey` via `cacheItem`. New queries/modules are covered automatically — see "Frontend cache invalidation" above. Without it an edit on one surface (e.g. in the panel) stays invisible on the view behind it until a hard refresh. Spec 23 (cycle view axis + staging cycles): the view builder's Columns/Swimlanes pickers gain **Cycle** (`ViewAxis.cycle`); grouping by cycle (`lib/view-utils.ts` `groupByCycle`) buckets over the FULL workspace cycle list (`cyclesQuery` fed into `AxisContext.cycles`) so empty **staging cycles** appear, ordered active→upcoming→draft→completed then by start date, each header showing a summary (`detail` = "Active · Aug 1 – Aug 14 · 3/8 done") + a done/total `progress` bar, with items lacking a cycle collecting under a trailing **Backlog** bucket; an optional per-view **Cycle filter** glob (`view.cycle_filter`, `ViewModal` input shown only when an axis is `cycle`; `matchesCycleGlob` — `*`/`?` wildcards, contains match) narrows the header set and drops items in filtered-out cycles (`view.tsx` keeps a cycle-grouped view rendered even with zero items so staging headers still show, and `axisSummary` echoes the filter). `ViewList` sections are now **collapsible** (chevron header + count + `detail` + progress, collapsed set persisted per view via `listSectionCollapseStorageKey`) and `ViewBoard` columns show the same summary; `ViewGroup` gains `detail?`/`progress?`. Cycles become **draft-aware**: `CycleStatus.draft` + `CYCLE_STATUS_META`/`CYCLE_STATUS_ORDER`, `Cycle.start_date`/`end_date` are `string \| null`, `/settings/cycles` dates are optional (both-or-neither, blank = draft/staging) and `cycle.tsx`/the settings row render "Not scheduled". Spec 62 (requester loop): a PUBLIC root-level route `/public/forms/$token` (`routes/public-form.tsx`, registered beside `/login` OUTSIDE the auth-gated `appLayoutRoute` — the idiom for any future no-login page, e.g. the spec-65 CSAT page): narrow centered card rendering `GET /public/forms/{token}` (field definitions arrive INLINED and are synthesized into `FieldDef`s for the shared `CustomFieldControl`) + email/name inputs, success screen shows the created key as PLAIN TEXT (no tracker link for anonymous visitors); the form builder gains a "Public link" toggle + copy-URL row (`publicFormUrl`, immediate PATCH of `allow_public`); the issue rail shows an External-requester chip via the 404-quiet `mailContactQuery` (`GET /items/{id}/mail-contact` → null on 404, chip simply absent). Spec 65 (CSAT): a second public root-level route `/public/csat/$token` (`routes/public-csat.tsx`, `?rating=N` from the survey email preselects a star via the route's `validateSearch`; the page POSTs, re-submits allowed) + a rail "Requester satisfaction" stars chip (`CsatChip` in `IssueProperties`, 404-quiet `itemCsatQuery` — appears only once answered, comment as tooltip) + a CSAT tile and weekly avg-rating BarChart on the Service-desk report card (`SlaCard`). | REST API contracts (specs 01–03 + `/openapi.json`) — no server imports | — | — |

RADD-1224: the login page has no method tabs. The directory form appears only when the public LDAP capability is enabled; a small local-account link always opens the password/MFA form. Configured SSO providers appear below as alternatives. Authentication policies remain unchanged.

## Audit trail, history & item links

The append-only `events` table is the **durable, attributable audit log** — every domain mutation already emits into it with `actor_id`. This wave makes it a first-class history + audit surface, plus adds external + version-control links:

- **Field-level diffs.** `items/changes.py:diff_item_reads` compares the pre-mutation and post-mutation `ItemRead`s; `item.updated` payloads now carry a `changes` `[{field, from, to, …}]` list (display values — state/assignee names, cf labels — resolved at write time so records stay accurate after renames/deletes) *alongside* the full snapshot (reporting still reads `state.id`). Computed in `items.service._finish` for every item mutation route (update, link add/remove — each hydrates a `before` snapshot); create + rank-only reorder carry none.
- **Per-item history.** `GET /items/{id}/history` merges the item's own change events with related events (comment/worklog/weblink/vcs) via `events.entity_activity`. `item.read`-scoped; internal-comment activity hidden without `comment.read_internal`. Related event types are wire strings in `items/history.py:RELATED_EVENT_TYPES` — **keep in sync** with the comments/timelogging/weblinks/vcs `*Event` enums.
- **Admin audit view.** `events.query_events` (filtered) backs the `audit` module. Indexes added in migration `f0f365b7e495`.
- **Link typeahead.** `GET /items/link-search?project_id&q&exclude_id` — items matching by title/number/key.
- **Frontend.** The issue view's Comments section became a tabbed **Activity** panel (Comments · History · Work log · Version control — `components/items/ActivityPanel`, `HistoryTab`, `WorklogTab`, `VcsPanel`); a **Related links** section (`RelatedLinksSection`) sits under Dependencies; the dependency add-row gained typeahead (`linkSearchQuery`); `/settings/audit` (`routes/settings/audit.tsx`) is the admin table. New cache tags `Entity.webLink`/`vcsLink`; `itemHistoryQuery` is tagged with every entity that can appear in the feed (item/comment/worklog/webLink/vcsLink) so any of their mutations refresh it.

## Beyond the server modules

- **`sdk/`** — the extensions SDK (spec 44): an INDEPENDENT **Apache-2.0** uv project (`radd-sdk`) so private studio connectors are unambiguously fine. `RaddClient` (PAT-authed httpx wrapper + convenience calls), `Runner` (shotgunEvents ergonomics: drop `*.py` plugins exposing `register(reg)`/`reg.on(glob, cb)` in a dir; at-least-once dispatch off `GET /events`; per-plugin crash isolation; mtime hot-reload; atomic JSON offset checkpoint; `--from-start`), `radd-runner` CLI (flags + `RADD_URL/RADD_TOKEN/…` env), example plugin (`sla.breached` → label + comment), own pytest suite. ftrack/air-gapped-chat connectors are the canonical EXTERNAL extensions built on this — deliberately not in-core.
- **Packaging (spec 48)** — `Containerfile` (node builds `web/`, uv-locked server install, non-root, migrate-then-serve CMD), `compose.yaml` app service, **Helm chart** `deploy/helm/radd/` (web Deployment + optional worker split via `RADD_RUN_WORKERS`, pre-upgrade migration Job, Secret-driven env, PVC-or-S3 attachments, probes on `/instance/login-options`), `docs/deploy.md` (quickstarts, backup/restore, env reference). **Worker split**: the background loops (webhooks/automations/notify/search/sla/googlechat/mailintake) gate their `on_startup` on `settings.run_workers`; realtime + storage init always run.
- **MFA/TOTP (spec 48)** — in `auth`: pure RFC-6238 `auth/totp.py` (RFC-vector-tested), `user_totp` table, `POST /auth/totp/setup|/confirm`, `DELETE /auth/totp` (code required), `GET /auth/totp`; `POST /auth/login` → 401 `totp_required` for enrolled accounts → `POST /auth/login/totp` (stateless second step, uniform 401). SSO/LDAP logins untouched (the IdP owns MFA).

## Not yet built

Every §8 pillar now has a first implementation, and pgvector semantic search shipped in spec 103 (`GET /search/semantic`, RRF fusion in `/search`, the `ai.embedder` consumer). Remaining cross-cutting gaps (tracked, no module): Yjs/pycrdt wiki co-editing (single editor + optimistic 409 + versions today), and DB-row config for the connectors still configured from env only (gitlab, googlechat, alertmanager — forgejo/sso/ai/storage/jira moved to DB rows in specs 100–111, mailintake with RADD-958; TOTP recovery codes landed with RADD-677). See "Known simplifications" below.

## Connection rules

- Cross-module calls go through the other module's `service.py` public functions only. Models are private to their module (Alembic metadata aside) — **except the shared spine tables, below.**
- Every domain mutation calls `events.service.emit()` **in the same session/transaction** (the outbox guarantee). Event types are per-module `StrEnum`s (e.g. `ItemEvent.CREATED = "item.created"`).
- Custom fields must serialize **inline on the entity** in every representation (REST now; webhooks/MCP later). Never a side-channel endpoint. (See `research/architecture.md` — the Plane failure.)
- The `fields` registry is the single source of truth for dynamic schema. Consumers: item validation (now), OpenAPI (now, via marker scan), UI form config / MCP tools / webhook payload docs (later — same registry, no parallel definitions).
- **RBAC (spec 03, engine reworked by spec 06)** has one seam: routers (or the items/comments services, which resolve the project themselves) call `auth.authz.require(session, user, Permission.X, workspace_id=…|project=…)` → 403; `CurrentUser` → 401 when anonymous. Only `/auth/login`, `/auth/logout`, `/docs`, `/openapi.json` stay open. Roles are DATA: a user's effective permissions on a project are the **union** of the roles granted to them there — directly, through a team they are on, or through a directory group that contains them (RADD-929 folded `project_members`/`project_teams` into `global_role_grants`; one table, one path) — and — for members of the project's workspace — the builtin `viewer` floor (**open-visibility default**); instance admins and the workspace's admins hold every permission. Workspace-scoped checks: admin → all, member → floor. Unscoped checks (`/users`, `/workspaces` create, `/events`) use the caller's best membership anywhere. `require()` returns the effective-permission frozenset (callers reuse it — items derives `has_manage`/`comment.read_internal` from it without a second lookup). Never re-implement checks in modules — call the seam in `auth/authz.py`. **A new action class is declared by the module that enforces it** (RADD-890): put a `PermissionSpec`/`CrudResourceSpec` on that module's `RaddPlugin` manifest, and add the matching member to `auth.types.Permission` (+ `_PROJECT_SCOPED`/`_SPACE_SCOPED` if it is not global) purely so call sites can name it — `tests/test_permission_ownership.py` fails if either half is missing. Nothing else in `auth/types.py` is touched: scope, prose, umbrella and CRUD family all come from the declaration.

**Spine tables (the honest exception to the first rule).** ~130 cross-module model imports exist, and two tables are the de-facto shared spine the tracker joins against: `auth.models.User` (47 import sites across 25 other modules) and `projects.models.Project` (35 sites, 20 modules). Those two may be imported directly — user/project FKs and joins are how the tracker fits together. A few near-spine models (`fields.FieldDefinition`, `items.WorkItem`, `events.Event`, `workflow.State`) also cross module boundaries today; that's tolerated reality, not license — new cross-module needs still go through the owning module's `service.py`, emitted events, or a declared extension point.

## Known load-order edges

`depends_on` orders plugin loading, but a handful of cross-module imports would cycle (or invert the declared order) at module scope, so they're **deferred into function bodies** — grep for `# deferred:`. The band-aids, mapped:

- `workflow/transitions.py` + `transitions_router.py` — defer `fields`, `items`, `timelogging`, `comments`, `approvals` lookups; those modules load after workflow.
- `cycles/service.py` — defers `teams.service`, `auth.authz`, `items.service`.
- `projects/router.py` — defers `settings.service` / `SettingKey` (settings depends on projects, so the import must stay one-way).
- `auth/authz.py` / `auth/grants.py` / `auth/router.py` — defer `teams.service` (teams loads after auth).
- `linktypes/service.py` defers `items.models.ItemLink`; `items/history.py` defers `comments.visibility` (a genuine import cycle).

## Frontend cache invalidation (the convention every module follows)

One domain object is cached under **many** TanStack Query keys (an item lives in
`["items"]`, `["viewItems"]`, `["slqItems"]`, `["item"]`, `["itemByKey"]`). If a
mutation forgets one — or a new module adds a new query that caches the same
entity — an edit made on one surface silently won't appear on the others until a
hard refresh. To make this **impossible to get wrong across modules**, invalidate
by ENTITY, never by key (`web/src/lib/cache.ts`):

- **Read queries self-declare what they cache:** every `useQuery`/`queryOptions`
  that returns a domain object sets `meta: entityMeta(Entity.item)` (list one tag
  per entity in its data — a query joining items+cycles tags both).
- **Mutations invalidate by entity:** `onSettled: () => invalidateEntities(queryClient, Entity.item)`
  — a predicate refreshes every query tagged with that entity, including ones
  added later by other modules. List every entity a mutation can change (e.g.
  commenting touches `Entity.comment` AND `Entity.item` for its `comment_count`).
- Optimistic writes still `setQueryData` the specific caches for an instant feel;
  the entity invalidation on settle is the safety net that keeps every surface live.
- **New module checklist:** add its object to the `Entity` map; tag its read
  queries with `entityMeta`; have its mutations call `invalidateEntities`. Then a
  future query anywhere that caches the same entity is refreshed automatically —
  neither side needs to know about the other. (Retrofitting the pre-existing
  non-item modules onto this is incremental; new modules start here.)

## Field grant semantics (spec 07 → spec 92)

Field visibility rides the generic access framework (spec 92): grants are `access_grants` rows on resource_type=`field` (resource_id = the field definition's id); builtin item fields take the same model on resource_type=`builtin_field` (resource_id = the field name). Subjects are user/team/role/group (RADD-832), scope global or per-project, accesses `read`|`write` with **write implying read** (a writer can see what they write) and deny rows beating allows (RADD-819). Checks resolve through `access/resolution.has_access` against the actor's `FieldAccessContext` (`fields/service.py` — `role_ids` incl. builtin-derived + team-granted roles, `team_ids`, transitive `group_ids`, `user_id`, `has_manage`):

- **No grants for an access → that access is default-open** (`default_open=True`): read for anyone with `item.read`, write for anyone with `item.update`.
- **Read grants exist** → the field's value is visible only if one of the actor's subjects holds a read OR write allow (and no deny matches).
- **Write grants exist** → writable only via a matching WRITE grant (read grants never confer write). Corollary: a field with only read grants stays write-open to `item.update` holders.
- `has_manage` short-circuits both checks, and since RADD-816 it means **instance admin** (the operator who administers the grant system), NOT `project.manage`: a project manager is denied like anyone else unless a grant names them. Grant management itself needs `field.manage` on the field's own scope (`_can_manage_field`).
- Grants are extra restrictions on top of action RBAC, never a bypass: without `item.read`/`item.update` on the project a grant is moot.
- History: migration `fa2747459c4b` converted the original min-role ladder into `field_permissions` rows; the spec-92 migration `6fc5f7c71481` moved those into `access_grants` and dropped the table.

## SLQ — the Radd Query language (spec 10)

`items/slq/` (`lexer.py` → `parser.py` AST → `compiler.py` SQLAlchemy; op tables in `catalog.py`). `GET /items?q=…` takes it; saved views store it (`views.query`). The grammar is FROZEN — frontends render their cheat sheet from it:

```
query     := [expr] [ORDER BY order (, order)*]
expr      := term ((AND|OR) term)*        # AND binds tighter than OR
term      := [NOT] (comparison | '(' expr ')')
comparison:= field op value | field [NOT] IN '(' value (',' value)* ')' | field IS [NOT] EMPTY
op        := = | != | ~ | > | < | >= | <=
value     := bareword | 'single' | "double" quoted | number | YYYY-MM-DD | today[±Nd|Nw] | me | none
order     := field [ASC|DESC]
```

Keywords case-insensitive; field names case-sensitive; quoted values are always literal (never sentinels/keywords). Builtin fields: `project` (key), `state` (name), `category`, `kind`, `priority`, `assignee` (email | `me` | `none`), `team` (name | `none`), `label` (`=` has, `!=` lacks, `IN` has any, `IS EMPTY` unlabeled), `title` (`~` case-insensitive contains / `=`), `key` (`= TD-12`), `parent` (key | `none`), `number`, `created`/`updated` (date comparisons with whole-day windows: `= d` means within the day, `> d` strictly after it), and the **planning fields (spec 14)**: `cycle` (`= name` \| `= none` \| `!=` \| `IS [NOT] EMPTY`; matches `cycle_id` by cycle NAME via subquery, workspace-unscoped like `state`), `release` (`= version` \| `= none` \| `!=` \| `IS [NOT] EMPTY`; by release VERSION), `blocks`/`blocked` (`IS [NOT] EMPTY` = has an outgoing/incoming `blocks` link — an EXISTS over `item_links`; `= <key>` = links to/blocked-by that item; `=` only, no `!=`/`IN`), `start`/`target` (plain Date-column comparisons — no whole-day windowing — `= != > < >= <=` + `IS [NOT] EMPTY`; **not sortable**), `flagged` (**spec 24** — first-class boolean: `= true` \| `= false`, sortable, autocompletes true/false, other values → 422), `starred` (**spec 24** — the requesting user's personal star: `= true` \| `= false`, an EXISTS over item_stars scoped to `current_user_id`), `rank` (**spec 24** — manual sort key, **`ORDER BY rank` only**: no compare ops, excluded from filter-field autocomplete) — plus **every custom field by bare registry key** (select/text/url: `= != ~ IN`; user: `= != IN`; multi_select: containment — `=` contains, `!=` lacks, `IN` contains any; number/duration/date: comparisons; boolean: `= true|false`; all cf support `IS [NOT] EMPTY`). Unknown field or type-invalid op → 422 `{detail, position}` (character offset), with a one-edit did-you-mean on unknown fields. Compilation is subquery-based (state/project/user/team lookups, EXISTS for labels, JSONB ops for cf — `@>` for equality, `->> ::cast` for ranges); labels resolve name→id through the labels service; cf typing uses the same key→definition map as the `cf` param (project registry when `project_id` is given, instance-wide otherwise). `ORDER BY` sortables: `created`, `updated`, `number`, `title`, `priority`/`kind` (enum rank), sortable cf types (cast per type); `created DESC` always appended as tiebreak/default. `!=`/`NOT IN` on a nullable to-one relation (assignee/reporter/team/type/cycle/release, the ancestors, the worklog dialect's issue/project/category) are the COMPLEMENT of the positive form (RADD-1139, `helpers.polarity(nullable=True)` compiles `IS NOT TRUE` rather than `NOT`): items with the relation unset DO match — `assignee != me` includes the unassigned, `= x` and `!= x` partition the rows — while `!= none` still means "set" (its positive form is `IS NULL`); scalar-cf `!=` means "has a different value" (missing excluded, unchanged); multi_select/label `!=` means "lacks" (missing included). **Relative dates (spec 69):** every date position (`created`/`updated`/`start`/`target` + date custom fields) also accepts bare `today`, `today+3d`, `today-2w` (units `d`/`w`; a QUOTED 'today' stays literal, like the me/none sentinels), resolved at COMPILE time against the server date (`helpers.relative_date`, pure with injectable `today`) — a saved view using `today` shows results as-of query time; `today` leads the date-field suggest values (tier-sentinel, bare insert). **Ancestor fields (spec 83, `items/slq/ancestors.py`):** `epic` = the NEAREST EPIC ANCESTOR (an issue's parent, a subtask's grandparent — one aliased P/G self-join chain per condition deriving `CASE WHEN P.kind='epic' THEN P.id WHEN G.kind='epic' THEN G.id END` as a correlated scalar subquery; NULL for epics themselves and parentless items, so `epic IS EMPTY` covers both), `parent` stays the direct parent; the bare forms take `= key` / `!= key` / `IN (key, none)` / `IS [NOT] EMPTY` with keys resolved at COMPILE time through the spec-68 alias-aware `find_item_by_key` (a `compile_query` pre-pass like label resolution; unknown key → positioned 422 — a change from the old match-nothing `parent = key` subquery, so moved items keep matching under their pre-move keys). Sub-fields `epic.state`/`epic.category`/`epic.assignee`/`epic.priority` (+ the `parent.*` twins) mirror the item-level value semantics (state NAMES case-INsensitive here per the spec; category/priority enum-validated with positioned 422s; assignee `me`/`none`/email via the shared `builtins.user_match` helper) and compile to `<ancestor id> IN (SELECT id FROM work_items WHERE …)` — correlated comparisons, never joins — so OR/NOT hold, and the negative forms read plainly (RADD-1139: `epic.category != done` includes work under no epic — the complement of `= done`; positive predicates, `epic.assignee IS EMPTY` included, never match an item without the ancestor); cross-project ancestors (spec 80) match naturally since traversal is by id. Filter-only: none are sortable (`ORDER BY epic.state` → the standard not-sortable 422); suggest completes all nine field names and reuses the item-level value sources (state names, categories, users incl. `me`/`none`, priorities; bare `epic`/`parent` offer `none` + item keys).

## Saved views ↔ items (specs 08 + 10)

Large-view measurements and the proposed independent Show more/rendering design
are recorded in [the large-view review](large-view-review.md)
(RADD-1203; implementation follow-ups RADD-1204–1206).
Planning's visibility/history settings live in Display; each section has a
server-backed title quick filter, and backlog ordering lives on the backlog card
(`usePlanningSectionSearch.ts`, RADD-1207). These filters preserve section stats
and use independent matching counts; full aggregate corrections remain RADD-1205.

A saved view carries SLQ (`query`) and its `ViewRead.query_string` is the ready-made `GET /items?` composition — `q=<urlencoded>` (omitted when the query is empty) plus `project_id=<id>` for project-scoped views (the demo scripts that walked this round-trip were deleted in RADD-1082). The spec-08 structured params (`state_id`, `category`, `kind`, `priority`, `assignee_id`/`team_id` incl. literal `none`, `label`, `cf=k:v`) all remain on `GET /items` and AND with `q`: within one param values OR, across params AND. Malformed values → 422. Performance note: cf equality uses JSONB containment (`custom_fields @> …`) — sequential scan today; expression/GIN indexes come later keyed off the field registry's `indexed` flag. Migration `6afdeaf4f62d` converted every stored `ViewFilters` document to equivalent SLQ (ids resolved to names/emails; `none` → `IS EMPTY`; unresolvable references dropped clause-wise, logged) and dropped the `filters` column.

## Known simplifications (fix when they matter)

- OpenAPI is rebuilt per request (no cache) and the field-schema cache refreshes in-transaction on field creation — a rolled-back field create could leave the cache one step ahead until next startup/mutation. Move refresh to an event consumer when the worker lands.
- Field schema in OpenAPI is instance-wide; per-project scoping is enforced at validation time but not yet reflected per-project in docs.
- RBAC coarse edges: workspace/project/team/label listings are visible to any workspace member anywhere (not filtered to their workspaces); `GET /events` is instance-wide for any workspace member (trusted consumers); `item.*` event payloads carry FULL `custom_fields` for in-process consumers, but **webhook deliveries redact them since RADD-1085** (`items/redaction.py` + `fields.outbound_restricted_keys` — an endpoint holds no grant, so any read-restricted custom field/builtin arrives blanked, restricted `changes` entries drop, and internal-comment excerpts are withheld; `/search` headlines likewise degrade to title-only for non-admins while `description` is read-restricted anywhere); any workspace admin can create new workspaces (unscoped admin rule) and the creator is not auto-membered into a workspace they create (instance admins don't need it; others would lock themselves out — add self-membership when non-admin creation matters).
- Actor threading: every mutation emits with `actor_id` except default-state seeding, which runs via the in-txn `project.created` hook — the hook seam doesn't carry the actor yet, so those `state.created` events are actor-less (system-seeded).
- Auth gaps: expired sessions are never pruned (rows just stop resolving); PAT `last_used_at` writes are throttled (`RADD_TOKEN_LAST_USED_THROTTLE_SECONDS`, default 60s) so it's approximate.
- Roles (spec 06): builtin permission SETS are immutable (409) but builtin name/description/position may be patched; nothing stops a workspace from removing/demoting its last admin (instance admins can always recover); custom roles may list workspace-scoped permissions (`role.manage` etc.) but project-scoped checks never consult them — the catalog's `scope` field is the UI's guard; role permission grows are visible immediately (no caching), every check re-queries.
- Field grants (spec 07): the ladder shim is deleted from authz, and the `ProjectRole` enum followed it out of `teams/types.py` in the RADD-893 dead-code sweep. Deleting a role/team leaves no dangling grants problem for roles (RESTRICT via `global_role_grants` guards deletion of referenced roles (RADD-929), and unreferenced-role grants just stop matching) but grant rows pointing at deleted subjects are not garbage-collected — they keep the field restricted, which fails closed. `FieldDefinitionCreate` no longer takes visibility args (grants are set post-create via PUT); old clients sending `min_read_role`/`min_write_role` get them silently ignored. `item.created`/`item.updated` payloads embed the acting user's `comment_count`.
- Bootstrap: `uv run python -m radd.seed --email … --password …` (idempotent) creates the instance admin + global builtin roles + default work categories (spec 86 — the workspace entity is gone).
- A state's `is_default` can't be moved to another state (nothing writes it — not in `StateUpdate`, no endpoint). State delete-with-reassignment (spec 87) and label rename/delete exist.
- Item labels replace wholesale on PATCH (no add/remove ops); fine for API, revisit for UI ergonomics.
- Comment lists are unpaginated.
- items ↔ comments would be a dependency cycle (comments validates items via `items.service.require_item`), so items soft-imports `comments.service.comment_counts` inside hydration, guarded by `RADD_MODULES` — with comments disabled, `comment_count` degrades to 0.
- Item `kind` converts with the hierarchy enforced (`POST /items/{id}/convert`, RADD-1089); items archive + hard-delete (spec 38), clone (`POST /items/{id}/clone`, RADD-1088) and merge (`POST /items/{id}/merge`, RADD-1090).
- `ConflictError` has two message forms: `(entity, identifier)` → "… already exists", or `(entity, reason="…")` for rule violations (hierarchy, cross-project refs) — use `reason=` for anything that isn't a duplicate.
- Item list filters (spec 08): cross-project `cf` filtering resolves keys against an instance-wide key→definition map (oldest definition wins on cross-workspace key collisions; project-scoped listings use the project's own registry); the `label` filter resolves names through the distinct label ids in `item_labels` (the labels table stays private to its module) — OR within the param, and a name in use nowhere yields an empty result; no GIN index on `custom_fields` yet (see performance note above).
- Views: `shared`/`project_id` are create-only (recreate to change); `position` is client-managed (no auto-append/reorder ops); view names are not unique; listing requires membership in the view's workspace; a stored query is validated at write time only — later deletions/renames of states/labels/fields it names just change its results (unknown-name clauses match nothing), except a *deleted field key* which 422s the next query edit.
- SLQ (spec 10): a cf key shadowed by a builtin field name (`state`, `title`, …) or spelled like a keyword (`in`, `order`, …) is unreachable in queries — the migration drops such clauses with a log line; `me` works for `assignee` only; `state`/`team`/`project` name matching is exact + case-sensitive and workspace-unscoped (RBAC narrows cross-project noise); item keys in `key`/`parent` match any workspace's project key; no sort on `state` name or `key` (needs joins); `~` compiles to `ILIKE %…%` (no index); compile validates shape, not existence — unknown label/state/team names yield empty matches, not errors. The `slq` package is items' public seam (views imports it like `items.filters`).
- Cycles/releases/planning (spec 14): **`Permission.CYCLE_MANAGE` is held by workspace admins + instance admins only** — cycle CRUD is a workspace-scoped check, and the workspace-member floor is pinned to the builtin viewer set by `test_authz.py` (outside this spec's edit scope), so plain workspace members can't run sprints yet (the seeded admin, which the demo uses, is unaffected); widen `WORKSPACE_MEMBER_FLOOR` / the member set when member-run sprints matter. `cycle`/`release` SLQ match by NAME/VERSION, workspace-unscoped like `state` (RBAC + the `project_id` list filter narrow the noise); `blocks`/`blocked` support `IS [NOT] EMPTY` (must-have) and `= <key>` only — no `!=`/`IN`; `start`/`target` are queryable and IS-EMPTY-able but **not sortable** (spec lists only comparisons + IS EMPTY — add to `_SORT_COLUMNS` + the ORDER-BY suggest exact-set test if roadmap sort is needed); adding the `start` field made `stat` a did-you-mean tie that now resolves to `start` (alphabetically first over `state`). A link add/remove re-hydrates the acted item and emits `item.updated` (no dedicated `item_link.*` event); `relates` is stored as one directional row with the mirror rejected. `released_at` is naive UTC (matching `created_at`); `release` version-rename re-checks the `(project, version)` uniqueness.
- Intake forms (spec 17): **`Permission.FORM_MANAGE` (project-scoped) is held by workspace/instance admins + the builtin `admin` project role only** — the builtin `member` permission set is pinned by `test_authz.py`'s exact-set assertion (outside this spec's edit scope), so a plain project member can't yet manage forms (short of the spec's "members create intake forms" goal); add `FORM_MANAGE` to the builtin `member` role AND update that test when member-authored forms matter (the seeded admin the demo uses is unaffected, mirroring the `CYCLE_MANAGE` note above). Submit is authenticated-member-only (`item.create`) — **public/tokened submit is deferred**. Default resolution at submit is name-based and lenient: an unknown `state_name`/`cycle_name`/`release_version` degrades to unset (item takes the project default state / no cycle / no release) rather than erroring; `labels` auto-create via the labels service; a disabled form (`enabled=false`) rejects submits with 409. Admin gains `form.manage` automatically via `PROJECT_PERMISSIONS`; new workspaces seed it into the builtin `admin` row, but existing workspaces' stored role rows are not backfilled (no consistency check enforces the mirror — admins hold every permission regardless).
- My Work + polish (spec 32): the landing page `/` is the personal "My Work" dashboard (assigned/due-soon/starred via plain `GET /items?q=` SLQ + an unread-inbox preview; rows open the peek panel); the projects index moved to `/projects`; saved views gained a client-side **CSV export** of the fetched page (inherits the 200 cap); bare `/` opens the command palette. Deferred with reasons in the spec: light theme, item DELETE/archive, WIP limits, issue templates, reactions, j/k nav, builtin story points (a `number` custom field covers it until velocity should count points).
- GitLab connector (spec 31): env-level config only (per-workspace connector settings arrive with the extensions SDK); pipeline events ignored; no two-way comment sync; the merge transition applies to every referenced item regardless of workspace (the secret gates the endpoint); runs in-process (the out-of-process runner is the planned `extensions` module).
- Service desk (spec 30, largely superseded by specs 63/64/67 + the RADD-95x/98x/10xx mail/SLA waves — **do not trust this bullet for CURRENT gaps beyond what it still lists**): `reporter` defaults to the creator and was backfilled from the event log (`item.created` actors); pause states match by NAME (a rename silently stops pausing); SLQ `me` works for `assignee` AND `reporter`. **What spec 30 originally called missing is now built**: business-hours SLA windows shipped in spec 63 (`business_start_minute`/`business_end_minute`) and holidays pause the clock too (RADD-1031, via the kernel `NON_WORKING_DAYS` socket that `leave` feeds — see the `slas`/`leave` rows); first-match policy resolution (spec 63) replaced evaluate-all; board/list/view/planning SLA chips shipped (`POST /items/sla/batch`, an opt-in slot, not detail-rail-only); `sla.breached`/`sla.due_soon` ARE automation triggers (item-scoped, the spec-65 Service-desk trigger group); email-to-issue intake shipped in spec 47 and was hardened through the RADD-95x wave (see the `mailintake` row); queues got a dedicated view type (spec 64, `ViewType.queue` — fixed queue columns, breached-first ordering) on top of the saved-view escape hatch. **What genuinely remains a simplification**: no PER-QUEUE SLA policy (a policy is project-wide, filtered by priority/issue-type — not scoped to one queue/view); no LOAD-based assignment (RADD-1044 added round-robin, `assign_round_robin`, but nothing balances by current open-ticket count); the ack email body is no longer one of these — it became an admin-editable instance setting (`mail_ack_body`, RADD-1045).
- SSO (spec 40 → 110 → 121): providers are DB rows (`sso_providers`, google|oidc|github kinds over one code+PKCE engine — a kind supplies endpoints + a profile strategy, `idp.py`; env `RADD_OIDC_*` is seed-only) with `user_identities` (provider, `sub`) → user; discovery metadata cached per provider, invalidated on admin edits; no RP-initiated logout.
- LDAP (spec 42): AD-flavored only (UPN bind, `sAMAccountName`, the AD-only transitive nested-group rule — plain-OpenLDAP DN templates would need a strategy toggle); wire bind not exercised against a real directory in CI (stubbed-seam ASGI verification + first real-AD login at deploy time); one directory, admin/member mapping only.
- Wiki (spec 43): no real-time co-editing (single editor + optimistic 409 + versions; pycrdt lands behind the same PATCH seam); per-space AND per-page access shipped on the spec-92 framework (RADD-791/792/948), and pages take attachments (spec 102, entity key `page`); no page mentions; cosmetic slugs (URLs by id). (The Confluence importer landed in spec 117.)
- Extensions SDK (spec 44): polling (2s), not push; ONE offset per runner process (run several for isolation); the in-process GitLab connector isn't hoisted onto the runner yet.
- MCP (spec 45): tools capability only (no resources/prompts/SSE streaming/sessions); catalog v1 = tracker + docs read; `search_items` workspace scoping post-filters a page (may under-fill `limit`).
- AI (spec 46): pgvector semantic search + SSE streaming shipped in spec 103 (`ai/client.py` `stream`, the `ai.embedder` consumer, RRF fusion in `/search`); summaries not cached; NL→SLQ single validation retry.
- Connectors (spec 47): googlechat is fire-and-forget (no retry/DLQ — the webhooks module is the guaranteed path); mailintake trusts IMAP `\Seen` as its cursor and was verified pure-only (no IMAP server in dev); alertmanager verified pure + ASGI-mocked (table applied at the merge); env-level config (per-workspace connector config later).
- Packaging/MFA (spec 48): worker split all-or-nothing (single worker tier); worker still serves HTTP (nothing routes to it); Helm minimal (no HPA/PDB/NetworkPolicy); compose runs migrations in the app entrypoint (Helm uses a Job).
- Light theme (spec 39): first-pass palette inversion via Tailwind-v4 variable remap (`html.light`) — hand-tune surfaces as they annoy; density = `zoom: 0.9`; prefs per-browser (not on the profile yet); no prefers-color-scheme auto mode.
- Archive/delete (spec 38) + pagination (spec 41): attachment BYTES orphan on hard delete (storage GC later); no SLQ `archived` field yet; archived items stay searchable; bulk archive not in BulkActionBar; pagination since the pagination wave: views page CLASSICALLY (Pager + true /items/count total, page in the URL), roadmaps auto-stream in capped bursts — the old silent 200 cap is gone (RADD-687 verified on a 50,406-item board: 253 pages, true total, page 2 loads different items).
- RBAC extensions (spec 36): per-entity manage permissions (`state/release/field/label/webhook/canned.manage`) with umbrella implication (`expand_permissions` — project.manage/workspace.manage still cover everything they used to); workspace-scope member checks widened to include `cycle.manage` + `timesheet.view`; builtin member role gains `form.manage` (rows backfilled by `43c250fb2a01`); `automation.manage`/`sla.manage` stay admin-only (rules run as the SYSTEM actor — privilege-bearing); **builtin-field WRITE rules** (`builtin_field_rules`, default write-open, any rule restricts to its role/team subjects or project.manage, enforced in item create/update via pure `denied_builtin_fields`, managed at `PUT /field-rules` + the Builtin fields section of `/settings/fields`); no workspace-level custom-role assignment surface yet; rules are unconditional (no SLQ conditions).
- Builtin-field READ rules (spec 50): `builtin_field_rules` gained an `access` column (read|write; existing rows backfilled to `write` by `5a53e459d033`). A READ rule blanks the field out of every item representation for non-granted principals — pure `denied_builtin_reads` feeds `_filter_read` (alongside the spec-07 custom-field read filter) on the get/list/mutation-return paths. Only `READ_RESTRICTABLE_BUILTINS` (everything except title/state/priority — the structural row identifiers) may carry a read rule. Blanking uses the read-model's null/empty per field; the Builtin fields section of `/settings/fields` offers the read/write choice per grant. Follow-up surfaces closed since: MCP tool output rides the same actor-hydrated reads (verified RADD-1085), `/search` headlines degrade to title-only for non-admins while `description` carries any read grant, and webhook payloads redact restricted fields at the delivery seam; reports expose aggregates, not field values.
- RBAC CRUD split (spec 50): every resource now exposes granular `create/update/delete` atoms (`auth/types.py` `CRUD_RESOURCES` registry + `CRUD_MATRIX`; content resources add the missing `item/comment/worklog/doc.delete`). Each coarse `*.manage` is an umbrella that **transitively** implies its resource's atoms (`expand_permissions` is now a fixpoint; `global_scope_permissions` — named `workspace_scope_permissions` until the scope rename — expands too), so every pre-existing grant keeps working — mutation endpoints were rewired to the verb-specific atom (create→`.create`, update→`.update`, delete→`.delete`); project-access grants (members + team attach) use `member.*`. Reads stay open (no `.read` atom for config — value-level read restriction is field grants / comment visibility). Builtin `admin` (= all project-scoped perms) grew by 21 atoms, backfilled into existing rows by `41196e02047c`; `member`/`viewer` stored sets unchanged (capabilities flow through expansion). `PermissionScope` gains `instance`. New scope enum: `CrudAction`; the roles matrix groups by scope→resource.
- Issue mentions + field widgets (spec 52): the markdown editor autocompletes ISSUES on a `#` trigger (source: `GET /search`) and inserts `#[TD-1234](TD-1234)`; `markdown.tsx` renders that token (added to `INLINE_RE` before the generic-link pattern) as a chip-link to `/issues/KEY` — works in comments/docs/descriptions (people `@[Name](uuid)` mentions unchanged). Custom-field defs gained `display` (nullable `FieldDisplay` chips|dropdown) + `PATCH /fields/{id}` (`FieldDefinitionUpdate` name+display, `field.update`-gated) so a multi-select can render as a compact dropdown instead of space-filling chips. Migration `f6cb41ece0f1`. **Follow-ups now shipped:** field **`default_value`** (migration `bb706d3802ae`; seeded onto items that omit the key, inline editor in the fields settings panel + New-field modal reusing `CustomFieldControl`) and **issue-mention backlinks** (`ItemLinkType.mentions` auto-derived from `#[…]` tokens, read-only `MentionsSection` References / Referenced-by on the item view) — see the `fields` and `items` rows.
- Configurable screens (spec 53): the `screens` module (row above) drives the issue view's properties rail. `IssueProperties` fetches `GET /screens/effective` for the item's (project, issue-type) and renders arrangeable fields individually by placement — `primary` shown, `secondary` under a collapsible "More fields" (collapsed by default in the **peek** panel via `ItemDetailBody variant="panel"`, expanded on the full page), `hidden` omitted; a custom field with a validation error is force-shown. So with zero config the noisy custom-field long tail already collapses in the peek. The editor lives at project settings → **Screens** (`routes/settings/screens.tsx`, `project.manage`): per issue type (or the project default), a placement segmented control + up/down reorder, Save = full-list `PUT /screens`, Reset = `PUT` empty. **Still coarse-grained / deferred:** builtin fields are shown/collapsed/hidden but the four planning fields are individually placeable already; card display (board/list) is NOT yet screen-driven (next slice); no per-user overrides; the editor seeds from the resolved effective layout so Save pins every field's current placement.
